# Sequential MVP execution

The operator requested that each work item pass its checks before the next starts. Follow the order in [the build plan](../mvp-plan.md); record incomplete gates as incomplete. Tests exercise the agreed public workflow, HTTP, runner/provider, and browser boundaries, with real SQLite, Git, and local processes where applicable.

Historical revision IDs and source hashes below refer to the original private snapshots before the FOR-17 history rewrite. Sanitized copies are not byte-identical evidence for rewritten revisions; replay requires the private archive. New validation is recorded separately.

## FOR-87 — Approve execution profiles for discovered models (2026-10-19)

Code `176c550` and fix `7170303` on branch `for-87-work` from main `3f05023` (builds on the FOR-83 runner save path `a1ce41b`, `bf62c79`, `88bb6c9`). Checks ran in a fresh worktree prepared with `prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`, a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py`.

Simulated (fixture OMP that prints discovery metadata; no model runs):

- Unit `runner::profiles::tests` (7): profiles derived for Codex (`codex-app-server`), Claude Code (`claude-code-stream` with `resolvedModel` from the reported model) and OMP (each of `openai-responses`, `anthropic-messages`, `google-generative-ai`, taken from the provider definition) parse with `RuntimeConfig::parse`. `policy::with_profile` pairs them with the discovered offering: execution profile set, efforts narrowed to the chosen ones, tools true, and no admission blocker. Refusals: no effort chosen, an effort outside the advertised set, no advertised efforts, no reported Claude Code model, an undefined OMP provider, or an OMP provider without a supported `api`. The listing groups offerings per configured harness, lists two billing routes once, shows no profile until one is approved, and shows saved profiles that discovery did not list. An approval changes only `catalog` and leaves stored key references to the save path. Removal deletes the entry, and removing a missing profile is refused with 404. An approval for an undiscovered offering or an unconfigured harness is refused, and malformed requests are refused. A configuration without OMP providers gets no invented `models` section (the `7170303` fix).
- `tests/runner_settings.rs` (4, against the `crewroomd` binary): a new test saves an OMP-fixture configuration through the API. After the in-place reload and a discovery refresh, the offering is listed with efforts `low, high, xhigh` and no profile. Approving before any configuration exists is refused with 409; an unadvertised effort is refused with 400. Approving `high` writes the profile owner-only without secrets and reloads in place. The model catalog then shows the route with an execution profile, efforts `[high]` and no inference. Secrets stay stored, and removal reloads again with an empty catalog. The environment test now also refuses `POST /api/runner/profile` with 409 naming `CREWROOM_RUNTIME_CONFIG`.
- Desktop `discovered_models_are_approved_with_chosen_efforts_and_removed` (real embedded service, discovery from a fixture OMP): Settings → Runner shows `Example · fixture/model · Not approved` and says Codex is not configured. **Approve for agents** does nothing until an effort is chosen. After choosing `high` and approving, the line reads `Approved for efforts high`, the notice says a stop and start is needed (embedded service), and `runtime.json` holds the derived profile without secrets. **Remove approval** empties the catalog.

Live, isolated (debug `crewroomd` from `7170303` on port 4388 with its own data folder and a short private runtime root; the installed Codex and Claude Code CLIs and their existing local logins, metadata only; Mail pointed at an unused loopback port; no agent launched and no prompt sent; the operator service on 4318 and its data were not touched):

- A Codex and Claude Code configuration was saved through `POST /api/runner/settings` and applied by an in-place reload. After a discovery refresh, Settings → Runner listed 8 Codex and 5 Claude Code offerings, all approvable and none approved. OMP was listed as not configured.
- The first attempt, at `176c550`, refused both approvals with `Model policy and provider definitions are required.`. Approving had added an empty `models` section to this configuration, which has no OMP providers. Fixed in `7170303`.
- At `7170303`: approving Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` at `low` and Claude Code `default` (reported model `claude-opus-5-5`) at `low` reloaded in place each time. The execution-profiles prerequisite then read `2 verified profile(s) configured.`. The catalog routes showed execution profiles `codex-0.162.0-dynamic-tools-v1` and `claude-2.1.296-safe-sdk-tools-v1`, access `configured`, efforts `[low]` and no inference. `runtime.json` was 0600 without the Mail token. Removing both approvals reloaded again and left the catalog empty. No daemon remained afterwards.
- Not shown: that an approved model answers (no agent ran), and the operator's on-screen check in the 4318 app.

Checks:

- At `176c550`: `npm test` **346/346**; `npm run build` passes; `cargo test -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop -- --test-threads=4` **717 passed / 0 failed / 18 ignored** over 84 test binaries.
- At `7170303`: `cargo test -p crewroom-service --lib --test runner_settings` **25 + 4 passed**; Clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for core, runtime, service and desktop are clean. The fix changes only `runner/profiles.rs` and its tests.
- `npm run test:e2e`: not run (integrator).

**Integration on main `b938239`:** rebased with only `docs/status.md` and this file in conflict (both entries kept); `176c550` became `c2fbc30` and `7170303` became `bbd7362` with identical changes (`git range-diff`). At `bbd7362` content (branch head before this record), all through `system_limits.py run` in a worktree prepared with `--build-helpers` and a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (`-j6`, `--test-threads=4`): `npm test` **346/346**, `npm run build` passes (existing chunk-size warning), `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**, `cargo test -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop` **678 passed / 0 failed / 18 ignored** over 74 result lines (includes `runner::profiles::tests`, `tests/runner_settings.rs` and the desktop approval test), Clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for those crates clean. Simulated only; the operator's on-screen check in the 4318 app is still pending.

## FOR-84 — Target-refresh and retained-work journeys without the TypeScript oracle (2026-10-19)

Code `49f245b` (branch `for-84-work`, from main `3f05023`; test files only). Every check below ran at `49f245b` on Linux x86_64 with Docker Engine 29.7.2 (rootful) and Node 26, with Rust binaries (`crewroom-process`, `repository-fixture`) built from this revision in a target directory private to the worktree (worktree prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`). Git is real; no provider, Jira or GitHub service was contacted. The machine was shared with parallel agents.

**Before (main `3f05023`):** the two files were **15/25**. `target-refresh.test.ts` "unchanged native target names the exact same-head source candidate…" stopped with "Workflow database is newer than this application." `retained-work.test.ts` was 1/10: seven cases failed with "Needs an accepted implementation plan.", the dependency case with "Workflow database is newer than this application.", and the observer case timed out waiting for the observer because queueing had already failed.

**Oracle decision (consistent with FOR-61/FOR-82):** native-only expectations, not a pre-migration copy or fixed snapshot.
- Same-head refresh: the second candidate is seeded through the native `policy_fixture` operation. The retained `RepositoryWork.refreshBase` oracle compared the whole refresh receipt except `requestId` and `at`; the test now asserts that receipt exactly (`fromBase`/`fromHead` from the source candidate, `toBase` from `refs/heads/main`, `toHead`, `targetRef`, `sourceCandidateId`, `candidateId`, `state`), that a second request key yields the same receipt, that no Git process is started and the worktree head and status are unchanged, and that both receipts are persisted.
- Retained work: the native queue admits a coding successor only under an accepted implementation plan, so the fixture accepts one through `plan_source`/`plan_save`/`plan_decide`. Admission re-pins the plan source, so a refined brief or a moved target needs a re-accepted plan (with `predecessorId`); the moved-target case still asserts that the continuation stays pinned to the retained base until an explicit refresh. The dependency case seeds its upstream ticket, accepted plan, coding run, dependency and merged publication through native operations. No assertion was removed.

**Live journeys (real Git):** `target-refresh.test.ts` and `retained-work.test.ts` **25/25**, run twice. These journeys start no check container; `docker ps -a --filter label=io.crewroom.command` was empty before and after each run, the fixture checks every recorded Git process is stopped, and no fixture root remained in the temporary directory.

**Other checks at `49f245b`:** `npm test` **346/346** (simulated providers); `npm run build` passes (existing chunk-size warning); no Rust source changed, `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` clean. `npm run test:e2e` was not run here (the integrator runs it). Not re-run: rootless Docker, macOS.

**Integration on main `ef26863`:** rebased with only `docs/status.md` and this file in conflict (both entries kept); the code commit `49f245b` became `07c0d8c` with identical test changes. At `07c0d8c`, all through `system_limits.py run` in a worktree prepared with `--build-helpers` and a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: `npm test` **346/346**, `npm run build` passes, `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**, `target-refresh.test.ts` and `retained-work.test.ts` **25/25** (`docker ps -a --filter label=io.crewroom.command` empty before and after), `cargo fmt -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -- --check` clean. No Rust changed, so `cargo test` and Clippy were not run.

## FOR-85 — Daemon-scheduling restart usage test (2026-10-19)

Simulated checks only (fixture daemon, seeded agent events, private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, Linux x86_64); no live provider or delivery.

- Baseline on main `3f05023`: `npx tsx --test --test-name-pattern="provider usage" scripts/rust/daemon-scheduling.test.ts` (wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py`) **failed** at `assert.ok(JSON.stringify(got.usage).includes('0.2'))`. The projected usage showed one `unknown` billing route with `costUsd: null`, `costResponses: 0`, `estimateWithheld: true`, `unrecordedAttempts: 2`; the seeded attempt had 3 provider requests, 1 actual receipt, 1 deduplicated response and 20 tokens.
- Cause: the FOR-44 billing-route contract (`45b76ce`) withholds cost for unknown routes, and the fixture model records no route. FOR-54 (`96565b8`) only changed the unrecorded count and is not involved. Decision: correct the test, not the projection.
- At `2552e35`: `scripts/rust/daemon-scheduling.test.ts` **3 passed / 0 failed** (after building `crewroomd` and the `provider-daemon` example).
- `npm test`: **346 passed / 0 failed**. `npm run build`: passes.
- No Rust source changed. `cargo test -p crewroom-service --lib usage`: 5 passed; `cargo clippy -p crewroom-service --all-targets -- -D warnings`: clean; `cargo fmt -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-core --check`: clean.
- Not run: `npm run test:e2e` (integrator runs it serially).
- Integration on main `3f05023` (branch already on main, no rebase; `2552e35` and `ff3a1ff` unchanged), all through `system_limits.py run` in a worktree prepared with `--build-helpers`: `npm test` **346/346**, `npm run build` passes, `npm run test:e2e` **31/31** (run alone; a run concurrent with `npm test` had 3 timing failures that did not recur), `daemon-scheduling.test.ts` **3/3**, `cargo test -p crewroom-service --lib` **18/0 failed**, Clippy `-D warnings` for `crewroom-service` and fmt for core, runtime and service clean.

## FOR-76 — Per-project run capacity (2026-10-19)

Implementation `f51dedf` on branch `for-76-work` from main `9d409b5`. All checks below ran at `f51dedf` (the evidence commit changes only documentation) in a fresh worktree prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`, a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, and every test command wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py` (FOR-68). Linux x86_64.

Simulated (real SQLite, Git, tmux, OS processes and the production `crewroomd`; the agent runner is the scripted fixture RPC and provider replies are synthetic; no Jira, GitHub, Bitbucket or model service was contacted):

- `crates/crewroom-core/tests/capacity.rs` **10/10**: default capacity 1 per project with two projects admitted and the third plan held with "Waiting for a slot in alpha: 1 of 1 running"; raising a project's capacity admits it; the workspace ceiling holds a second project with "Waiting for a workspace slot: 1 of 1 running"; setting validation (1–16, unknown repository, empty or unknown keys); a cancelled holder frees its slot and the next plan is claimed after the store is reopened; a paused run whose ownership is unconfirmed keeps occupying its project even with its slot row removed, until ownership is confirmed stopped; a queued manual start admits no agent, is absent from agent activity, and becomes dispatchable only once it holds a slot; a crash-recovery race (cancelled run with unconfirmed ownership) makes a second run of the same ticket fail at queue, slot claim and attempt claim, and the slot table rejects a second row for one ticket; four concurrent connections fill exactly one slot per project; a forced third run in a full project is refused at attempt claim; a retained version-35 single slot migrates to its project.
- `scripts/rust/daemon-capacity.test.ts` **1/1** (run twice, 11–22 s): two projects' coding runs reach `running` at the same time with distinct coordinator attempts, distinct host PIDs and distinct worktrees; a third ticket in the full project is queued with the stated reason and has no attempts; a manual Start over capacity returns the queued plan with its reason and admits nothing; after `SIGKILL` of the daemon and restart both runs are running and the queued ticket is still held; cancelling the first project's run admits the queued ticket automatically while the other project keeps running. Cleanup confirmed every host and runner process stopped.
- `scripts/rust/daemon-scheduling.test.ts` (existing single-project review release, re-hold, queued-Resume priority and restart through actual Docker checks and scripted Bitbucket): **3/4**. The failing test, "daemon history and provider usage equal the retained projection after a real restart", asserts usage cost only and fails the same way on each run. It was not compared on main (the baseline build exceeded the `/tmp` quota and was abandoned), so it is recorded as unattributed and tracked in FOR-85.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-core` **183 passed, 0 failed**; `-p crewroom-service` **40 passed, 0 failed**; `-p crewroom-runtime` **205 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**; `-p crewroom-desktop` **153 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored** (desktop at the pre-review head; the later change removed one attempt-claim check and touches no desktop code). The desktop suite includes the new capacity summary test.
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt -- --check` clean.
- `npm test` **345/345**; `npm run build` passes. `npm run test:e2e` was not run by the implementer (fixed port; the integrator runs it).

During verification, a stricter rule that refused a coordinator attempt while another coordinator attempt of the same run was live broke the rebrief handoff in `daemon-scheduling.test.ts` (the coordinator is replaced before the old attempt's ownership is recorded stopped). The rule was removed; one attempt per assignment and the cross-run ticket claim remain. Not verified: the GPUI Settings controls through a live window (unit-tested summaries only), live providers, and macOS.

### FOR-76 review fix — a paused run with stopped ownership frees its slot (2026-10-19)

Review finding: the branch kept a paused run's slot even when every agent was confirmed stopped, contrary to the ticket ("pauses with stopped ownership" admits the next ticket; only unconfirmed-stopped paused runs keep their slot). Fix `fee2825` on branch `for-76-paused-slot` from `6fc0109` (`for-76-work`). `Store::release_paused_coding_slot` frees the slot (`slotRelease.reason = "paused"`) for a coding run paused in desired and actual state with every attempt admitted-only or ownership `stopped`. It runs at `finish_pause` and from the scheduler's holder pass. Review-gate pauses (`review`, `validation`, `merged`), transient stops with desired state `running`, and comparisons keep their slot. Resume reclaims a slot through `reclaim_coding_slot`, which clears the marker, or, in a full project, queues a `resumeRequest` that keeps priority over new plans. All checks ran at `fee2825` with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, and test commands wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py`. Linux x86_64. The shared `/tmp` was at its per-user quota, so the TypeScript suites ran with `TMPDIR` set to a short private directory. A long `TMPDIR` inside the worktree makes the tmux socket path too long, and 38 legacy supervision tests fail on that alone.

Simulated (fixture runner, synthetic provider replies; no live delivery):

- `crates/crewroom-core/tests/capacity.rs` **12/12**. New `a_paused_run_with_stopped_ownership_frees_its_slot_and_stays_resumable`: an operator pause keeps the slot while the stop is unconfirmed. At `finish_pause` with ownership stopped, the run is `paused`, carries `slotRelease.reason = "paused"` and frees the slot, and the next queued plan in the project is claimed. Resume in the full project queues a `resumeRequest`, and reclaim is refused. After the new holder is cancelled, the queued Resume keeps priority over a third plan, reclaims the slot and clears the marker. New `a_transient_or_review_pause_keeps_its_slot`: a recovery stop (desired `running`) and a `validation` pause keep the slot. The unconfirmed-ownership test is unchanged and passes.
- `scripts/rust/daemon-capacity.test.ts` **1/1**, run twice (about 8 s each). After the restart, `POST /runs/{alpha}/pause` stops the alpha holder: it is `paused`/`operator` with every attempt `stopped` and `slotRelease.reason = "paused"`. The queued alpha ticket reaches `running` automatically, beta keeps running, and the slots are {beta, alpha-second}. Resume of the paused run is queued. Cancelling alpha-second resumes it to `running` with the marker cleared.
- `scripts/rust/daemon-scheduling.test.ts` **3/4**, unchanged from `6fc0109`. The review release, re-hold, queued-Resume priority and restart test passes. The usage-cost test still fails (FOR-85).
- `cargo test -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-runtime` **430 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**. `-p crewroom-desktop` **153 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored**.
- Clippy (`-p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings`) is clean. `cargo fmt -p <crate> -- --check` is clean for the same crates.
- `npm test` **345/345**. `npm run build` passes. The implementer did not run `npm run test:e2e`.
- Other native journeys that pause, run as a regression sample: 44 pass and 12 fail, none on slot release. They fail for these reasons: the native driver binary (`scripts/test-native-resume.sh`) was not built (2); the macOS-only `/opt/homebrew/bin/tmux` path (1); "Needs an accepted implementation plan" or "Workflow database is newer" in retained-work and conflict journeys, tracked in FOR-84 (8); and one continuation-observer timeout in those same journeys. This sample was not compared against `6fc0109`.

## FOR-78 — Snooze and settle tickets in the project sidebar (2026-10-19)

Implementation `14df3af` (branch `for-78-work`, from main `e8b86de`). Shelf logic is in `crates/crewroom-desktop/src/workbench/shelves.rs`; the shelves extend the private per-dataset `native-ui-state.json` (`navigation.rs`, `Shelves`); the sidebar groups in `projects.rs` keep shelved tickets out of the four sections; the service's workspace view adds a read-only `publicationWatch` per run (`crates/crewroom-service/src/views.rs`) with the PR's recorded feedback ids and hosted-check state, so PR events can be watched without a provider call.

Desktop, GPUI test platform, injected synthetic workspace, no window on the operator's desktop and no service or agent (`shelves_tests.rs`, `navigation.rs`):

- `snooze_and_settle_shelve_tickets_without_changing_run_workflow_or_source`: Snooze… offers 1 hour, 4 hours, Tomorrow 09:00 and the three events; on a local ticket without a PR the events are disabled with "PR events need a confirmed PR; source status needs a linked ticket source." and pressing them changes nothing. Snoozing and settling move tickets to collapsed per-project shelves ("Expand Snoozed · 1", "Expand Settled · 2"); the project still says "● 2 running"; the source, workflow and activity chips of a settled running and queued ticket are identical to before; no command is sent and the workspace projection is byte-for-byte unchanged. Un-settle and Wake now return tickets to their sections. Shelves and their open state read back after a restart.
- `undo_is_offered_for_five_seconds_after_each_shelf_action`: Undo after settle, snooze, un-settle and Wake now restores the prior shelf entry exactly; on the test clock Undo is still offered at 4.9 s and gone at 5.1 s.
- `snoozed_tickets_wake_with_their_reason_and_survive_a_restart`: time, PR review and event snoozes survive a restart with their baselines; an unchanged refresh wakes nothing; the ticket wakes with "Snooze ended …", "PR review submitted", "CI finished · passed" and "Source status changed to GitHub closed"; a ticket snoozed for 48 hours wakes at once with "Needs you · Coordinator asks a question" when a pending decision appears and is listed under Needs you, with its workflow chip unchanged; wake reasons survive a restart until dismissed.
- `shelves_read_back_and_a_malformed_snooze_keeps_the_file_out`: a pre-FOR-78 file reads with empty shelves; a snooze with neither time nor event and duplicate or empty ids are dropped; an unparseable time keeps the file out.
- Service unit test `publication_watch_reports_recorded_feedback_and_hosted_check_state`: null without a confirmed PR; feedback ids keep their kind; running, finished (passed or failed) and none states; only the policy's status keys count.

Checks at `14df3af` (private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, tests through `scripts/system_limits.py run`):

- `npm test`: **345/345**. `npm run build`: passes.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop`: **161 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored**. `cargo test -p crewroom-service`: **41 passed, 0 failed**. Core and runtime are unchanged and were not rerun.
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-desktop -p crewroom-service --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for core, runtime, service and desktop: clean.
- `npm run test:e2e`: not run (integrator). The browser suite exercises the legacy TypeScript UI, which this change does not touch.

*Simulated only:* no live PR, CI or source status was watched; event wakes ran over injected `publicationWatch` and source status values. Pinning does not exist in the native workbench, so there is no unpin to undo. Not yet verified: the operator's on-screen check.

**Integration at `f2ed810` on main `b0054be` (2026-10-19).** `14df3af` and its evidence commit were rebased onto main `b0054be` as `d75ea0b` and the evidence commit. Code conflicts in `navigation.rs` (FOR-77 `OverviewPrefs` and FOR-78 `Shelves` in `Saved`, `update_overview` and `update_shelves`), `views.rs` (`landed_entry` and `publication_watch`, and their unit tests in one `tests` module) and `docs/user-guide.md` kept both sides. The merged tree did not compile because FOR-77 and FOR-78 each added `Workbench::ticket_key` and `Workbench::place`; `f2ed810` renames the shelf versions to `shelf_ticket_key` and `restore_shelf` without changing behavior. The checks above cite the pre-rebase tree. At `f2ed810`, all through `scripts/system_limits.py run` with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, the worktree prepared with `--build-helpers` and `TMPDIR` on a short on-disk directory outside the checkout:

- `npm test` **346/346**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**.
- `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop`: **643 passed, 0 failed, 18 ignored** over 69 test binaries.
- Clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for these crates: clean.
- Environment note: a first run with `TMPDIR` inside the worktree failed 12 tests (fixture folders inside a Git checkout read as repositories, and tmux socket paths became too long); a second with `TMPDIR` in the shared `/tmp` failed one runtime test with `Disk quota exceeded`. Both are environmental; the run above passed with an on-disk `TMPDIR`.
- Still simulated only; the operator's on-screen check is pending.

## FOR-72 — Recorded harness transcripts for version-stamped replay tests (2026-10-19)

Code `1816139` (branch `for-72-work`, from main `20a6149`). Every check below ran at `1816139` on Linux x86_64 in a worktree with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. Tests ran through `scripts/system_limits.py run` with `-j6` and `--test-threads=4`. No Jira, GitHub or real model provider was contacted.

**Recordings: real binaries, synthetic provider.** `scripts/harness_transcripts.py record` ran each installed executable through the production adapter (`codex-fixture` example) with a tee shim. A synthetic loopback provider streamed the model output.
- Harnesses: Codex `codex-cli 0.163.0`, Claude Code `2.1.303 (Claude Code)` and OMP `omp/18.11.3`.
- Scenarios: `tool-completion`, `steer-interrupt` and `reattach`, giving nine transcripts and about 0.5 MB in `crates/crewroom-runtime/tests/fixtures/harness-transcripts/`.
- Coverage: launch, tool approval (the Claude gate hook allows the call, and Codex and OMP answer the host tool), completion, steering, interrupt, and restart on the same session (`recoveryMode: session` on each harness).
- Behaviors the recordings captured:
  - Claude Code absorbs steering without a separate prompt result and aborts the turn.
  - Codex and OMP report both inputs as aborted.
  - Codex tries WebSockets four times, then falls back to HTTPS.
  - OMP has a built-in provider named `synthetic`; the recorder uses `crewroom-fixture` instead.

These recordings show harness protocol behavior. They do not show model behavior.

**Redaction gate.** The recorder normalizes paths, host name, user name, UUIDs and the provider origin, and redacts credential and account fields. It then checks the transcript and writes nothing that fails. During development the gate refused two candidates:
- a Codex ChatGPT token file field (a synthetic JSON web token);
- a Codex `gitInfo.originUrl` from a recording made inside a checkout. Recordings now run outside any checkout.

`python3 scripts/harness_transcripts.py check`: 9/9 pass. A manual search of the committed transcripts found no home path, user name or account value.

**Replay (simulated).** `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime --test harness_replay` passed **4/4**, then three more times. Each of the nine transcripts reports `SIMULATED replay (not live evidence)` with 0 client drift notes. `CREWROOM_REPLAY_REPORT` rows are labelled `"evidence":"simulated-replay"`, all `passed: true`. `a_changed_harness_frame_fails_the_replay` checks two failures:
- An unadmitted model in a recorded assistant frame changes the adapter's events.
- A renamed client frame kind is a hard peer failure.

**Version diffs (real binaries, synthetic provider).** `tool-completion` was re-recorded on two other installed versions, outside the repository, and compared with `diff`:
- **Claude Code 2.1.302 against 2.1.303:** only the version and executable identity changed, plus the order of two independent client frames.
- **Codex 0.162.0 against 0.163.0:** the version and identity changed, `account/login/completed` moved earlier, and 0.163.0 adds a `thread/readState/changed` notification before the turn ends.

**Other checks at `1816139`:**
- `npm test` **346/346**, including `scripts/test_harness_transcripts.py` (7 tests: gate over committed transcripts, refusals, normalization, diff, provider).
- `npm run build` passes.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime`: **209 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**.
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` are clean.
- `npm run test:e2e` was not run here; the integrator runs it.

**Limits:**
- Transcripts cover the three named scenarios only. A tool denial is not recorded separately: Crewroom answers `can_use_tool` with deny, but the recorded flows go through its allow gate.
- OMP reattach replays the adapter's session-restore exchange, not OMP's own session files.

**Review fix at `e91c680` (simulated).** At `1816139` a replay `wait` searched every event collected so far. In `omp/reattach.jsonl` the second session waits for `agent_end` without an id, so the first session's `agent_end` satisfied it. The replay then closed without waiting for the resumed turn and passed only because the peer had already written its frames. The replay now keeps a cursor: a `wait` matches only events after the last satisfied wait or close, as the recorder's per-process queue does. Checks at `e91c680`, run the same way as above:
- `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime --test harness_replay`: **6/6**, five consecutive runs. The new `the_reattached_turn_must_complete_on_its_own` removes the second session's `agent_end` and adjusts the expectation, so only the wait can catch the gap. It requires a wait timeout and failed when the wait was temporarily reverted to search from the first event. `a_wait_only_matches_unconsumed_events` covers the cursor rule.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime`: **211 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**.
- `npm test` **346/346**. `cargo clippy -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` are clean.
- The recorded transcripts were not re-recorded. The OMP `done()` step in the recorder is still not session-aware; the replay cursor makes this harmless.

**Integration at `da7ab61` on main `0fe0364` (2026-10-19).** `1816139`, its evidence commit, `e91c680` and its evidence commit were rebased onto main `0fe0364` as `07e3269`, `d978728`, `da7ab61` and the evidence commit `fa315bb`, with conflicts only in this record and `docs/status.md`; the code is unchanged, so the checks above still cite the pre-rebase SHAs. Checks at `fa315bb` (code tree of `da7ab61`), all through `scripts/system_limits.py run` with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, `TMPDIR` on a short on-disk directory and the worktree prepared with `--build-helpers`:

- `npm test` **346/346**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**.
- `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-runtime`: **247 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored** over 21 binaries (main now includes the FOR-74 allowance tests). `harness_replay` **6/6** in that run and two more.
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check`: clean.

## FOR-74 — Remaining subscription allowance for Codex and Claude Code (2026-10-19)

Code `6d19f33` (branch `for-74-work`, from main `20a6149`). Checks ran on Linux x86_64 with Rust binaries built from this revision in a target directory private to the worktree (prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`), all through `scripts/system_limits.py run`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`. The machine was shared with parallel agents.

**Live, operator machine (metadata only).** `python3 scripts/verify_allowance.py --binaries target/debug --port 4398` started a disposable `crewroomd` with its own data directory and no runner configuration, called `POST /api/allowance/refresh` and read `GET /api/allowance`. Installed: Codex `codex-cli 0.163.0`, Claude Code `2.1.303`. No prompt, agent or inference ran; no run was created. The redacted report is in [for-74-allowance-2026-10-19.json](for-74-allowance-2026-10-19.json) (plan names and shares removed).

- Run 1 (daemon built before the desktop and documentation changes; runtime and service sources identical to `6d19f33`): Codex `available`, one `Weekly` window; Claude Code `available`, `5-hour`, `Weekly` and one model-specific weekly window. Every window had a remaining share within 0–100 and a reset time. Both readings ran in 2.5 s.
- Run 2 (daemon built at `6d19f33`, about 33 minutes and several manual reads later): Codex `available` as before; Claude Code `unavailable` with "Claude Code could not read plan rate limits from claude.ai for this login." A direct `get_usage` probe at the same time also returned `rate_limits: null`, so the source returned no limits; Crewroom showed that, not zero.
- Both runs: `inferenceRequested` false, an observation time on every reading, and none of 132 local login-file strings found in the allowance or workspace views. The Codex login file was still a regular private file afterwards, and no link to it remained in the daemon's data directory.

**Simulated.**
- `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime --lib allowance`: 4 passed. Response parsing for available, partial (missing share or reset), empty and over-100 % Codex windows; Claude Code available, partial and unavailable (`rate_limits_available` false, `rate_limits` null, no utilization). API and unknown logins are refused before any process starts. No account id, balance or spend value reaches the reading.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime --test model_allowance`: 4 passed. Fake harnesses fail unless they receive only `initialize`/`initialized`/`account/rateLimits/read`, or only the `initialize` and `get_usage` control requests with `skip_behaviors`. Cases: available, partial, unsupported (`unknown variant` / control error), and a hung harness that times out as unavailable. The cache names harnesses without a target.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-service --test model_catalog allowance`: 2 passed. Refresh needs the token and the service's own origin (403 otherwise) and rejects a body (400). Before a read each harness is "Not read yet"; afterwards the PATH Codex is `available` with 5-hour and weekly windows, and the missing Claude Code is "not installed on the service PATH". With a configured runner, harnesses it does not configure are "not configured in the runner". No credential or account canary appears in the workspace.
- Desktop: 3 passed. Line formatting; the results panel shows allowance beside recorded usage with partial and unavailable reasons and no "0% left"; Models & budget reads through a real service fixture with the Refresh allowance control.

**Suites at `6d19f33`.** `cargo test -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop --no-fail-fast` **583 passed / 0 failed / 18 ignored** over 68 binaries; `npm test` **345/345**; `npm run build` passes; Clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` clean for these crates. Two earlier full runs failed for the machine, not the code: the shared `/tmp` tmpfs hit its quota ("Disk quota exceeded"), and a `TMPDIR` inside the worktree made tmux socket paths too long and placed fixture folders inside a Git worktree. The passing run used a private `TMPDIR` outside any repository. `npm run test:e2e` was not run (left to the integrator).

**Integration at `26896ed` (2026-10-19).** Rebased from `6d19f33` onto main `ead4b21`. Two code conflicts were additive and kept both sides: the FOR-73 picker listing test and the FOR-74 allowance test in `model_picker/tests.rs`, and the FOR-83 `runner` and FOR-74 `allowance` fields in the service workspace projection. The live receipt stays bound to `6d19f33`; the allowance runtime and service code is unchanged by the rebase. In a worktree prepared with `--build-helpers`, wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py run`: `npm test` **345/345**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **31/31** with `TMPDIR` on an on-disk directory (two runs with the default `/tmp` failed most browser specs with Chromium `Target crashed` while the shared `/tmp` tmpfs was nearly full; this change does not touch the browser UI); `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -j6 -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop -- --test-threads=4` **632 passed / 0 failed / 18 ignored** over 70 binaries; Clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` clean. Not yet verified: the operator's check of the allowance in the native app after a service restart.

**Limits.** Readings are kept in memory and read only on request; after a restart each harness shows "Not read yet". Claude Code's `get_usage` is marked experimental by Claude Code, and its shape may change; an unanswered or changed request reads as unavailable. Codex reads use the same symlinked-login method as Crewroom's existing token refresh.

## FOR-77 — Overview home across projects (2026-10-19)

Implementation `ddc7fae` (branch `for-77-work`, from main `e8b86de`, building on the FOR-75 sidebar). The screen lives in `crates/crewroom-desktop/src/workbench/overview.rs`; the thresholds extend `navigation.rs` (`OverviewPrefs`, defaults 20 and 60 minutes, bounded to 1–1440 with promotion strictly later, serde defaults so older files read unchanged). The service projection (`crates/crewroom-service/src/views.rs`) adds `runProgress` per unfinished run and `landed`; `Store::run_progress` (`crates/crewroom-core/src/observations.rs`) takes the latest of an attempt's `lastEventAt` (now stamped when a new agent event is recorded; replays are not new output), peer-message `createdAt`, repository checkpoint `at` and the run's `startedAt`.

Desktop, GPUI test platform, no window on the operator's desktop (`overview_tests.rs`, synthetic projects and paths):

- `the_home_screen_renders_every_section_with_an_empty_state`: with no ticket chosen the home screen is the Overview and its sidebar entry is current; Needs you, Running, Landed and Next each render "· 0" with an empty-state sentence; returning from a ticket through the sidebar entry shows the same screen.
- `without_the_execution_layer_the_overview_says_what_it_cannot_list`: no `needsYou`/`landed` in the projection gives explicit "cannot be listed" sentences instead of empty claims.
- `needs_you_lists_only_actionable_items_highest_impact_first`: order is attention-required run, unconfirmed publication, rejected GitHub token, ticket-source mismatch, plan review, PR feedback, learning proposal; a running run with recent progress and a scoping intake are not items. Each item is labelled "Project · KEY" and has its action (Open session, Reconcile publication, Reconnect GitHub, Open project settings, Review plan, Resume run, Review proposal); an unavailable reconciliation says why. Review plan opens the ticket's Brief; Open project settings opens Settings → Projects at that project.
- `a_quiet_run_is_flagged_then_promoted_without_claiming_failure`: Running lists only executing runs (a paused run with old progress is not listed or flagged), with coordinator and specialists from the saved launch and "Last progress 3 min ago · agent output". Runs quiet for 30 and 90 minutes get "No progress for 30 min" / "No progress for 90 min" as a separate chip; only the 90-minute run is listed under Needs you, with "This does not mean the run failed"; no line contains "fail" otherwise. Raising the thresholds to 35/105 minutes removes the 30-minute flag and the Needs you item.
- `landed_names_the_covered_revision_and_next_names_the_reason`: "PR #7 · merged" with "Evidence covers revision 0123456789ab", "PR #8 · published · open", research "Evidence covers research revision 3", and "Evidence revision not recorded" when the head is missing; Next shows "#1 in queue · <hold reason>" and "#2 in queue · Eligible · scheduling" with project labels.
- `the_local_service_projects_progress_and_landed_work_for_the_overview` (real authenticated local service, synthetic Git checkout, queued coding plan): the service sends `landed: []` and `runProgress` with `{"lastAt":null,"source":null}` for the queued run; the Overview shows Landed · 0, Running empty and Next · 1 with the project name and "#1 in queue · …".
- Updated `every_panel_has_exactly_one_selected_sidebar_control`: Overview is a sidebar control, and is current when no ticket is chosen. `project_preferences_survive_reopening_and_keep_ticket_navigation` checks threshold bounds and persistence.

Core and service: `crates/crewroom-core/tests/progress.rs` (run start, then agent output, a replayed sequence ignored, then a peer message, under an injected clock) and `views::tests::landed_names_the_published_revision_and_skips_unconfirmed_work` (open and merged PRs name the published head; an uncertain PR is not landed; completed research names its revision).

Checks at `ddc7fae`'s tree (private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, `TMPDIR` on disk because the shared `/tmp` tmpfs was at its quota, all tests through `scripts/system_limits.py run`):

- `npm test`: **345/345**. `npm run build`: passes.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop`: **163 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored** (run before a formatting-only change to one test file). A first run with fixture roots in `/tmp` lost 66 tests to `Disk quota exceeded`; the rerun on disk passed.
- `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-runtime`: **420 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**.
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for these crates: clean.
- `npm run test:e2e`: not run (integrator). The browser suite exercises the legacy TypeScript UI, which this change does not touch.

Integration at `8147260` on main `cb8efca` (rebased from `ddc7fae`; the only conflict was the `Panel` enum, where FOR-83's `Runner` and FOR-77's `Overview` were both kept; the checks above cite the pre-rebase tree). Worktree prepared with `prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`, private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, all tests through `scripts/system_limits.py run`:

- `npm test`: **345/345**. `npm run build`: passes.
- `npm run test:e2e`: **31/31** with `TMPDIR` on a short on-disk directory. A first run with the default `/tmp` failed 8 browser specs with Chromium `Target crashed` while the shared `/tmp` tmpfs user quota was nearly exhausted; this change does not touch the browser UI. An `npm test` run with `TMPDIR` under the long worktree path stalled and was stopped; with the default `TMPDIR` it passed.
- `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop`: **619 passed, 0 failed, 18 ignored** over 69 test binaries.

## FOR-83 — Configure the agent runner from Settings (2026-10-19)

Code `a1ce41b`, `bf62c79` and `88bb6c9` on main (rebased without code changes from `45d58a1`, `66cc86b` and `145acfb`, written on main `9d409b5`, onto main `beb340c`). Checks below cite the pre-rebase revisions they ran at, except the integration checks at the end. Checks ran in a fresh worktree with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py`.

Simulated (real `crewroomd`, SQLite and encrypted store; the Mail endpoint is an unused loopback port; no agent runs):

- `tests/runner_settings.rs` (3, against the `crewroomd` binary with isolated data folders): with no configuration, every prerequisite reads ready, missing or failing with a remedy. Validation refuses a missing Mail token, a form key placed in Advanced and a symlinked harness executable (naming its real path) before anything is written. A valid save writes `runtime.json` with mode 0600 and only `$crewroomSecret` references; neither the file, `credentials.json` nor any response contains the synthetic Mail token or provider key. The daemon reloads in place on the same origin with execution available, the source reads `settings` and nothing is pending. Editing from the projected form without re-entering secrets keeps them. A broken saved file leaves the service reachable with the runner off and the reason shown. A Codex-only configuration saves without an invented `models` section (the `66cc86b` fix). With `CREWROOM_RUNTIME_CONFIG` set, that file stays active and read-only, its inline secret is reported as `inline` and never returned, and a save is refused with 409. An embedded service saves, reports the change as pending and says a stop and start is needed; Apply is refused with 409, and a cross-origin mutation with 403.
- Unit tests: runner secrets are encrypted and bound to their name (a value moved under another name does not decrypt; invalid names are refused; integration tokens are unaffected); the advanced view never carries `apiKey`, inline Mail tokens or header values; the live-process rule matches the pause rule (unresolved attempt ownership) and also holds running or pausing runs.
- Desktop `runner_page_lists_prerequisites_and_saves_the_configuration_privately`: Settings → Runner shows the prerequisite lines and fixes, the form is filled and saved through the real service, typed secrets are cleared from the form and never rendered, the view reports both secrets as stored, Apply now sends nothing on an embedded service, and the file is 0600 without secrets.

Live, isolated (debug `crewroomd` built from `66cc86b` for the first launch and from `145acfb` for the rest, on port 4372 with its own data folder in a private scratch directory; a real Rust Agent Mail server on a loopback port with a synthetic bearer token; the installed Codex CLI 0.163.0 and its existing local login; Docker engine on its system socket; the operator service on port 4318 and its data were not touched):

- Before configuration: tmux and the process helpers read ready; runtime root, Mail, Docker, OMP providers and execution profiles read missing with their fixes.
- A runner configuration was created only through `POST /api/runner/settings` (the same request the Settings page sends): runtime root, Mail URL/folder/token, Docker socket, Codex executable and auth file, one Codex `gpt-6-astra` execution profile. The service reloaded in place. `runtime.json` mode 0600; the Mail token was absent from the file, `credentials.json` and the view. Source `settings`, active, nothing pending; runtime root, tmux, helpers, Mail (MCP handshake with the saved token), Docker (`/_ping`), check images, Codex harness (`codex-cli 0.163.0 · verified`) and execution profiles ready, so the required set read ready. OMP and Claude Code were not configured and read missing (optional).
- The first save used the version manager's `latest` path; the launch failed with `Process directory is not canonical`. That led to `145acfb`, which now refuses such a path at save and shows the harness as failing.
- With the real path, queueing an Explore run launched a real Codex coordinator from the UI-written configuration: host ready, ownership held, state idle, model `gpt-6-astra` at low effort. Cancel stopped it (`exited`, `stopped`). A second launch stayed held and idle for 300 s, then cancelled and stopped cleanly. This check read no settled turn, so it shows a launched and supervised agent, not completed inference. Two more launches ran while the shared `/tmp` reached its per-user quota (EDQUOT). One failed with `Agent host did not establish its identity before the deadline`. The other was left with ownership unresolved, and the service failed closed (`attention_required`, and close reported `Workflow ownership remains unresolved`). Both are attributed to the quota, not to this change. No host, Codex or Mail process from the check remained afterwards.
- Not done here: the operator's app on port 4318. The operator check that resolves "Runner not configured" from that app and launches an agent is pending.

## FOR-73 — Model catalog: typed capabilities, bundled model facts and a readable picker (2026-10-19)

Implementation `8c9f837` and wording fix `7053ab0` on main (rebased from `4ae7449` and `517466d`, written on main `9d409b5`, onto main `8ce59f1`; the only code conflict was the `model_catalog` module list, merged to keep FOR-71's `codex_protocol` and `known_broken` beside FOR-73's `bundled`, `capabilities` and `login`). The wording fix makes capability notes name the release, for example `Claude Code 2.1.303 (compatible, probed)`. Checks below cite the pre-rebase revisions they ran at, except the integration checks at the end. Checks ran in a fresh worktree with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py`.

Simulated (unit and fixture tests; no inference):

- `capabilities::tests` (4): verified versions carry the MH2/MH3/OMP baseline (Codex: no request counts, cost or pre-request budget; Claude Code: cost estimated; OMP: gateway counts and budget, no cost). A probed version claims only what its receipt exercised (host tools, effort) and states steering as held and interruption, resume and token accounting as degraded. A receipt without the handshake refuses admission. A frozen record is kept; routes admitted before FOR-73 keep the baseline; a tampered record claims nothing.
- `bundled::tests` (4): the shipped file parses and covers all three harnesses; duplicate, malformed or unknown-field entries are rejected; bundled facts fill only unreported capabilities; a live-only model stays listed with `unknownCapabilities`; a model missing only because the installed Codex/Claude Code predates it names the release; OMP never gets a version claim.
- `login::tests` (2) and `unconfigured::tests` (2 new): login models are launchable only through a runner-configured provider; others state the subscription or API-key reason; a credential-shaped field in the harness output never reaches the view; a login listing on a non-OMP entry is rejected.
- `tests/catalog_policy.rs` (4 new): admission freezes `harnessCapabilities` and `degradedModes`; a refusal blocks admission and the picker alike with bare `blockers`; a login blocker is the only reason shown; choices carry bundled status and unknown capabilities.
- `catalog::tests::passing_reprobe_of_a_changed_executable_lets_the_launch_proceed`: after a re-probe to `omp/19.0.1` the attempt's record becomes `probed`; `steering_mode` changes from `steer` to held and `resumes_session` to false. The store accepts the record and its notes changing with the identity.
- Desktop `picker_listing_groups_collapses_and_states_shared_reasons_once`: one line per model (name · route · efforts) under harness headers; legacy rows collapsed (shown when expanded or saved); a reason shared by every runner-listed model appears once with its remedy and not on rows; version-update rows name the release. The three existing picker journeys were updated to the new row text.

Live, on this machine (metadata only; no prompt sent; operator service on 4318 and its data untouched):

Capability records: OMP and Codex `verified`, Claude Code `probed` with steering held and interruption, resume and token accounting stated as degraded. Choices: 162 (149 OMP login, 8 Codex, 5 Claude Code); 22 with bundled facts, 7 legacy, 140 live-only; no credential-shaped text in the catalog; job selections saved and read back; no runs. The private report stays outside the repository.

## FOR-71 — Harden the harness compatibility probe (2026-10-19)

Implementation `b68910b` on main (rebased without code changes from `9f9e217`, written on main `9d409b5` with FOR-69 merged, onto main `e8b86de`). Every check below, except the integration checks at the end, ran at `9f9e217`. Each ran in a fresh worktree prepared with `scripts/prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`, with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6` and `--test-threads=4`, wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py run`.

Simulated (synthetic schemas and Python harnesses; no inference, no network):

- `codex_protocol::tests` (6): the reviewed baseline passes with no differences. One unhandled server request fails with `Codex reports server request Crewroom does not handle: item/browser/requestNavigation`, and several are all named. A missing handled request (`account/chatgptAuthTokens/refresh`) or required notification (`turn/completed`) fails. Added or removed notifications are recorded without failing. Methods are read from `enum` or `const`, and malformed schemas are refused.
- `tests/compatibility_probe.rs::codex_protocol_diff_names_unhandled_requests_and_fails_closed`: a simulated `codex-cli 0.164.0` describes the baseline plus a new notification. It passes with `protocol-schema` among the receipt checks and the notification recorded. An extra server request fails with `failed check: protocol-schema` and names the method before the app-server handshake runs. A Codex without the schema subcommand fails closed (`generate-json-schema is unavailable or failed`). A receipt with the v1 contract is invalid.
- `known_broken::tests` (3): no verified baseline version is on the shipped list. Ranges are inclusive and per harness. `a_listed_version_is_refused_even_when_its_probe_passes` uses a simulated OMP `omp/19.0.0` run through the real `crewroom-process` helper. It passes the full probe and is launchable with an empty list. With a synthetic entry it is refused with the recorded reason (`failed check: known-broken`), and no probe process runs.
- `catalog::tests::replacement_attempts_reprobe_changed_executables_and_stale_receipts`: an admitted, claimed, stopped and paused attempt is replaced as resume does it (`replace_attempt`). The replacement keeps the frozen selection. After the executable changes and fails its re-probe, the replacement is held: `Launch held: the OMP executable changed after admission (omp/18.4.12) and its re-probe failed`. A passing `omp/19.1.0` re-probe is recorded on the replacement attempt with a v2 receipt. A selection whose receipt carries the v1 contract is re-probed even though the executable is unchanged; the new receipt reports the same SHA-256 as admission. An admitted version on a synthetic known-broken list is held with its reason.
- The existing FOR-69 compatibility, catalog and runner tests pass unchanged except the Codex receipt check list, which now includes `protocol-schema`. The Claude Code fixture asserts that `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false`, `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_CONNECT_IDE=0` and `CLAUDE_CODE_IDE_SKIP_AUTO_INSTALL=1` reach the launch handshake.

Claude Code isolation check (installed 2.1.303, read-only inspection, nothing launched): `claude --help` describes `--bare` as skipping hooks, LSP, plugin sync, attribution, auto-memory, background prefetches, keychain reads and CLAUDE.md discovery. It describes `--safe-mode` as disabling customizations including MCP servers. Neither mentions claude.ai connectors or IDE setup. The binary contains all three variables. Its IDE auto-connect returns early only on `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_CONNECT_IDE` false. Extension auto-install is skipped only by `CLAUDE_CODE_IDE_SKIP_AUTO_INSTALL` or a user setting. claude.ai connectors are also suppressed by safe mode and strict MCP configuration. Crewroom therefore sets all three in every probe and does not rely on `--bare`.

Live, isolated (installed CLIs; isolated HOME and configuration; no prompt sent):

- `CREWROOM_LIVE_DISCOVERY=1 cargo test -p crewroom-runtime --test compatibility_probe installed_harnesses -- --ignored`: **1/1**, contract `harness-compatibility-probe-v2`:
  - Codex `0.163.0` (verified; probe forced): checks `version, model-discovery, bundled-model-catalog, protocol-schema, control-protocol`. The real `generate-json-schema --experimental` matches the baseline exactly (no added or removed notifications). 8 offerings.
  - Claude Code `2.1.303` (probed): checks `version, model-discovery, stream-protocol`, with the isolation variables set. 5 offerings.
  - OMP `18.11.3` (verified; probe forced): `rpc-frame-contract` against a synthetic loopback provider that is never contacted. 1 offering.
  - Each CLI behind a wrapper reporting `99.0.0` is admitted as `compatible (probed 99.0.0)`. The wrapper simulates only the version string.
- Not live: the known-broken refusal and resume/replacement re-probe are simulated. The shipped list is empty, and no real run was resumed across a harness update. The operator service on port 4318 was not touched.

Checks at `9f9e217`:

- `npm test`: **345/345**. `npm run build`: passes.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop`: **581 passed / 0 failed / 18 ignored** over 67 test binaries.
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for these crates: clean.
- `npm run test:e2e`: not run (integrator).

## FOR-82 — Workflow and repository journeys without the TypeScript oracle (2026-10-19)

Code `0ef395d` (branch `for-82-work`, from main `9d409b5`). Every check below ran at `0ef395d` on Linux x86_64 with Docker Engine 29.7.2 (rootful) and Node 26, with Rust binaries built from this revision in a target directory private to the worktree (worktree prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`). Docker and Git are real; no provider, Jira or GitHub service was contacted. The machine was shared with parallel agents (load average 9–21).

**Before.** At main `9d409b5` `scripts/rust/repository.test.ts` was **16/19**: "native admission preserves operator changes…", "dirty manifests and bounded literal, regex and pinned searches…" and "provider merge records require actual ancestry…" stopped with "Workflow database is newer than this application." `workflow-transitions.test.ts` and `workflow-interruption.test.ts` passed and contain no TypeScript oracle.

**Oracle decision (consistent with FOR-61):** native-only expectations, not a pre-migration copy or fixed snapshot. The TypeScript `Workbench` stops at schema 26, and a pre-migration copy holds no native worktree record, so the retained `RepositoryWork` could not observe the state under test.
- The two oracle cases now compare the native file list and revision with values derived from the owned worktree using the retained contract in `server/repository-work.ts`: Git's tracked and non-ignored names in UTF-16 sort order, `lstat` mode, SHA-256 of content or link target, `deleted` entries, the worktree hash of that manifest, base/head/tree from Git, the dirty flag from porcelain status, the instruction hash of `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`, and the seeded requirements through the shared `requirementsHash`. The four searches the oracle compared are asserted as exact results.
- The provider-merge case used the `Workbench` to seed, not as an oracle. It now seeds the upstream ticket, accepted implementation plan, coding run, dependency and merged publication through native fixture operations. The native queue creates a coding run only after an accepted plan. `repository-fixture` gains a `set_dependencies` operation for this.

**Live journeys (real engine):** `repository.test.ts`, `workflow-transitions.test.ts` and `workflow-interruption.test.ts` **26/26**, run twice. The Docker-backed cases (ad-hoc read-only preparation, coding handoff with actual checks) ran real containers. `docker ps -a --filter label=io.crewroom.command` was empty before and after each run.

**Other checks at `0ef395d`:** `npm test` **345/345** (simulated providers); `npm run build` passes (existing chunk-size warning); `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-runtime -- --test-threads=4` **205 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**; `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` clean. `npm run test:e2e` was not run here (the integrator runs it).

**Found, not fixed here:** `scripts/rust/target-refresh.test.ts` (8/9, the same oracle failure) and `retained-work.test.ts` (1/10, mostly "Needs an accepted implementation plan.") fail at `9d409b5`; tracked in FOR-84. Not re-run: rootless Docker, macOS.

**Integration at `676f759` on main `9d409b5`** (fast-forward, no rebase; same code tree as `0ef395d`), all through `scripts/system_limits.py run` with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and the worktree prepared with `--build-helpers`: `npm test` **345/345**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**; `repository.test.ts`, `workflow-transitions.test.ts` and `workflow-interruption.test.ts` **26/26** against real Docker, with no `io.crewroom.command` container before or after; `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-runtime -- --test-threads=4` **205 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**; Clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` and fmt clean.

## FOR-69 — Newer harness versions run after a compatibility probe (2026-10-19)

Implementation `5d15a5f` on branch `for-69-probed-harness-versions` from main `a1faa87`. All checks below ran at `5d15a5f` in a fresh worktree with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, wrapped by `scripts/system_limits.py` (FOR-68).

Simulated (Python harnesses that speak each launch protocol; no inference, no network):

- `compatibility::tests` (3): per-harness version parsing; listed, newer, new-major, older and unparseable versions are classified for each harness, refusals naming `failed check: version` and a remedy; receipts bind harness, version, canonical path and content hash, and a forged receipt (inference flag, missing check) is invalid.
- `tests/compatibility_probe.rs` (4): for each harness a newer and a new-major version (`codex-cli 1.0.0`, `3.0.0 (Claude Code)`, `omp/19.0.0`) pass the probe with one handshake each and are labeled `compatible (probed <version>)`; verified versions run no handshake; older and unparseable versions are refused before any handshake, and a probe-failing version is refused with `failed check: control-protocol`, `stream-protocol` or `rpc-frame-contract`; a retained receipt skips the handshake and a rebuilt executable with the same version is probed again; a refresh retains the receipt across restart, a launch re-probe reuses it, and a failing replacement is reported without retaining a receipt.
- `runner::tests::launch_admits_listed_or_exactly_probed_versions`: a native adapter starts a listed version or the exact probed version and executable recorded in the frozen selection; no receipt, another version, changed content or another harness's receipt is refused.
- `catalog::tests::probed_newer_versions_are_offered_and_changed_executables_hold_launch`: a probed OMP 19.0.0 gets an execution profile and no execution note; admission records version and receipt (`inferenceRequested: false`); after the executable changes, the launch is held with `Launch held: the OMP executable changed after admission (omp/19.0.0) and its re-probe failed`; a receipt for other content never makes the version launchable.
- Service and desktop: the catalog view reports `compatibility` per harness (`verified` for Codex 0.163.0); the workbench shows it in the harness row and the route hint (`Version: verified`).

Live, isolated (installed CLIs on this machine; isolated HOME and configuration; no prompt sent):

- `CREWROOM_LIVE_DISCOVERY=1 cargo test -p crewroom-runtime --test compatibility_probe -- --ignored`: **1/1**. Claude Code `2.1.303` basis `probed`, checks `version, model-discovery, stream-protocol`, 5 offerings. Codex `0.163.0` and OMP `18.11.3` are verified; the probe was forced and passed `bundled-model-catalog, control-protocol` and `rpc-frame-contract` respectively (8 and 1 offerings; OMP against a synthetic loopback provider that is never contacted). Each CLI behind a wrapper that reports version `99.0.0` and delegates every other call to the real binary is admitted as `compatible (probed 99.0.0)`. The wrapper simulates the version string; the protocols exercised are the real binaries'.
- `scripts/verify_unconfigured_catalog.py --port 4397` with a new private data directory and no runner configuration: state `success`; OMP `omp/18.11.3` `verified` (0 offerings: no provider configured, as before), Codex `codex-cli 0.163.0` `verified` (8), Claude Code `2.1.303 (Claude Code)` `compatible (probed 2.1.303)` (5), no execution notes, no runs. Job selections `code` = a Claude Code offering and `judgment` = a Codex offering were saved (revision 1) and read back unchanged. Before this change the operator's catalog refused Claude Code 2.1.303 as unverified.
- Not done here: the operator service on port 4318 was not touched; its restart and model selection in that app are left to the coordinator.

Checks at `5d15a5f`:

- `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop -- --test-threads=4`: **391 passed / 0 failed / 18 ignored** over 33 test binaries. The first run failed one desktop expectation for the new route hint text; it was updated and the rerun passed.
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for those crates: clean.
- `npm test`: **345/345**. `npm run build`: passes.
- `npm run test:e2e`: not run (integrator).

Review fixes at `591968c` (parent `1efaf36`). Two blocking findings were fixed. At `5d15a5f` a passing launch-time re-probe still held the launch until a refresh and re-admission. FOR-69 asks for a hold only on failure. Also, a newer OMP with no provider configuration was refused as a `model-discovery` probe failure because it lists zero models.

- `catalog::tests::passing_reprobe_of_a_changed_executable_lets_the_launch_proceed` (simulated OMP run through the real `crewroom-process` helper): the admitted OMP `omp/18.4.12` self-updates to `omp/19.0.1`. The re-probe passes and returns the selection with the new identity (`basis: probed`, `inferenceRequested: false`, the new SHA-256). The receipt reads `admittedVersion omp/18.4.12`, `version omp/19.0.1`, `compatible (probed 19.0.1)`. Offering, billing route, effort, preview revision and the resolved route are unchanged. `verify_frozen_catalog_route` accepts the new selection, the catalog retains the probe receipt, and `record_attempt_reprobe` stores the model and a timestamped `reprobes` entry on the attempt. A second check of the recorded selection finds nothing to re-probe. The store refuses a re-probe record that also changes the effort. A further update to `omp/19.0.2` that no longer lists the admitted model is held with `… is compatible (probed 19.0.2), but no longer lists Example`.
- `catalog::tests::probed_newer_versions_are_offered_and_changed_executables_hold_launch`: an unchanged executable needs no re-probe, and a failing one is still held with `re-probe failed`.
- `tests/compatibility_probe.rs::newer_omp_without_configured_providers_is_compatible_with_no_offerings`: a simulated OMP `omp/18.11.4` that lists no models still runs its RPC handshake. Directly and through a catalog refresh, it is labelled `compatible (probed 18.11.4)` and is launchable, with 0 offerings and no error. Codex and Claude Code still need at least one offering.

## FOR-70 — Add a repository by choosing a folder (2026-10-19)

Implementation `4f9f295` on branch `for-70-repository-folder-picker` from main `a1faa87`. The core module is `crates/crewroom-core/src/repository_folders.rs`, and the service routes are `GET /api/repository-folders/inspect` and `POST /api/repository-folders/initialize`. The desktop flow is in `crates/crewroom-desktop/src/workbench/settings/repository_folder.rs`.

Real Git, temporary directories, no remote contacted (`crates/crewroom-core/tests/repository_folders.rs`, 6 tests):

- Refusals with reason and remedy: relative or empty path, missing folder, file, unreadable folder (mode 000), bare repository, folder inside `.git`, repository without commits, Crewroom-owned worktree, `.git` symlink, and a checkout an active repository already uses. The repository being edited is excluded.
- A subfolder resolves to the root, with the notice `Using repository root <root>`. Name, branches, default branch from `origin/HEAD` (`develop`), current branch, and publish remote `origin` are prefilled. Remote kinds are GitHub (`Octo-Fixture/Sandbox`), Bitbucket and unrecognised. A synthetic credentialed address never appears in the answer. A snapshot of every file's mtime and size under the checkout is identical before and after inspection.
- Default fallbacks: a remote `HEAD` with no local branch gives `origin/trunk`, and no remote gives the current branch.
- A plain folder with one file yields `notRepository` and stays unchanged. An unconfirmed request or an invalid branch writes nothing. Confirmed initialization on `trunk` gives `rev-list --count` 1, an empty tree, an empty index and `?? existing.txt`. Repeating it, initializing a subfolder of the new repository, or initializing a missing folder is refused, and nothing is written.

Real local service over HTTP (`crates/crewroom-service/tests/repository_folders.rs`): inspection without the service token is 403 and writes nothing. Initialization without an `Origin` header or from a foreign origin is 403. An unconfirmed request is 400. A confirmed same-origin request is 200, with one commit on `trunk` and no indexed files. `POST /api/repositories` then re-verifies and saves, and a second inspection is refused as already configured unless `repository=<id>` names the repository being edited.

Desktop over the real authenticated service, GPUI test platform with simulated picker answers, no window on the operator's desktop (`crates/crewroom-desktop/src/workbench/settings/tests.rs`):

- `choosing_a_subfolder_prefills_name_branches_and_remotes`: Choose folder issues a directory-only, single-selection prompt. The picked subfolder fills the root path, name `Sandbox` and target `develop`. The UI shows the root notice, the default-branch source, `Publish remote · origin · github.com/Octo-Fixture/Sandbox` and one branch choice per branch. Choosing `main` and saving persists it. A new draft for the same checkout shows the refusal and the remedy.
- `cancelled_or_unavailable_picker_keeps_the_typed_path`: a cancelled picker leaves the name and path unchanged. An unavailable picker (a simulated portal error, since the test platform cannot fail a prompt) shows its reason, and Inspect path on a typed path still prefills.
- `initializing_a_plain_folder_requires_confirmation`: Don't initialize leaves no `.git`. After confirmation the folder has one empty commit on `trunk` and an untracked `notes.txt`, and Save succeeds.
- `repository_path_and_branch_errors_are_distinct` (rewritten): each field shows its problem before saving. Save names its blockers and sends nothing while it is blocked. An unknown branch and an invalid prefix are reported inline. A branch renamed after inspection is still refused by the service on save (`Target branch 'main' was not found.`).
- The existing repository tests now inspect the typed path before saving and pass unchanged otherwise.

Checks at `4f9f295` (private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `-j6`, `--test-threads=4`, all through `scripts/system_limits.py run`):

- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` for these crates: clean.
- `npm run test:e2e`: not run (integrator). The browser suite exercises the legacy TypeScript server, which this change does not touch.
