## FOR-60 — `npm test` under machine load, closed (2026-10-18)

Code revision `6791de4` (branch `for-60-close`, from main `4a2ac78`). It has two commits: `0007bf0` names the readiness blockers in the DEL-04/05/06 assertion message, and `6791de4` makes the lease helper's owner receipt atomic. This closes the two criteria that the first pass (section below) left partial, and adds one fix. Machine: 16 cores, shared with other agents. Every run started from this agent session, which has `RLIMIT_RTTIME` soft = hard = 0 (`grep "realtime timeout" /proc/self/limits`). Load came from a loop of clean `cargo build --locked -j16 -p crewroom-desktop` builds in a private target directory, started through `system_limits.py run`. All 93 builds succeeded, and the 1-minute load was about 17–25 for the whole series. All tests used simulated providers and fixture Git remotes; nothing contacted a live service.

**Five consecutive runs through the supported path** (`python3 scripts/system_limits.py run -- npm test`, default concurrency, worktree prepared with `prepare_worktree.py`, tree clean at `6791de4` except the untracked load build directory):

| Run | Result | Duration | 1-min load start → end |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 345 passed, 0 failed | 359 s | 18.6 → 20.3 |
| 2 | 345 / 0 | 333 s | 20.3 → 18.5 |
| 3 | 345 / 0 | 343 s | 18.5 → 19.4 |
| 4 | 345 / 0 | 348 s | 19.4 → 20.8 |
| 5 | 345 / 0 | 322 s | 20.8 → 21.2 |

Under the same load, `npm test` started directly in this session (not through the wrapper) failed 3 of 3 times at `4a2ac78`: 342/340, 335/332 and 336/334 tests/passed. An earlier wrapped series of five at `4a2ac78` (plus only the assertion-message change) passed four runs 343/343. Its fifth run failed REVIEW-R2 `Both independently leased processes became live.`, which led to the receipt fix below. After the series, `npm run build` passes (with the existing Vite chunk-size warning) and `npm run test:e2e` passes **31/31** (port 4331, isolated data directory, through the wrapper).

**Root cause per named failure.** "Captured" means the failing run recorded the kill. A scratch `--import` hook on `ChildProcess.prototype.spawn` logged every child that exited by signal, with its arguments, age, and whether Node had sent the signal. TAP recorded the file processes. "Reproduced" means a PATH shim made exactly one chosen Git child SIGKILL itself, inside the wrapper so that no RTTIME kill could interfere. To its Node parent, such a child looks the same as a kernel RTTIME kill: `signal: 'SIGKILL'`, `killed: false`.

| Failure (issue) | Evidence | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| Whole test files end with no failing subtest; totals vary (FOR-60, FOR-63 `live-viewer`, `cancel-continuation`; FOR-66) | Captured: TAP `signal: 'SIGKILL'`, `exitCode: ~` for `feedback-continuation.test.ts` in a direct focused run. Direct full runs lost `feedback-continuation`, `repository-work` and `repository-http` files, and a `target-refresh` file stopped 13 subtests early. | RTTIME kill of the file's `node` process (FOR-68). |
| `Repository configuration is unavailable.` (FOR-60, M6.3) | Captured 3 times: the configuration probe `git config --includes --name-only --get-regexp ^filter\.` was SIGKILLed 2–27 ms after spawn, with Node not the sender, in the run where the test failed. | RTTIME kill of that Git child. `RepositoryWork.git` fails closed. |
| `Git could not complete check-ref-format / rev-parse / worktree …` (FOR-60, M6.3) | Captured: `check-ref-format` (twice) and `rev-parse --verify` (twice) SIGKILLed at 0–3 ms, each with its matching message. | Same. |
| `Controller lock failed.` (M6.3 interrupted reasoned retry; same class) | Captured: `process-lease.py hold` SIGKILLed 6 ms after spawn. | Same, on the lease helper. |
| `Publication process ownership is unresolved.` (FOR-66) | Captured: `/bin/ps -axo pid=,pgid=,stat=,lstart=` SIGKILLed 46 ms after spawn, during the failing M6.3 case. That `ps` is the process-table read in `inspectOwned`. Its rejection reaches the catch-all `uncertain`, and `PublicationGit.run` reports that as unresolved ownership. | Same. The first pass inferred a killed Git leader instead; this capture shows the killed `ps` reader. |
| DEL-04/05/06 `evidence.assess(...).ready === false` (FOR-66) | Reproduced: across a sweep that killed the K-th `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all` (K = 1…34), the kill at K = 24 failed exactly this assertion with blocker `Git could not complete status. Retained work needs inspection.` Other K values failed other assertions that also depend on Git, or passed. Directly in this session under load, the file passed 19 of 20 times, and its one failure was a SIGKILLed file process. | A killed Git read in `RepositoryWork.status`, which readiness correctly reports as a blocker. **Not a timing assumption.** `assess` has a single await, `work.status`, and then decides synchronously from the store. The test records the passing check and review synchronously before it calls `assess`. Neither `Publication` nor `Evidence` starts a timer or background writer that could change those records. |
| REP-02 `Target branch must resolve to a commit` instead of the launch-reference error (FOR-63) | Reproduced 7 of 7: killing any `rev-parse --verify … refs/heads/<target>^{commit}` gives exactly this input to the failed `assert.rejects`. | A killed `rev-parse` in `validateRepository`: its fallback to `refs/remotes/` then fails, and validation fails closed. |
| EVD-04/DEL-01 "expected a requirements blocker" (FOR-63) | Reproduced: killing `git status` during the case fails `assert.ok(stale.blockers.some(item => /requirements/.test(item)))`, because the blocker becomes `Git could not complete status.` | Killed Git read; readiness fails closed. |
| `replacing a failed check environment …` (FOR-63) | Reproduced: killing `git status` fails it with `false !== true` and `Git could not complete status.` | Same. |
| `FileExistsError … owner.json` from a duplicate checked helper (FOR-54/60/63) | Fixed in the first pass (`3ec4f26`), with a test. | A real lease race. |
| REVIEW-R2 `Both independently leased processes became live.` (seen here inside the wrapper) | `process-lease.py` created `owner.json` with `open('x')` and wrote it afterwards. `liveLeases` waits for the file to appear and then inspects it, so it could read an empty receipt (`Launch ownership cannot be established.`). The scratch probe mirrors `liveLeases`, inspecting two leases as each `owner.json` appears, at load about 19: 14 of 300 inspections were `uncertain` with the old helper and 0 of 300 with the fix. | **Fixed (`6791de4`).** The receipt is written to `owner.json.partial`, fsynced, hard-linked to `owner.json` (which fails if one exists) and the partial file removed, like the native `publish_owner` (FOR-64). A checked helper that finds a leftover partial receipt refuses with `Process lease already held.`. New tests: "an owner receipt is never visible before it is complete" freezes the real helper as it starts writing. "A checked helper killed while writing its receipt leaves a lease no duplicate can execute" checks the leftover case. On the old helper both fail: the first sees `owner.json` while it is being written, and in the second the duplicate ran the command. |
| `Coding schedule reconciliation failed: fetch failed / bad port` (FOR-60, FOR-63) | Unchanged from the first pass: log output from a test that configures Mail at port 9. | Not a failure. |

No assertion was weakened, skipped or given a longer timeout. Kill logs, run logs and shim scripts stayed in the session's scratch directory and are not committed.

**Limitations.** The cause is outside Crewroom. Commands that an agent session starts directly still inherit the zero limit until Claude Code stops lowering it. The DEL-04/05/06, REP-02, EVD-04/DEL-01 and check-environment failures were reproduced by a chosen kill, not captured in a natural RTTIME kill during these runs. For each of them, a killed Git read is the only nondeterministic input that produces the observed message.

## FOR-68 — The outside SIGKILL is the kernel's RLIMIT_RTTIME check (2026-10-18)

Code `54102f6` and `320ea14` (branch `for-68-desktop-test-sigkill`, from main `c9b9a31`). These change only Python scripts, one TypeScript test and docs. The Rust sources in the cargo runs below are those of `c9b9a31`. All runs are local tests with simulated providers and fixture Git remotes. Nothing contacted a live service. The machine has 16 cores and is shared with other agents. No privileged tracing was used.

**Sender.** No process sends the signal. The kernel sends it. The Claude Code 2.1.302 process that runs the agent sessions has `RLIMIT_RTTIME` soft = hard = 0 (`Max realtime timeout 0 0 us` in `/proc/<pid>/limits`). Its parent shell, `herdr`, the terminal, Hyprland and `systemd --user` all have `unlimited`. A plain Bun 1.3.14 started from a clean service keeps `unlimited`, so Claude Code itself lowers the limit. The code path that does this was not found. Every command an agent starts inherits the limit, including `systemd-run --user --scope` and `setsid` children. An unprivileged process cannot raise a hard limit. When the kernel's CPU-timer check evaluates a thread, a zero hard limit leads to SIGKILL ("at the hard limit, send SIGKILL"). This explains every earlier observation: no sender in the process tree or PID namespace, no OOM, kills at random points in a run, and none in `systemd --user` services, which get systemd's default limits.

| Check | Placement | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Arm a 10 ms `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID` timer and busy-loop (`scripts/system_limits.py probe`) | Agent shell (RTTIME 0/0) | SIGKILL (exit 137), every time |
| Same | `systemd-run --user` service (unlimited) | SIGALRM delivered, exit 0 |
| Same | `systemd-run --user -p LimitRTTIME=0` service | `Result=signal`, status 9 |
| Canary that forks short `sh -c` loops and logs each child that ends with SIGKILL (it sends no signals itself) | Agent tree; agent tree with minimal environment; `setsid` orphan reparented to `systemd --user` | 11 children SIGKILLed 3–19 ms after spawn in about 30 min |
| Same canary | `systemd-run --user` service, with and without the agent's full environment | 0 of about 10 million children in 40–50 min each |
| Same canary | `systemd-run --user -p LimitRTTIME=0` service | 1 child SIGKILLed (6 ms) in 25 min |

So the limit alone decides. Process ancestry, cgroup, session and environment do not. A process logger that recorded every new process system-wide was itself SIGKILLed after about 790 s in the agent tree. In that time no `kill`, `pkill`, `killall` or `fuser` process started, including around two canary kills it covered.

**Desktop and four-crate runs, default test threads.**

| Placement | Runs | Result | Load (1 min) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Agent tree, `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop` | 3 + 6 | 3 of 3 passed at load 4–7. Then 3 passed, and runs 4–6 lost the test binary to SIGKILL after 48 s, 77 s and 134 s | 4–40 |
| Agent tree, four crates | 1 | desktop binary SIGKILLed 9.4 s into its run; the other binaries passed (397 / 0 / 2) | 7–14 |
| A/B beside 12 CPU burners, desktop binary, alternating | 2 + 2 | agent tree: 2 of 2 SIGKILLed (125 s, 161 s). `system_limits.py run`: 2 of 2 passed, 149 / 0 / 15 each | 14–24 |

**Five consecutive four-crate runs through `python3 scripts/system_limits.py run -- cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop -j6`** (a transient `systemd --user` service, default test threads, private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`):

| Run | Result (64 test binaries) | Duration | Load (1/5/15 min) start → end |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 546 passed, 0 failed, 17 ignored | 481 s | 27.2 / 26.8 / 23.9 → 16.0 / 20.9 / 22.6 |
| 2 | 546 / 0 / 17 | 459 s | 16.0 / 20.9 / 22.6 → 11.5 / 17.9 / 21.2 |
| 3 | 546 / 0 / 17 | 408 s | 11.5 / 17.9 / 21.2 → 10.4 / 15.5 / 19.4 |
| 4 | 546 / 0 / 17 | 341 s | 10.4 / 15.5 / 19.4 → 3.1 / 10.6 / 16.4 |
| 5 | 546 / 0 / 17 | 304 s | 3.1 / 10.6 / 16.4 → 1.5 / 6.9 / 13.4 |

No binary was killed. Load fell during the series, so runs 4 and 5 ran on a quieter machine than the agent-tree runs that failed. The A/B pair above compares the two placements at the same load.

**Change.** `scripts/system_limits.py` has three commands. `check` reports the limit and exits 1 when it is unsafe. `probe` reproduces the kill in the current process. `run -- <command>` runs the command directly when the limit is safe and as a transient `systemd --user` service otherwise. The environment goes to the service through a private 0600 file that is deleted before the command starts, so no value appears on a command line. Without a user manager the command is refused with the reason. `prepare_worktree.py` warns about the limit. The operations guide and testing guide record the cause. Nine unit and live kernel tests (`scripts/test_system_limits.py`) run inside `npm test`. No test assertion was weakened or skipped, and no timeout changed.

**Other checks at `320ea14`.** `npm test` **341/341** through `system_limits.py run` and **341/341** directly in the agent tree (load about 2). An earlier wrapped run at `54102f6` failed only `prepare-worktree.test.ts`, which pinned the old Python test count of 6; `320ea14` fixes it. `npm run build` passes (existing Vite chunk-size warning). No Rust source changed, so Clippy and fmt were not rerun. `npm run test:e2e` is left to the integrator.

**Limitations.** The cause sits in Claude Code, outside Crewroom. The wrapper avoids it but does not remove it. Commands started directly by an agent session still inherit the zero limit. Evidence runs must use the wrapper, or a shell that Claude Code did not start, until Claude Code stops lowering the limit. The exact kernel condition that triggers the check in processes that set no CPU timers (the `sh` canaries, test binaries) was inferred from the measurements above. Kernel sources were not available to confirm it.

**Integration at `29cfadb` and `ef95cf5`** (rebased onto main `90b46ab` without code changes; only the evidence docs conflicted and both sides were kept; worktree prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py`; every command through `python3 scripts/system_limits.py run --`, because the integrating agent session also inherited RTTIME 0/0; 1-minute load about 1–4):

- `npm test` **343/343**.
- `npm run build` passes (existing Vite chunk-size warning).
- `npm run test:e2e` **31/31** (port 4331, isolated data directory).
- `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop -j6`, default test threads, private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: **548 passed / 0 failed / 17 ignored** over 64 test binaries, no binary killed. The two extra tests are FOR-54's service tests on main.
- No Rust source changed in FOR-68, so Clippy and fmt were not rerun.

## FOR-54 — Usage counts only started attempts as unrecorded (2026-10-18)

Implementation `96565b807f160308074ff64f766ae682881e6f8e` on branch `for-54-usage-unrecorded-started-only-rebased` from main `db0be2c`. It is `a41f46ed56f3883f92508acaeec0324e85cabd66` (branch `for-54-usage-unrecorded-started-only`, from main `aa2d619`) rebased without code changes; the original evidence commit `e10dc36` was replaced by this section. `route_total` in `crates/crewroom-service/src/views/history.rs` counted unrecorded-route attempts over every route member, including attempts still awaiting start, while `attempts` counted only started ones. It now counts started attempts only.

Simulated (SQLite store fixtures, no OS processes, no inference):

- `queued_attempts_are_neither_started_nor_unrecorded`: a research run with a started API attempt, a started attempt without a recorded route, a queued attempt without a recorded route, and a queued subscription attempt. Unknown route: 1 attempt, 1 unrecorded. API: 1 attempt, 0 unrecorded. The subscription route holds only the queued attempt: awaiting start, 0 attempts, 0 unrecorded, no tokens. Every route has unrecorded ≤ attempts, and there is no cross-route total.
- `queued_attempt_counts_as_unrecorded_once_started`: one attempt without a recorded route reads 0 attempts / 0 unrecorded while admitted, then 1 / 1 after it is claimed.
- At `96565b8`, with `members` temporarily restored in place of `started`, both tests fail; with the fix, both pass. The existing FOR-44 tests (recorded attempts on all three routes; historical unrecorded attempt) still pass.

Checks at `96565b8` (private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` in the worktree, `-j6`, 1-minute load average 7–12):

- `npm run build`: passes.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=4`: all suites pass (lib 13, daemon 4 after `cargo build -p crewroom-runtime --bins` in the fresh target, FOR-59).
- `cargo clippy -p crewroom-service --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-service --check`: clean.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop usage`: 2/2 (`usage_is_grouped_by_billing_route_without_a_dollar_figure_for_subscription`, `missing_cells_and_unavailable_usage_never_become_positive_results`); the desktop crate is unchanged and renders the service counts.
- `npm run test:e2e`: not run (integrator).

## FOR-60 — `npm test` under machine load (2026-10-18)

Code revision `45b434f` (branch `for-60-reliable-npm-test`, from main `2b1b5e7`), integrated on main as `3ec4f26` without code changes. This also covers the failures listed in FOR-63 and FOR-66. All runs are local tests with simulated providers and fixture Git remotes; nothing contacted a live service. The machine has 16 cores and is shared with other agents. Load came from 16 busy-loop CPU burners (`sh -c 'while :; do :; done'`) running for the whole series, plus the other agents' builds. Each run used `tsx --test --test-reporter=tap tests/*.test.ts`, which is the `npm test` command with a TAP reporter added so that each failed file's exit signal is recorded.

**Acceptance status.** The lease-race fix and its test are met; `npm run build` passes. Two criteria are **partially met**, and FOR-60 is blocked by FOR-68:

- *Root cause recorded for each observed failure: partially met.* Captured with a kill signal: whole test files ending without a failing subtest (TAP `signal: 'SIGKILL'`) and one Git child error (`signal: 'SIGKILL'`, `killed: false`). The sender of that SIGKILL is unidentified (FOR-68). **Inferred, not captured:** DEL-04/05/06 `evidence.assess(...).ready === false` (FOR-66), REP-02 `Target branch must resolve to a commit`, the check-environment and publication EVD-04/DEL-01 blockers (FOR-63), and `Publication process ownership is unresolved` (not reproduced in 320 launches). None of these was recorded alongside a SIGKILLed Git child or file process. FOR-66 also asked to rule out a timing assumption in readiness; that was not investigated.
- *`npm test` passes 5 consecutive times under load: partially met.* The 5/5 (and 8/8) passes come only from runs started as a `systemd-run --user` service, outside the agent session's process tree. Inside that tree, where every FOR-60, FOR-63 and FOR-66 failure was observed, 0 of 4 runs passed at this revision.

Review-fix check (docs only, same code as `45b434f`): one literal `npm test` inside the agent's process tree passed 340/340 at load 4.5 (1 min). That is a single unloaded run and does not close the loaded criterion.

**Integration at `3ec4f26`** (rebased onto main `d42130c`; docs conflicts only, resolved by keeping every entry; worktree prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py`): `npm test` **342/342** inside the agent's process tree at 1-minute load 7–12 (a single, lightly loaded run that does not close the loaded criterion); `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**. No Rust code changed, so no cargo tests were run. Simulated providers only.

To close either criterion: FOR-68 names the sender and the suite passes inside an agent session, or failing runs record a SIGKILLed Git child or file process for each inferred failure above, and readiness timing is ruled out.

**Root causes (observed and inferred).**

| Failure (issue) | Cause | Change |
|---|---|---|
| Duplicate checked helper reports `FileExistsError: … owner.json` instead of `Process lease already held.` (FOR-60, FOR-63; reported at FOR-54) | A real race in `server/runtime/process-lease.py`. The lease is one-shot, but the lock alone does not show that. Under load, the losing helper can reach `flock` only after the winner has exited, so the lock is free. It then failed while creating `owner.json` with `O_EXCL`. Nothing ran twice, but the reason was wrong. | After taking the lock, an existing `owner.json` shows the lease was used. The helper exits with `Process lease already held.` before reading the manifest or opening logs. The "duplicate helpers execute at most once" test now also starts a helper after the winner has exited, which is the case the scheduler hits under load. It requires the lease message, no `FileExistsError` or traceback, and output and logs unchanged. Without the fix, the new assertion fails with the exact `FileExistsError` from FOR-54. |
| Whole test files end with no failing subtest, so the totals vary (283–337) (FOR-60: `live-viewer`, `cancel-continuation`; here: `admission-restart-review`, `feedback-continuation`, `publication`, `coding`, `target-refresh`) | The file's `node` process was killed by SIGKILL. The TAP record shows `signal: 'SIGKILL'`, `exitCode: ~`. Node's test runner never sends SIGKILL to test files, and nothing in `server/` or `tests/` signals a process it did not start. | None in code (see the next section). |
| Git-backed cases fail closed in different tests on each run: `Repository configuration is unavailable.`, `Git could not complete rev-parse/worktree …`, `Target branch must resolve to a commit …`, `evidence.assess(...).ready === false` (the DEL-04/05/06 feedback case), REP-02 launch-reference errors, check-environment and publication blockers (FOR-60, FOR-63, FOR-66) | *Inferred* for the named readiness, REP-02, check-environment and publication failures: the same outside SIGKILL hits one Git child. Only one captured error records `signal: 'SIGKILL'` with `killed: false`. A Node `execFile` timeout would set `killed: true` and send SIGTERM, so this kill came from outside the process. Each caller then correctly fails closed: the configuration probe, `RepositoryWork.git`, the repository validation, and readiness (which reports the unavailable revision as a blocker). | None. Retrying a Git command that was killed would hide an uncertain outcome. |
| `Publication process ownership is unresolved.` after `close` (FOR-66) | *Inferred*, not reproduced. No case reproduced it here. 320 owned `git ls-remote` launches (8 at a time, load ~31) were each inspected right after `close` and all were `stopped`. It fits the same kill. When a Git leader is SIGKILLed while a child of it (for example the local transport's `upload-pack`/`receive-pack`) still holds the inherited lease lock, `inspectOwned` correctly reports the surviving holder as uncertain. | None. Treating it as stopped would weaken process ownership. |
| `Coding schedule reconciliation failed: fetch failed` / `bad port` (FOR-60, FOR-63) | Log output, not a failure. The REVIEW-R2 "HTTP Save launch details" case in `tests/scheduler-replay-holds.test.ts` configures Mail at `http://127.0.0.1:9/mcp/`. Port 9 is on undici's blocked-port list, so the unavailable integration is reported truthfully and the scheduler retries on its next signal. The test passes. | None. |

**Where the SIGKILL comes from.** The same revision and load gave these results:

| Where the run was started | Runs | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Inside the agent session's process tree (a Bash tool background task); unloaded working tree, then 2 loaded runs | 3 | 0 of 3 passed: 3/334, 3/337 and 3/301 failures. File processes and Git children were SIGKILLed. |
| `systemd-run --user --scope`: a new cgroup, but still a descendant of the agent process | 1 | Failed: the `target-refresh` file process was SIGKILLed (290/291). |
| `systemd-run --user` service: a child of `systemd --user`, outside the agent's tree, with the same burners | 3 + 5 | 8 of 8 passed, 340/340 each. |

The trigger follows the agent session's process tree, not the cgroup. Canary processes that block and log every catchable termination signal were never signalled. That fits a direct SIGKILL to chosen PIDs, not a SIGTERM-then-SIGKILL sweep. Earlier FOR-58 evidence also ruled out OOM and kills by process name. Without kernel signal tracing, which needs operator approval (FOR-68), the sending program is still unnamed. These results narrow the search to programs that act on descendants of the agent session. They do not show that each named Git-backed failure was such a kill. The [testing guide](feature-map/README.md#fresh-worktrees) now shows how to run repeated-run evidence outside that tree.

**Five consecutive runs at `45b434f`, outside the agent's process tree only** (a `systemd-run --user` service, 16 CPU burners for the whole series):

| Run | Result | Duration | Load (1/5/15 min) at end |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 340 passed, 0 failed | 360 s | 29.7 / 29.3 / 32.5 |
| 2 | 340 / 0 | 338 s | 21.9 / 26.4 / 30.5 |
| 3 | 340 / 0 | 333 s | 23.6 / 25.4 / 29.0 |
| 4 | 340 / 0 | 303 s | 24.4 / 24.2 / 27.5 |
| 5 | 340 / 0 | 324 s | 30.3 / 25.2 / 26.8 |

Three more service runs passed 340/340 earlier, with the same change uncommitted on `2b1b5e7` (load 30–54). No assertion was weakened or skipped, and no timeout was changed. `npm run build` passes.

## FOR-64 — Model discovery cancellation under load (2026-10-18)

Code revision `d3247fb` (branch `for-64-discovery-cancel-flake`, from main `2b1b5e7`; rebased without code changes onto main `35f1922` as `e73a484`; the repeated-run evidence below was recorded at `d3247fb`), built in a private target directory in the worktree. All results are local tests with Python fixture harnesses; nothing contacted a live service. Other agents were building and testing on the same 16-core machine throughout.

**Root cause.** Recorded by FOR-58 (`8f39564`). Cancellation killed the `crewroom-process` helper before it published `owner.json`. Inspection cannot read an owner, so it reported uncertain ownership. `close()` now treats a reaped helper with no receipt, a free lease and an empty process group as stopped. One window remained: the helper creates `owner.json` and then writes it, so a kill between those two steps left a torn receipt, and inspection reports that as uncertain. At `d3247fb` the helper writes `owner.json.partial` and hard-links it to `owner.json`, which fails if a receipt already exists. A torn file can no longer be read as a receipt. A missing receipt still proves the command never ran. A held lease, a surviving group member or a complete receipt still goes through `stop_owned` and fails closed. Unit tests cover both outcomes, including a leftover partial file.

**Tests.** New: cancellation before start returns "cancelled" and creates nothing. Cancellation after the harness writes a readiness file returns "cancelled", leaves no member of the group alive and inspects as `stopped`. Because it waits for that file, this case does not depend on scheduler speed. The existing 100 ms cancel-anywhere step now has a 20 s bound instead of 3 s, so the timeout cannot win first. `failures_are_sanitized_and_children_are_reaped` used a 200 ms bound for every body, so at a load average near 60 it timed out in the version step: 112 of 958 suite runs failed before this change. Only the hung-harness body keeps a short bound (5 s). The assertions are unchanged.

**Repeated runs at `d3247fb`.** Each of the three cancellation tests ran 20 consecutive times as a separate process while 16 copies of the same test looped beside it. Load average was 24–29.

| Test | Consecutive | Concurrent load copies |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cancellation_unavailable_version_drift_and_cursor_loops_fail_closed` | **20/20** | 413 pass / 0 fail |
| `cancellation_after_the_harness_starts_stops_its_group_and_reports_cancelled` | **20/20** | 468 pass / 0 fail |
| `cancellation_before_start_launches_nothing` | **20/20** | 4941 pass / 0 fail |

A 180 s soak then ran 16 concurrent copies of the full `model_discovery` binary (`--test-threads=4`): **425 pass / 0 fail**, load 34/31/33. On the uncommitted working tree, without the bound change, the cancel-anywhere test passed 100/100 consecutive runs under 16 load copies at load average 37.

**Remaining external kills (FOR-68).** In earlier soaks at load average near 60, about 1 suite run in 400 failed with "Harness rejected the discovery command". Temporary diagnostics, removed before the commit, reported `CLD_KILLED`/`SIGKILL` for the helper or its `ps` child, about 25 ms after spawn. A log of every signal that discovery and `stop_owned` sent showed none sent to those processes. During the same soaks these processes stayed alive: 35 long-lived sleepers named like Crewroom processes, 300 other long-lived sleepers, and 117k short-lived unrelated sleepers. No OOM events were recorded. The sender is still unknown, which matches FOR-68. In that case discovery failed closed with an error and returned no partial catalog.

**Checks at `d3247fb`.** `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime` (`--test-threads=4`): all pass, including `model_discovery` 7 / 1 ignored. `cargo test -p crewroom-service`: all pass. `cargo clippy -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` pass. `npm test` **340/340**. `npm run build` passes. The integrator runs `npm run test:e2e`; it was not run here.

**Integration at `e73a484`** (rebased onto main `35f1922`; only the evidence docs conflicted; worktree prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py --build-helpers`, private target directory, load average 3–24): `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=4` **230 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**; `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` clean. `npm run build` passes. `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**. The first full `npm test` run passed 339/340: in `tests/assignments.test.ts` the Git child of the fixture seeding step was SIGKILLed about 10 ms after it started (the FOR-68 pattern; that file does not exercise discovery). The file then passed 3/3 alone, and a second full `npm test` run passed **340/340**. The desktop crate's tests were not run. Simulated providers and local fixture harnesses only.

## FOR-61 — evidence journey without the TypeScript oracle, re-verified on main (2026-10-18)

## FOR-58 — Runtime and desktop suites in parallel (2026-10-18)

Code revision `58580c3` (branch `for-58-reliable-parallel-tests`, from main `aa2d619`), built in a private target directory in the worktree. All results are local tests with simulated providers; nothing contacted a live service. Other agents were running builds and tests on the same 16-core machine throughout, so each result records the 1/5/15-minute load average.

**Root causes.** Each flaky test was reproduced by running 8–16 copies of its test binary at once. That is heavier than a normal suite run, but it shows the same failures.

| Test(s) | Failure | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| `runner::codex::tests::a_lost_backend_reply_retains_unknown_effect_and_closes_admission`, `…terminal_before_steering_reply_does_not_acknowledge_rejected_or_unknown_input`, `runner::journal::tests::restart_reconciles_original_call_receipt_and_never_readmits_unknown_effect` | `Host journal already has a writer.` on reopen | The test drops its journal and reopens it in the same process. Every `fork` by another test thread copies all open descriptors, `O_CLOEXEC` included, and a `flock` belongs to the shared open file description. Until that child execs or exits, it keeps the dropped journal locked. | `crewroom_core::authority::try_lock_settled` retries for up to `INHERITED_LOCK_WINDOW` (1 s) before reporting a writer. `Journal::open` uses it. |
| `repository::git::lifecycle::tests::driven_ticket_retains_actual_controller_lease_after_caller_drops` (the runtime lease test) | final `Authority::acquire_for_execution(..).is_ok()` fails | Same mechanism, on the controller `lease.lock`/`dispatch.lock` | `Authority` locks use the same settle |
| Desktop workbench-ownership race | `Another controller owns this workbench.` after a service restart in a test | The desktop fixtures close and reopen the in-process service, which runs the same `Authority::acquire` after a drop | Same settle. This failure was not reproduced in this session: the desktop runs that failed were killed by an outside SIGKILL instead (below). The new core test reproduces the mechanism deterministically. |
| `model_discovery::cancellation_unavailable_version_drift_and_cursor_loops_fail_closed` (also found) | `Discovery process ownership needs inspection.` instead of `cancelled` | Cancellation can kill the `crewroom-process` helper before it writes `owner.json`. `stop_owned` cannot read an owner and reports `uncertain`. This is a product bug: a cancelled refresh reported a cleanup problem. | `inspect::stopped_before_owner`: the helper writes `owner.json` durably before exec, so a reaped helper with no receipt, a free lease and an empty process group launched nothing, and `close()` reports it stopped. Every other case still goes through `stop_owned`. |
| same test, cursor-loop check (found under 16× stress) | discovery timed out before the repeated cursor arrived | The 3 s bound covered two Python starts on a loaded machine | The bound is now 20 s; it only guards a hung fixture. The assertion still requires the cursor error and prints the actual error. |
| `runner::auth::tests::refresh_child_retains_source_lease_after_parent_death` (also found) | `ParseIntError { kind: Empty }` | The test polled for the PID file's existence, and Python's `open('w')` creates the file before writing the PID | The fixture writes `ready.tmp` and renames it |

The settle changes one product behaviour: a genuine second journal writer or second controller is now refused after 1 s instead of at once. It is still refused. New tests: `authority::tests::a_released_lease_still_held_by_an_inherited_copy_is_acquired_once_it_closes` (core) holds the lease through a child that inherited it and checks that a plain `try_lock` is refused. It fails without the settle and passes with it. `inspect::tests::a_helper_reaped_before_its_owner_receipt_launched_nothing` and `…an_owner_receipt_a_held_lease_or_a_live_member_needs_inspection` (runtime) cover the new stop check. No assertion was removed or relaxed, and no test was skipped. The other changes are the fixture rename and the longer guard on the hung-fixture bound.

**Stress before and after** (same binaries, concurrent copies × rounds):

| Binary | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `crewroom_runtime` unit tests, 8 × 5 / 8 × 6 | 7 of 40 failed (journal reopen 4, controller lease 2, auth PID file 1) | 0 of 48 (load 27) |
| `model_discovery` `cancellation…`, 12 × 5 then 16 × 15 | 34 of 60 failed (`ownership needs inspection`) | 0 of 120 at 12 ×; 1 of 240 at 16 ×, the cursor-check timeout fixed above |

**Clippy.** `cargo clippy -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` is clean at `58580c3`. The desktop `useless_conversion` in `feedback_tests.rs` named in the ticket had already been fixed on main in `1eae08e` (FOR-45). `cargo fmt --check` is clean for all four crates.

**Repeated suite runs at `58580c3`** (`cargo test --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-desktop -j6`, default test threads):

| Run | Core + service + runtime | Desktop | Load |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 380 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored | test binary SIGKILLed after ~60 s, no test failure reported | 6.3 / 10.4 / 11.4 |
| 2 | 380 / 0 / 2 | 146 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored | 2.8 / 8.4 / 10.6 |
| 3 | 380 / 0 / 2 | 146 / 0 / 15 | 4.2 / 8.2 / 10.2 |
| 4 | 380 / 0 / 2 | 146 / 0 / 15 | 3.7 / 7.9 / 9.8 |
| 5 | 380 / 0 / 2 | 146 / 0 / 15 | 6.1 / 10.1 / 10.6 |
| 6 | 380 / 0 / 2 | test binary SIGKILLed, no test failure reported | 7.2 / 10.0 / 10.6 |

Core, service and runtime passed 6 consecutive times, as they did in 5 earlier runs of the same code before the core test was added. The desktop suite passed 4 consecutive times (runs 2–5) with every test passing, but the 5-consecutive criterion is **not met** for desktop, because of the kill below.

**Desktop SIGKILL (open; found in this work, not caused by it).** The `crewroom-desktop` test binary sometimes dies from SIGKILL partway through a full run. No test has failed at that point, and the death came 2 s to 100 s after start. It happened in 2 of 6 runs at `58580c3` and in 2 of 5 runs before the core test was added. It also happened in all 5 full runs made while the load was about 30, and 2 of those were built from main `aa2d619` sources. What it is not:
- Memory: `oom_kill` is 0 in every cgroup up to `user.slice` and in `/proc/vmstat`, and peak RSS of a full run is 0.54 GB.
- A kill by process name: a `sleep` copy named `crewroom-5ec9a2ddeb755075` ran for 40 minutes untouched, and no `pkill`/`killall`/`kill -9` command line appeared during the runs.
- A kill from the test's own process tree: run as PID 1 of a private PID namespace (`unshare -Upf --mount-proc`), where init ignores SIGKILL sent from inside, the binary was still killed after 58 s.

So the signal comes from outside the test's process tree, or from the kernel. An `LD_PRELOAD` `kill`/`killpg` logger and a gdb syscall trace saw no self-kill. The gdb run is not a valid suite result, because 7 tests timed out under it. Every desktop module passes when run alone. The in-flight tests at one 2 s kill were the first 16 started (mostly `acceptance_tests`). The same machine-level kill would explain the FOR-52 record of `git` subprocesses SIGKILLed under load. It would also explain this session's first `npm test` run: 328/329, where the `tests/feedback-continuation.test.ts` process ended with no failing subtest. That file passed alone, and the full rerun passed 334/334. Finding the sender needs signal tracing with privileges (`perf`/bpftrace `signal:signal_generate`), which was not used on the operator's machine without approval. Follow-up: Linear FOR-68.

**Other checks at `58580c3`.** `npm test` **334/334** on rerun; the first run is described above. `npm run build` passes. `npm run test:e2e` is left to the integrator.

**Integration (2026-10-18).** Rebased without code changes onto main `ed05621` (FOR-57) as code `8f39564`; only this file's and `docs/status.md`'s entries conflicted, and every other ticket's entries were kept. Checks at `8f39564`, in a worktree prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py` and a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (load 0.8 / 4.0 / 7.8 at the start):

- `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop -j6` at default test threads: **544 passed, 0 failed, 17 ignored** (one run; the desktop binary was not killed in it). This is one more run, not the 5-run desktop evidence the acceptance criterion asks for.

- `npm test` **340/340**; `npm run build` passes (existing Vite chunk-size warning); `npm run test:e2e` **31 passed** with the fixture-only OMP and Rust Mail runtime linked by the preparation step.

## FOR-57 — Docker-backed verification finds the Docker socket on Linux (2026-10-18)

Branch `for-57-docker-socket-resolver` from main `aa2d619`. Code `6c754fa` (resolver and consumers), `46c4765` (resolver bound into feature-recipe source hashes). Checks below ran on `6c754fa` on Linux x86_64, Docker Engine 29.7.2 (rootful, `default` context only), Node 26. `46c4765` changes only `scripts/verify-feature.py`; its guard tests passed again there.

- **Resolver.** `scripts/rust/docker-engine.ts` resolves the socket from `CREWROOM_DOCKER_SOCKET`, then a `unix://` `DOCKER_HOST`, then the active Docker CLI context, then the platform default (`/var/run/docker.sock` on Linux, `~/.docker/run/docker.sock` on macOS). The active context is `DOCKER_CONTEXT`, else `currentContext` in `DOCKER_CONFIG`/`config.json`. Its endpoint is read from the CLI's own `contexts/meta/<sha256(name)>/meta.json`, and `docker` is never run. Contexts and `DOCKER_HOST` values that are not `unix://`, and unreadable context metadata, are refused with a remedy. `requireDockerEngine()` sends `GET /_ping` to the resolved socket. If the engine does not reply `OK`, it fails with "Docker engine is not reachable at <socket> (from <source>): <reason>", followed by the remedy (start the engine, or set `CREWROOM_DOCKER_SOCKET`, a `unix://` `DOCKER_HOST` or `docker context use`), plus a group hint on `EACCES`. `scripts/docker_engine.py` mirrors it for Python scripts (`--require` exits 2 with the same message).
- **Consumers.** Nothing outside the two resolvers hard-codes a Docker socket any more. Moved: the native container, host, evidence, owner, repository, workflow, supervisor, Jira and comparison fixtures under `scripts/rust`; `tests/{checks,container,cleanup}.integration.ts`; and the Python installation, application-runtime/interruption and native-scenario drivers. `repository-fixture` no longer guesses `$HOME/.docker/run/docker.sock`; `repository-adapter.ts` passes it the resolved socket. In `verify-feature.py`, preflight pings the engine, reports an unreachable one as `unavailable`, records `dockerSocket` and passes it to the journey and to fallback container cleanup. The only remaining `docker.sock` literal is the evidence journey's deliberately unavailable socket.
- **Images.** The journeys named in the ticket took the historical arm64 image ID `sha256:9d2e5553…`, which cannot run on Linux. They now take `checkImage()` (pinned busybox index) or the new `pythonCheckImage()`: `python@sha256:2d9aefe2fef018a7eb2c13064c89c71929800fd2e5dccdbf52ea5da5bb8d929a` (python:3.13-alpine index), used for checks that start `/usr/local/bin/python3`. Both keep the historical image on macOS and honour `CREWROOM_CHECK_IMAGE` / `CREWROOM_PYTHON_CHECK_IMAGE`.
- **Unit checks (simulated engine).** `tests/docker-engine.test.ts` **5/5**. It covers the resolution order, including a context from `DOCKER_CONTEXT`, from `config.json` and from `DOCKER_CONFIG`, with `default` falling through. It covers the refusals, TypeScript/Python parity over the same environments, and the unreachable message for a missing socket and for a loopback Unix socket that answers 500 (both languages). A loopback socket answering `OK` passes.
- **Live Docker journeys on `6c754fa`** (real engine; `crewroom-host`, `crewroom-process`, `container-fixture` and `repository-fixture` built from this revision in a target directory private to the worktree):
  - `scripts/rust/container-extra.test.ts` (container-backed check): **3/3**. It passed **3/3** again through a temporary Docker context created with `docker --config <tmp> context create … --docker host=unix:///var/run/docker.sock`, resolved as source `docker context`.
  - `scripts/rust/host-container.test.ts`: **4/4**.
  - `scripts/rust/evidence.test.ts`: **11/15**. All 4 failures stop with "Workflow database is newer than this application.", raised when the retained TypeScript `Workbench` (schema ≤ 26) is opened as a parity oracle on the native-migrated store. That is the same drift FOR-41 found in the publication journeys; follow-up **FOR-61**. *Correction (review):* an earlier version of this line said every Docker-executing case passed. Only the 11 passing cases, which include SIGKILL receipt recovery, interrupted receipts and unavailable-engine cleanup, were verified. Three of the 4 failing cases are Docker-backed, and their Docker assertions were not verified on Linux at `6c754fa`. "Gate the exact candidate and preserve removed receipts" failed in its first assessment, before any check container started, so its exact-candidate gate, removed `container.json` receipt and stopped `ContainerCommand.inspect` were not exercised. "Failed, timed out and source-tampering commands" and "explicit check environment corrections" ran Docker checks but failed before their assertions completed. The pinned-research case does not use Docker. All 15 pass at `a9ca1b4`; see the review fix below.
  - `tests/cleanup.integration.ts` **2/2**; `scripts/rust/container-owner.test.ts` **1/1** (with `CREWROOM_CHECK_IMAGE` set to the local busybox ID).
  - `tests/container.integration.ts` **3/4** and `tests/checks.integration.ts` **0/1**. These retained TypeScript paths run root with `CapDrop: ['ALL']`, and on Linux that root cannot write an operator-owned 0700 workspace (reproduced directly with `docker run --cap-drop ALL`). Follow-up **FOR-62**, which also covers the fixtures that still pin the macOS image.
  - Unreachable engine: `CREWROOM_DOCKER_SOCKET=<absent path>` makes `container-extra` fail at load with the message above, naming the absent path. No check container was left behind (`docker ps -a --filter label=io.crewroom.command` empty).
- **Other checks.** `npm run build` passes (existing Vite chunk warning; a first attempt was killed by the loaded machine and is not counted). `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` and `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-runtime --example repository-fixture -- -D warnings` are clean. `python3 -m unittest test_verify_feature` **8/8**. The TypeScript `Workbench` cannot open a store past schema 26, and the native store's later migrations rewrite data it would read (schema 31 rewrites ticket sources), so a pre-migration or forced-version copy would not hold the native state under test. `expectAssessment` now compares the whole native assessment (`ready`, the exact saved candidate, the exact ordered blockers in the retained `server/evidence.ts` wording, `confirmedInvalidation`) instead of a TypeScript re-assessment. With the oracle gone, that case reached an assertion it had never run: a citation of an absent path was rejected as "Pinned source must be a regular text file.", because `git ls-tree` prints no entry for an absent path. `crewroom-runtime` `read_research` now reports it as "Pinned source path … does not exist at <commit>.", as the retained contract does. The case also asserts `read_research` directly for a present and an absent path. Checks at `a9ca1b4` (Linux x86_64, Docker Engine 29.7.2 rootful, binaries built from this revision in a target directory private to the worktree): `scripts/rust/evidence.test.ts` **15/15** (twice: once on the identical uncommitted tree and once on the commit); `container-extra` and `host-container` **7/7**; no check container left behind (`docker ps -a --filter label=io.crewroom.command` empty). `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime` **185 passed, 2 ignored**; `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` clean; strict `tsc` over `scripts/rust/evidence.test.ts` clean; `npm test` **337/337** (load average about 11); `npm run build` passes.
- **Boundary.** Docker and Git are real. The rootless Docker context was checked only through resolver unit tests and a temporary context pointing at the rootful socket; this machine has no rootless engine. macOS was not re-run. `npm run test:e2e` is left to the integrator.
- **Integration (rebased head `f9bb6da`, on main `72521e0`).** The five FOR-57 commits rebased onto main (resolver `6c754fa` → `6e7c8c6`, recipe hashes `46c4765` → `9a6e4fd`, evidence `7fd510c` → `b1decad`, review fix `a9ca1b4` → `c944b9e`, review-fix evidence `d906c87` → `f9bb6da`). One code conflict: FOR-53 changed the returned object in `scripts/rust/jira-workflow-fixture.ts` beside the socket the resolver replaced; main's version was kept with its socket taken from `dockerSocket()`. Only the `docs/status.md` and this file's entries conflicted otherwise, and every ticket's entries were kept. No file outside the two resolvers names a Docker socket after the rebase. Checks ran at `f9bb6da` in the review-fix worktree, prepared with `python3 scripts/prepare_worktree.py`, and with a target directory private to the worktree, on Linux x86_64, Docker Engine 29.7.2 rootful: `npm test` **340/340** (includes `tests/docker-engine.test.ts`); `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **31/31**; `scripts/rust/evidence.test.ts`, `container-extra.test.ts` and `host-container.test.ts` **22/22** (15 + 3 + 4) against the real engine, with no check container left behind; `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-runtime -- --test-threads=4` **189 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**; `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime -- --check` clean. ## FOR-55 — GitHub PR feedback includes review summaries (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `730a780e69e42d3dd966e42407c4fe51cd162573` on main (rebased without code changes from `a9a8c3f`, which was built on main `aa2d619`, onto main `0b7f398`). `GitHubPullClient::comments` now reads `/pulls/{n}/reviews` (every page) beside conversation and inline comments. Approved, changes-requested, dismissed and summarised comment-only reviews become feedback items with `kind: "review"`, `review.state`, `review.commit` (the revision it was submitted at), author and submission time. A comment-only review with no summary is the container of its inline comments and is not a separate item; pending drafts are not feedback. The store matches feedback by kind and id because reviews have their own GitHub id space. GitHub records no edit time for a review, so a dismissal or edited summary replaces the retained version instead of reading as a conflict (versioned comments keep the conflict rule). `observe` adds each reviewer's standing decision (latest approve / changes-requested / dismiss per reviewer; comment-only reviews change nothing; dismissed decisions dropped). `publication::assess` holds readiness on a standing changes request and names the revision it was submitted at ("at this revision …" or "at earlier revision …"); a GitHub observation without decisions is reported as unobserved, not as approval; a human decision does not latch `reviewInvalidated`. The desktop feedback list labels review items "Review #id" with their decision and revision.

Simulated evidence (loopback GitHub REST fixture and SQLite store, not github.com):
- `crewroom-runtime/tests/github_publication.rs` `github_feedback_includes_submitted_reviews_with_and_without_inline_comments`: a changes request with only a summary (no inline comments, id equal to a conversation comment id), a summarised comment-only review, an approval with an empty body, 100 summary-less comment-only reviews (one of them carrying an inline comment) and a pending draft over two review pages. Exactly the three decisions/summaries appear, attributed and timestamped; both review pages are read; a malformed review on the first page leaves the read `partial` with what was read.
- `github_pull_requests_publish_continue_and_report_readiness_at_a_named_revision` (extended): a standing changes request at the head blocks readiness with "Reviewer reviewer-fixture requested changes on GitHub at this revision 333333333333…" and `reviewInvalidated: false`; the same review at an earlier commit is named "at earlier revision"; a later approval by that reviewer or a dismissal releases readiness; an observation without `reviews` is blocked as unobserved; an unexpected review state fails the observation.
- `crewroom-core/tests/provider_records.rs` `github_reviews_are_feedback_with_their_own_identity_and_reobserve_without_duplicates`: a review and a comment sharing id 1 are kept apart; re-observation adds nothing; a review dismissal and edited summary replace the retained version while a same-time comment edit stays a conflict; a review missing from a later read keeps its history with unknown currentness.

Live evidence (github.com, operator-approved private sandbox `<owner>/crewroom-e2e-sandbox`, repository `github:1424057732`; production `GitHubPullClient` through `crates/crewroom-runtime/examples/github-publication-live.rs reviews`, binary sha256 `52469a23…1166`, built from the implementation sources before a formatting-only `cargo fmt` of the example; the operator's `gh` token was passed in the environment and never printed). Synthetic PR #111, head `c2da583`, base `c94f06f`, with three synthetic reviews submitted at the head through the REST API: a summary-only comment review, an inline-only comment review with an empty summary, and a comment review with a summary and one inline comment. Observed at 2026-10-18T14:51:07Z: feedback `complete`; the summary-only review and the summarised review appeared as `kind: review`, `COMMENTED`, commit `c2da583…`, attributed and timestamped; the inline-only review appeared only as its inline comment, and both inline comments appeared; one further attributed conversation comment on the PR (not posted by the probe; content not recorded) also appeared. A second read returned the identical item set. Standing decisions: none. Readiness: blocked only by "The required PR pipeline has not validated this head and base." (the sandbox has no CI). GitHub refused both `REQUEST_CHANGES` and `APPROVE` on the probe's own PR ("Can not request changes on your own pull request", "Can not approve your own pull request"), so a live changes-requested decision and its readiness hold were **not** observed; they are covered only by the simulated checks above. The PR was closed and its branch deleted after the read.

Checks on `a9a8c3f`: `cargo test -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -- --test-threads=4` all suites pass (`github_publication` **4/4**); `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=4` all suites pass (desktop **146 passed / 15 ignored**); `cargo clippy -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-desktop -p crewroom-service --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean for the touched crates; `npm run build` passes; `npm test -- --test-concurrency=1` **334/334**. On the loaded shared machine (load average 15–25), two default-concurrency runs and one `--test-concurrency=2` run each failed different TypeScript files that pass alone; those failures are recorded as follow-up FOR-63. `npm run test:e2e` was not run by the implementer (the integrator runs it on port 4331). This ticket changes no TypeScript; the legacy TypeScript workbench has no GitHub host.

Integration checks on `730a780` plus its evidence commit (rebased onto main `0b7f398`, disposable worktree): `npm test -- --test-concurrency=1` **334/334**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **31/31** (one earlier run timed out in `feedback.spec.ts` while Rust tests ran in parallel on the loaded machine, then passed in a full rerun); `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean for core, runtime and desktop; `cargo test -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-desktop -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=4` all suites pass (`github_publication` **4/4**, desktop **146 passed / 15 ignored**; one `model_discovery` test failed once under load and passed in two reruns).

Not executed: a live changes-requested or approving review by a second account, and therefore a live readiness hold on one; a full Crewroom run observing live review feedback through the daemon and Resume.

## FOR-53 — Per-project Jira refresh (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `2e23990450460471cfffe322afbb583a91ecc4f3` on branch `for-53-project-jira-refresh` from main `aa2d619`. A project's confirmed Jira site (FOR-47) drives its own Jira refresh with its own filters; schema 35 (`jira_project_sync`) moves the former workspace-level setting into the projects that confirmed its site. Receipt: [for-53-project-jira-refresh-2026-10-18.json](for-53-project-jira-refresh-2026-10-18.json).

**Simulated (loopback Jira fixtures and store tests, not a live Jira site):**
- Core (`crates/crewroom-core/tests/jira_projects.rs`, unit tests in `src/jira_projects.rs`):
  - A confirmed site starts with no filters; saving requires a project key, saved filter or JQL; a `siteUrl` in the filters and JQL with `ORDER BY` are refused. Filters combine into `filter = … AND project = "…" AND assignee = "…" AND (…) ORDER BY Rank ASC`.
  - Two projects on one site keep separate filters; saving one leaves the other unchanged. A commit for another project's source is refused.
  - A refresh keeps Jira rank order, targets first-imported tickets at the project, stores Jira status as source status only and queues nothing. A failure keeps the last complete refresh and task order; a stale failure is dropped.
  - Confirming another Jira site starts over with no filters and refuses the old source's commit; re-confirming the same site keeps filters; confirming GitHub Issues removes the record; imported tickets keep source, site and status.
  - Schema 35 copies a schema-34 workspace setting (saved filter, assignee, last refresh) into every project confirming its site, each its own copy; a project on another site and a GitHub project get none; re-offering the same setting changes nothing.
  - An unused setting is reported, never adopted by another site, and adopted by the next project confirming its site; a later project starts empty.
- Runtime (`src/jira_sync.rs` unit test): 401/403 classify as rejected, other HTTP as unavailable, no answer as unreachable, missing credentials as not connected, invalid responses as invalid; messages carry no provider body.
- Service (`crates/crewroom-service/tests/jira_refresh.rs`, updated `credentials.rs`):
  - Three projects (two on the connected site, one on another site). Refresh before filters returns 409 and sends nothing; JQL with `ORDER BY` returns 400.
  - `POST /api/jira/sync` refreshes the two connected-site projects, each with its own JQL (`project = "CR" AND assignee = "operator-1" ORDER BY Rank ASC`, `(labels = beta) ORDER BY Rank ASC`); the other-site project is never queried and shows not connected naming the connected site.
  - 401 shows rejected and 503 unavailable for that project only, keeping its tickets and last complete refresh. An origin with nothing listening shows unreachable; a service with no Jira connection shows not connected and `/api/jira/sync` returns 409.
  - Confirming GitHub Issues for one project stops its Jira queries; its tickets keep source and site. No response or workspace carries the token or provider error body.
  - `credentials.rs`: the jira.json saved filter is adopted by the project that confirms its site; refresh without a token reports not connected before any request; stored-token refresh, whole-state restore and unreadable-key handling behave as before per project.
- Desktop (`jira_refresh_tests.rs`; ignored journey `native_journey_jira_refresh_outage_retains_operator_brief_and_never_queues` updated and run with `--ignored`): in Settings → Repositories the operator confirms the offered connected site, sees the no-filters state and another project's not-connected note, saves a project key, refreshes and sees "2 tickets matched the last complete refresh"; Settings → Integrations names the project; tickets target the project, keep rank order and show their Jira status; no run is queued.
- Native scripts (`scripts/rust/jira-sync.test.ts`, updated for per-project refresh through the `repository-fixture` `jira_project` op): 4/4 — paginated order and reopen, retained active issues, shared driven refresh with cancelled waiters and close drain, rollback on a failed final transaction. `scripts/rust/jira-client.test.ts` and `provider-config.test.ts`: 10/10 with the optional workspace filter.

## FOR-56 — Neutral source identifiers and native provider oracle (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `2832986234025ffcb90b120583ee893c4430ad85` (branch `for-56-neutral-source-identifiers`, from main `aa2d619`). Rust builds used a worktree-private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. All evidence is simulated: synthetic fixtures and a loopback service, with no Jira or GitHub calls. The machine was shared and heavily loaded during these runs (load average 15–30).

- `npx tsx scripts/rust/verify-providers.ts` against this revision's `ledger-oracle` example: **passed**, 92 operations and 25 compared tables. Before this change, it failed at seed on the 12 native-only tables. A first neutral-only check also failed at seed, because the copied schema-26 store still had `jiraStatus` before the native process migrated it. That check now runs on every post-seed comparison and on every native result, and found no retired field. 597 neutral source-field reads were checked. The native `getTask` result reports `sourceStatus` "In Progress", `sourceStatusId` "3", `source` "jira", `sourceId` "1", `sourceSite` = the fixture site and key `FIX-1`. It has no `jiraStatus`. Native-only tables were checked empty after every step: `choreography_revisions`, `comparison_*`, `github_project_sync`, `github_sync`, `github_updates`, `implementation_plans`, `intake_requests`, `plan_decisions` and `plan_sources`. The native-only `acceptedSource` field was observed 38 times; it is reported, not compared.

- `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop -j6 -- --test-threads=4`: **147 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored**. New: `native_github_ticket_uses_neutral_identifiers_and_names_github_in_its_change_labels`. A GitHub issue is accepted, re-synced with a new title and opened. It checks: `row-source-{id}` "GitHub open"; `fact-source` "open"; no `row-jira-*`, `fact-jira` or `fact-cell-jira`; "GitHub requirements changed.", "Changed in GitHub: title.", previous/current title, "GitHub source revision 1 → 2" and "save the brief to accept the changed GitHub requirements."; no other visible text naming Jira except the integration status bar. Existing Jira, local and GitHub row/fact tests now read `row-source-*` / `fact-source`. Unit tests in `blockers.rs` cover the Jira and GitHub blocker wording.
- `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -j6 -- --test-threads=4`: **376 passed, 1 failed, 2 ignored**. The one failure is `model_discovery::cancellation_unavailable_version_drift_and_cursor_loops_fail_closed`, a 100 ms cancellation timing check unrelated to this change. In isolated reruns under the same load it failed 2 of 3 and passed 1. Filed as [FOR-65](https://linear.app/forgeinnovations/issue/FOR-65).

## FOR-52 — OMP 18.11.3 execution (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `7466e0c1d6959fdee298f5445192271b071aa92b` (branch `for-52-omp-18-11-3-launch`, from main `3a2add0`). Every result below was produced by binaries built from that revision in a private target directory; their hashes are in the [receipt](for-52-omp-18-11-3-2026-10-18.json). The shared target was not used for evidence: a parallel worktree with older sources rebuilt it during this work.

**Change.** `runner::execution_versions("omp")` lists `omp/18.4.12` and `omp/18.11.3`. Wire check: 18.11.3 `rpc-frame.ts` keeps the chunk and `rpc_frame_error` frames; the only encoder change serializes a shared `message_update` snapshot once and is byte-identical to `JSON.stringify`. `host-tools.ts` is unchanged, and RPC types are additive (new commands and frames Crewroom does not send or need). The offline fixture accepts either fixture-only version. The host suite shares the assignment budget root across restore attempts, as `supervisor/workflow/configuration.rs` does. The live picker test resolves an offering key without a billing route to its single FOR-42 route. Tests: `launch_verified_omp_18_11_3_offerings_are_eligible` (runtime), the execution-note test for an unlisted OMP version, and the GPUI picker test asserting 18.11.3 carries no execution caveat.

**Review fix (admission withdrawn).** Review found that `7466e0c` admitted 18.11.3 to the execution versions without the live launch FOR-52 requires, and relabelled the admission bar from "passed live acceptance" to acceptance against the actual CLI. The review-fix commit on this branch restores `runner::execution_versions("omp")` to `omp/18.4.12` and the "passed live acceptance" wording. 18.11.3 offerings stay listed with no execution profile and keep the execution note. The tests now say what was verified: `contract_checked_omp_18_11_3_offerings_wait_for_live_launch` (runtime) and the GPUI picker test both assert that 18.11.3 keeps its execution caveat. The `omp/18.11.3` entry is added back only in the same commit as a passing live launch receipt. The synthetic and scripted results below were produced at `7466e0c`, when 18.11.3 was admitted. To rerun the scripted or live launch, the build must include that entry. Checks at the review fix: `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service` **373 passed, 2 ignored**; `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop` **146 passed, 15 ignored**; `cargo clippy -D warnings --all-targets` for runtime/service is clean, and desktop reports only the existing `useless_conversion` in untouched `feedback_tests.rs`; `cargo fmt --check` for runtime and desktop is clean. No OMP launch was rerun at the review fix.

**Simulated: actual OMP, synthetic provider replies** (npm package under bun in the offline guard, no network route):

| Suite | OMP 18.11.3 | OMP 18.4.12 (control) |
|---|---|---|
| `verify-omp-rpc.py` | 12/12 | 12/12 |
| `verify-omp-host.py` | 11/11 | 11/11 |
| `verify-omp-supervisor.py` | 4/4 | 4/4 |
| `verify-omp-gateway.py` | 4/4 | 4/4 |

All owned processes exited, no provider errors, and the guard denied external and other-loopback connections. Earlier failed attempts are kept as findings. The first 18.11.3 host run failed its session-restore turn: the gateway refused the request with `input_budget`/`unknown_reasoning`, because 18.11.3 replays the three opaque reasoning items (18.4.12 replays none) and that run gave the restore host a fresh budget root. The first supervisor runs of both versions stopped at startup because the output path was too long for the tmux socket.

**Scripted production launch: installed binary, no live inference.** `scripts/verify_omp_scripted_workflow.py` ran the installed `omp/18.11.3` executable (sha256 `01fbb5f0…2d0a`) through `verify_catalog_workflow.py`. The upstream provider was a loopback fixture that answers each operator instruction with the named tool or a short reply. Result **7/7**: native keyboard picker selection, refresh/budget changes leaving frozen assignments equal, separately owned host/runner pairs, an addressed `send_message` delivered through real Rust Mail, SIGKILL reattachment without another Mail effect, pause/resume with new processes on the same sessions, and a specialist artifact plus coordinator completion that survives a controller restart. Run `completed`, all attempts `stopped`, 9 scripted provider requests from Coordinator and Specialist1. The first attempt stopped in the native picker: `verify-native-workflow.ts` passes an offering key without the FOR-42 billing-route suffix, so the picker found no matching choice.

**Live: one launch at `2c5d05359f5855e0fd7dfa675294decab9663ce9` (2026-10-18), passed.** The operator approved one live OMP 18.11.3 workflow launch using the existing OMP login. Commit `2c5d053` (branch `for-52-live-launch`, from main `aa2d619`) adds `omp/18.11.3` back to `runner::execution_versions("omp")`. The binaries for the launch were built from that tree in a private worktree target directory. `scripts/verify_catalog_workflow.py` then ran the installed `omp/18.11.3` (sha256 `01fbb5f0…2d0a`) against a real model service. Provider `zai`, model `glm-4.7`, API `anthropic-messages`, low effort; the gateway sent thinking enabled with a 2048-token budget. The API-key credential came from OMP's login store. It was copied only into a private 0600 runtime configuration outside the repository, and every runtime file that held it was deleted with the run directory. The data directory and runtime root were new and isolated, and the daemon bound an ephemeral loopback port, not 4318. The operator's `crewroomd` on 4318 was not stopped or contacted. Result **7/7**, exit 0:
- native keyboard picker selected `offering:omp:zai:glm-4.7:unknown` (access configured, execution profile `omp-18.4.12-host-tools-v2`, no execution note)
- refresh and budget changes left both frozen assignments equal
- separately owned host/runner pairs
- an addressed `send_message` delivered through real Rust Mail (`received`, once)
- SIGKILL reattachment without another Mail effect
- pause/resume with new processes on the same sessions
- a specialist artifact, then coordinator completion that survived a controller restart

The run ended `completed` with all four attempts `stopped`. The sessions hold 9 assistant turns from `zai`/`glm-4.7` (1521 output tokens). A single 8-token direct API request checked the credential beforehand; it is not a workflow launch and is not counted. Receipt: the `live` block of the [receipt](for-52-omp-18-11-3-2026-10-18.json). Non-secret raw receipts are kept in ignored local state. This closes FOR-52 AC2 and AC3 and the OMP part of FOR-38's "one real launch per harness". Limitation: one provider and API (`anthropic-messages`) ran live; other OMP providers and APIs on 18.11.3 are covered only by the synthetic suites.

**Checks at `7466e0c`.** `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service` (private target) **373 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**; `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop` **146 passed, 15 ignored** (with `--test-threads=2`; an earlier run failed with `EDQUOT` because `/tmp` was out of user quota); `cargo clippy -D warnings` for runtime/service (all targets) and desktop (bin) clean (desktop `--all-targets` still reports only the existing `useless_conversion` in untouched `feedback_tests.rs`); `cargo fmt --check` for runtime and desktop clean; `npm test` **332/332** (a first full run had 2 failures where `git` subprocesses were SIGKILLed under load; both files passed on rerun, then the full suite passed); `npm run build` passes. `npm run test:e2e` is left to the integrator.

**Integration onto main `fd059ae` (2026-10-18).** Rebased as `e8a82dd` (pre-rebase `7466e0c`), evidence `dffa678` (pre-rebase `f43a251`) and review fix `267587c` (pre-rebase `55c2ad0`); the only conflict was the `docs/status.md` position list, resolved by keeping every ticket's entry. At `267587c`: `npm test` **334/334**, `npm run build` passes, `npm run test:e2e` **31/31** (the ignored `.crewroom/mh2-omp-fixture` and `.crewroom/experiments` Rust Mail runtime linked into the worktree; a first run without the Rust Mail link failed INT-03 and INT-07/EVD-08 in fixture setup), `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime` **185 passed, 2 ignored**, `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop` **146 passed, 15 ignored**, `cargo clippy -D warnings` runtime (all targets) and desktop (bin) clean (desktop `--all-targets` still reports only the existing `useless_conversion` in untouched `feedback_tests.rs`), `cargo fmt --check` runtime and desktop clean. ## FOR-45 — Per-project GitHub Issues source (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `6a0a65df0bbb61871ff4dffc08172044d2ee30e0` on branch `for-45-per-project-github-source` from main `3a2add0`. This closes the remaining FOR-45 gaps. Each project now has its own GitHub Issues source with label, assignee, milestone and state filters, and the project's FOR-47 confirmed ticket source drives its sync. Schema 34 (`github_project_sync`) moves the former workspace-level source into the projects that confirmed its repository. Receipt: [for-45-per-project-source-2026-10-18.json](for-45-per-project-source-2026-10-18.json).

**Simulated (loopback fixtures, not github.com):**
- Core (`crates/crewroom-core/tests/github_issues.rs`, `github_delivery.rs`, `project_source.rs`):
  - A project needs a confirmed GitHub source before it can save filters or labels, or sync.
  - Confirming GitHub Issues starts the project with open issues only. A `repository` key in the filters is refused.
  - Each project keeps its own filters and sync result. Re-confirming the same repository keeps them. Confirming Jira removes the project's sync. Confirming another repository starts the sync over, and a sync of the replaced source is refused.
  - A ticket first imported by a project's sync targets that project.
  - Schema 34 adopts a schema-33 workspace source into the matching project (filters, milestone, workflow labels, last sync) and removes the old record. A workspace source that no project uses is kept until a project confirms its repository.
- Service (`crates/crewroom-service/tests/github_issues.rs`, `github_delivery.rs`):
  - The `/api/repositories/{id}/github-issues/{filters|labels|sync}` routes return 409 before confirmation and 400 for a bad filter.
  - The paged import, pull-request exclusion, re-acceptance, rate limit, revoked token, close and reopen re-read, gone issues and secondary rate limit all behave as before.
  - The no-access and issues-disabled cases run in their own projects and leave the first project's last sync intact.
  - The workspace lists each project's status by id and name, and reports a workspace-level source that no project has adopted.
- Runtime (`crates/crewroom-runtime/tests/github_delivery.rs`): issue delivery takes its source and labels from the project that confirmed the ticket's repository.
- Desktop (`github_issues_tests.rs`, `acceptance_tests.rs`):
  - In Settings → Repositories, the operator confirms the suggested source, saves filters and workflow labels, sees the missing connection stated, and syncs after connecting.
  - Settings → Integrations names the project beside its status.
  - Tickets show "GitHub open" or "GitHub closed" and target the project.

**Live (github.com, at `6a0a65d`):** debug `crewroomd` (sha256 `aa9840e8…c29b`) ran on loopback port 4391 with an isolated data directory, deleted afterwards. `CREWROOM_GITHUB_TOKEN` came from the operator's gh login, a gh OAuth token rather than a fine-grained or classic PAT, and was never printed. It was absent from every response and from the daemon log. The target was the approved private sandbox `<owner>/crewroom-e2e-sandbox`. The checkout's origin pointed at the sandbox and was never fetched.

The run used 101 synthetic issues, #10 to #110, labelled `for-45-pages`. They were created for this run and closed as not planned afterwards.
- Before confirmation, sync and filters were refused with 409. Confirming GitHub Issues gave the project filters `state open`. A `repository` key in the filters was refused with 400.
- With filters `labels for-45-pages · state open`, the sync imported **101 issues across two pages of 100**. All were "GitHub open" and all targeted the project. No pull request was imported and no run was created.
- #10 was then closed on github.com. In the next sync, GitHub's open listing still returned #10, already marked closed, so the ticket recorded "closed" and 101 issues matched. Changing the filters to `state all` kept the same 101 tickets, with only #10 closed. Back on `state open`, the listing returned 100 issues. #10 was not listed and was not re-read: saving the filters starts a fresh sync record with no previous tickets and the project had no runs, so the ticket kept the closed status observed earlier (source update time unchanged). The live closed state came from the lagging open listing's payload. The re-read of a ticket that leaves the filter under unchanged filters is covered by simulated tests only.
- A sync without an Origin header was refused with 403.
- After a restart with no token, the filters, the 101 tickets and the last sync time were all kept. The sync reported `not_connected`, and the summary still named the project.

**Checks at `6a0a65d`:**
- Rust tests: `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=4` gave **375 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**. The four `model_catalog` service tests first failed because a long `TMPDIR` overflowed the tmux socket path. They passed (5/5) when rerun with a short `TMPDIR`.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop -- --test-threads=4`: **146 passed / 15 ignored**. `/tmp` had reached the per-user quota on the shared machine, so for this run only the desktop fixture root (`test_support.rs`) was pointed at a home directory. The change was reverted and is not committed.
- `cargo clippy -D warnings` is clean for core, runtime and service (all targets) and for the desktop bin. Desktop `--all-targets` reports only the existing `feedback_tests.rs` `useless_conversion`.
- `cargo fmt --check` is clean.
- `npm test` gave **332/332**, and `npm run build` passes.
- Rust ran in a target directory private to the worktree. The shared target directory compiled tests against another worktree's stale `crewroom-core`, the same failure recorded at the FOR-47 integration.

**Review fixes (on `ab7680f`):**
- The in-progress label refusal now points to the project's GitHub Issues workflow labels under Settings → Repositories, not Settings → Integrations. `crewroom-runtime/tests/github_delivery.rs` asserts the new location.
- The desktop pending text follows the per-project routes: `…/github-issues/sync`, `…/filters` and `…/labels` show "Syncing GitHub issues…", "Saving GitHub issue filters…" and "Saving GitHub workflow labels…" again instead of "Saving…".
