## FOR-48 — Deliver progress back to GitHub issues (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `966b856d80dc2ef25817e6c1e43f3cd2d981b00a`, live probe and composition tests `6a8dae3527d485a30822a62a1560d83f1306ee4e`, documentation `5fe105e11f54ce49496928a0d52a2a01c345994e`, on branch `for-48-github-issue-delivery` from main `ba3ec4c` (FOR-45). `crewroom-runtime` `github_delivery/` is the GitHub counterpart of Jira delivery: the reporter's milestone, blocker, evidence, PR-link and research-outcome updates are saved, then posted as issue comments ending in `Crewroom update <id>`; every pass re-reads the issue's comments, so an earlier write is confirmed rather than repeated, and an unknown write stays uncertain until found or retried by the operator. After a merged publication Crewroom reads the issue and records GitHub's open/closed as the ticket's source status (`record_github_issue_state`); it never closes the issue and never changes workflow state. Optional in-progress/in-review labels apply only when named. Agents on GitHub-sourced tickets get `github_report`/`github_updates`; every other ticket keeps the Jira tools. Evidence and research composition moved to the shared `delivery_evidence` module and the verified PR link now accepts a GitHub PR. Schema 33 adds the `github_updates` outbox. The service adds `POST /api/github/issues/labels`, `POST /api/github/delivery/reconcile`, `POST /api/github/delivery/{id}/retry`, `githubUpdates` in the workspace and GitHub items under Needs you; the desktop adds the labels fields and a **GitHub issue delivery** section in Results with Retry. Machine-readable receipt: [for-48-github-issue-delivery-2026-10-18.json](for-48-github-issue-delivery-2026-10-18.json).

Simulated evidence (loopback GitHub API, not github.com):
- `crewroom-runtime/tests/github_delivery.rs`: exactly one comment across a 503 outage, a lost reply (502) and a store reopen with a new outbox; an unknown write stays uncertain over repeated passes without a second POST and later updates wait in order until the operator retries; not connected stays pending with its reason, a 403 blocks with the Issues write remedy and is retried after the fix; labels are refused and nothing is derived while off, then in-progress and in-review apply and are removed after the merge; the merge observation waits while the issue is open and, once the fixture closes it, records `closed` as source status with run status and desired state unchanged.
- Unit tests: comment labels and markers for every kind, issue keys, GitHub and Bitbucket PR links, provider-named refusals, issue-delivery remedies, tool policy (`github_report` only for the reporter or the research-coordinator fallback), and GitHub-versus-Jira delivery selection.
- `crewroom-core/tests/github_delivery.rs` (labels, observed issue state, receipts and notices, schema-32 upgrade), `crewroom-service/tests/github_delivery.rs` (label routes, outbox projection, reconcile and retry routes, no token in responses) and a Needs-you unit test; desktop tests for the labels setting and the Results section.

Review fix `5f15de6` (simulated only): `crewroom-runtime/tests/github_delivery.rs` now queues evidence, PR-link and research-outcome updates through `GitHubDelivery::prepare` against a ready Evidence/Research fixture (a real run worktree through `crewroom-process`; the required-check and passing-review receipts are recorded directly without a container runtime, and readiness is still assessed by `Evidence::assess`) and flushes them against the loopback API. Evidence and the PR link each post exactly one comment across a lost reply and a store reopen; the PR link names the GitHub PR and ends with the GitHub closing sentence, which the send-time recomputation reproduces exactly; a PR link whose PR is no longer open is blocked as no longer current with nothing posted. A research outcome cannot be queued before completion is requested; after the request the completion-gate predicate `needs_research_summary` holds the run until the outcome is queued; the outcome stays pending ("Waiting for confirmed research completion.") while the run is running and is posted once after completion, across a lost reply and a reopen. `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime` passes (github_delivery **7**), Clippy and formatting are clean, `npm test` **332/332** and `npm run build` pass. The supervisor transition loop itself is not driven; `npm run test:e2e` is still not run on this branch and acceptance criterion 6 remains open for the integrator.

Two comments were confirmed with GitHub comment ids; after reopening the store with one update replayed as uncertain and one as interrupted mid-send, both were confirmed by marker with the same ids and no third comment; an invalid token was blocked with the HTTP 401 remedy and a retry posted exactly one more comment; labels stayed off. A synthetic merged publication left the merge observation pending ("Waiting for GitHub to close #5") with source status open; after the operator closed #5 with `gh issue close`, a reconcile recorded GitHub closed as source status while the run stayed running.

## FOR-44 — Usage view separates API cost from subscription use (2026-10-18 UTC)

Implementation revision `45b76ce` on main, integrated onto FOR-47 main `67a2b67` (pre-rebase `33503db` from main `8967496`; the checks at `33503db` below name the pre-rebase revision). Receipt: [for-44-usage-by-billing-route-2026-10-18.json](for-44-usage-by-billing-route-2026-10-18.json).

**Change.**
- Service (`views/history.rs` `usage`): each attempt row carries `billing` from its frozen model selection (`selection.offering.billing`, else `selection.billingRoute`) and `billingRecorded`. A selection without either (admitted before FOR-42) reads `unknown` with `billingRecorded: false`. The single `total` is replaced by `routes`, one group per present route in the order API, subscription, unknown. Each group has its own requests, responses, tokens, state, started `attempts`, `assignments`, `tokenAttempts` and `unrecordedAttempts`. No figure is summed across routes. Subscription and unknown rows and groups never carry `costUsd`; a withheld harness estimate is flagged `estimateWithheld`. An API row without an estimate says it is unavailable, not zero. Native Codex/Claude rows keep request counts unavailable and describe their tokens as partial, not zero and not complete.
- Desktop: Results → Recorded usage reads "Usage by billing route · never totalled across routes" with sections "API · estimated cost", "Subscription · runs and tokens" and "Unknown billing route". API shows "Estimated cost (runner list price, not provider billing): $…". Subscription shows "Subscription use · no dollar figure is shown". Unknown shows "Billing route unknown · no cost estimate is shown", plus "Billing route not recorded…" for older assignments. Each attempt row names its route; attempt history uses the same route-aware numbers.
- Visibility only: no spending cap or enforcement; the Budget control still means reasoning effort.

**Simulated evidence (no live provider).** Service tests over a real SQLite store with synthetic Claude-native cumulative snapshots: `usage_groups_by_billing_route_without_cross_route_totals` (API, subscription and unknown assignments in one research run; API estimate only, subscription estimate withheld, request counts unavailable rather than zero, no top-level total); `historical_assignment_without_a_recorded_route_is_unknown` (a pre-route assignment is unknown and unrecorded; a selection-level `billingRoute` is honoured); the existing cumulative-replay test now asserts the API group. GPUI test `usage_is_grouped_by_billing_route_without_a_dollar_figure_for_subscription` renders all three groups and asserts that exactly two labels contain `$`, both the API estimate.

**Live evidence.** None. Real per-route usage needs agent inference, and this machine has subscription logins only (no API-billed login). The projection reads stored events, so the simulated store covers the same code path; the rendering of real native snapshots is unverified live.

**Checks at `33503db`.** `cargo test -p crewroom-service` pass (lib 7, all integration suites); `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop` **144 passed / 15 ignored**; `cargo clippy -p crewroom-service --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy -p crewroom-desktop -p crewroom-service -- -D warnings` clean (desktop `--all-targets` reports only the pre-existing `useless_conversion` in untouched `feedback_tests.rs`); `cargo fmt --check` for both crates clean; `npm test` **332/332** (one earlier full run failed `tests/repository-http.test.ts` under load; it passed alone and on the full rerun); `npm run build` passes. `npm run test:e2e` was not run; the integrator runs it.

**Integration onto FOR-47 main `67a2b67` (implementation `45b76ce`).** The rebase conflicted only in `docs/status.md` and this file; every earlier ticket entry is kept beside FOR-44. `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=1` **351 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**; `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-desktop -- --test-threads=4` **145 passed / 15 ignored**; `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-service --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-desktop -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` for both crates clean; `npm test` **332/332**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **28 passed, 3 failed**: `refinement.spec.ts:28` INT-03 and `research.spec.ts:27` INT-07/EVD-08 stop in fixture setup because the fixture-only OMP 18.4.12 is not installed, as at earlier integrations, and `history.spec.ts:109` RTE-09 failed while Rust tests loaded the machine and passed on a rerun of `history.spec.ts` (3/3). ## FOR-47 — Suggest a project's ticket source from its repository remote (2026-10-18)

Implementation revisions `049737438db211e56828a39d55ee50fd90e8bd8c` (core), `eac1e84ff24da9a8f4c009a53ff3d379ecce72fe` (service) and `8796a7cd39c1b3772e87d4a1845edb0afc5a0eb7` (desktop) on branch `for-47-suggest-source-from-remote` from main `ba3ec4c`; checks below ran on `8796a7c`. A project is a configured repository. `crewroom-core` `project_source.rs` reads the publish remote from Git (the target's remote, branch push remote, `remote.pushDefault`, upstream, `origin`, or the only remote; push URL preferred) and classifies it without keeping the address: `github.com/<owner>/<repo>` suggests GitHub Issues with owner/repo prefilled and GitHub PRs; `bitbucket.org` suggests Jira (site picked by the operator) and Bitbucket PRs; anything else is the operator's choice. Credentialed remotes are "unrecognised" and never shown. Remote parsing moved to core; the FOR-41 PR host selection now calls it. `POST /api/repositories/{id}/ticket-source` is the single confirmation; it re-reads the remote and stores it with the choice on the repository record (no schema change, no revision bump; repository edits keep it). A later remote that differs projects "Source no longer matches remote" with the saved source unchanged. A change is refused while queued or open work references the repository, naming it. Imported tickets are not rewritten. Settings → Repositories shows the block per repository. Receipt: [for-47-project-source-2026-10-18.json](for-47-project-source-2026-10-18.json).

Simulated evidence (real Git checkouts, synthetic remote addresses; nothing contacted):
- `crewroom-core` unit tests: host classification, suggestions, choice validation, mismatch and blocker text, Jira site collection.
- `crewroom-core/tests/project_source.rs`: publish-remote resolution (only remote, origin, pushRemote, push URL, credentialed); no save before confirmation; persistence across reopen; same-source no-op; repository edit keeps the source; moved remote reports mismatch without change; Jira needs a site; queued work blocks a change; after cancel the change succeeds and the imported GitHub ticket keeps its source fields.
- `crewroom-service/tests/ticket_sources.rs`: GitHub, Bitbucket and credentialed GitLab repositories get the right suggestion, the secret is absent from the workspace, cross-origin confirmation is 403, Jira without a site is 400, a saved site is offered to other projects, a moved remote stays a mismatch across restart.
- Desktop `project_source_tests.rs` `native_ticket_source_is_suggested_confirmed_warned_and_blocked`: through the real local service, the suggestion is shown with owner/repo prefilled, Confirm saves, a moved remote plus queued work shows the warning and named blocker and the disabled control sends nothing, and an entered Jira site then confirms Jira + Bitbucket.

Daemon run (not a provider check; the feature contacts no remote): a debug `crewroomd` from `8796a7c` on loopback port 4373 with an isolated, since-deleted data directory and a disposable checkout whose origin was the public Crewroom address. Linking suggested GitHub Issues `the-data-sherpa/crewroom`; a confirmation from another Origin got 403 and saved nothing; confirmation saved; switching origin to a synthetic Bitbucket address showed the mismatch warning, kept the GitHub source and refused Jira without a site (400); the warning survived a restart; confirming a synthetic Jira site saved Jira + Bitbucket; a GitLab origin then showed the mismatch with "operator chooses".

## FOR-42 — Model offerings show subscription, API or unknown billing (2026-10-18 UTC)

Implementation revision `0d7a769502fe5511adfd4c8464a94cd88a63b8fd`, review fix `695efb0019eb95401f7dff941b2e7866f896d9a8`, integrated onto FOR-45 main `ba3ec4c`. Pre-rebase: implementation `7be5d969a7e0f29b6932ec2be18adfa623405189` (tree `35a0c4713322ddc4d94b4a51f1a3fdadb537908b`, branch `for-42-billing-route-offerings`) and review fix `56577b9` on FOR-38 main `7975dcc`; the checks and live runs below name those pre-rebase revisions. Receipt: [for-42-billing-routes-2026-10-18.json](for-42-billing-routes-2026-10-18.json).

**Change.**
- Runtime: new `model_catalog::billing` detects `subscription`, `api` or `unknown` from the login each harness is launched with. Codex uses `auth.json` `auth_mode` and stored tokens or key. Claude Code uses its `.credentials.json` OAuth login (inference scope plus subscription plan) or a Console `primaryApiKey`; both or neither is unknown. OMP uses runner provider definitions: an API key on a first-party API host is API, and anything else is unknown. Detection opens files without following symlinks and returns kinds and file names only. Offerings gain an optional `billing`, and the route is part of the key (`offering:<harness>:<provider>:<model>:<route>`). Each discovered model expands into one offering per reachable route. A pre-billing selection or equivalence member resolves only when a single route exists; otherwise it asks for an explicit choice. A Codex API route is listed but blocked, because Crewroom starts Codex only through the ChatGPT login. Selections and previews record `billingRoute`. `verify_frozen_catalog_route` refuses a frozen route the login no longer offers ("Re-admission is required"), so launch and recovery never switch routes. Frozen offerings written before this change deserialize unchanged.
- Service: without a runner, billing is detected from the service's own `HOME`/`CODEX_HOME`. Choices carry `label` ("Harness · model · route") and `billingRoute`. Each configured harness row carries its detected routes and basis.
- Desktop: Models & budget and the role override list "Codex · GPT-… · subscription"-style rows; route text stays searchable. The refresh panel shows "Billing (provider): route · basis". Assignment details (previews and history) show "Billing: …", or "not recorded" for older selections.

**Simulated evidence.** Runtime unit tests cover each Codex, Claude and OMP login shape, plus canary checks that no credential value is serialized. Catalog tests cover dual-route listing, explicit choice, the blocked Codex API route, preview and admitted route recording across a store reopen, and launch refusal after the login changes. Offering identity tests cover routed keys, forged-route rejection, labels, backward-compatible frozen offerings and legacy resolution. Service tests check labels and routes over HTTP. GPUI tests check picker labels, the refresh-panel billing line from a synthetic ChatGPT login in an isolated HOME, and "Billing: subscription" in history details.

**Live evidence (this Linux machine, metadata only, no inference).**

## FOR-45 — GitHub Issues as a ticket source (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `ad18021037d026731f14d8f7f11e084a7128d43a`, documentation `73491feeb4901bfad7b3583b8b2ce53c868ed9b7`, review fixes `cf0e89baa1e5318175bcbea804db7687cfa8ceeb`, integrated onto FOR-46 main `cf9c203`. Pre-rebase: implementation `6a495e5`, documentation `6835b52` and review fixes `697d86b` on branch `for-45-github-issues-source` from main `80f9421` (FOR-43); the checks and live run below name those pre-rebase revisions. `crewroom-core` `github_issues.rs` validates the saved source (repository, labels, assignee, milestone, state) and each GitHub issue, then commits one complete observation through FOR-40's `import_source_rows` (key `#N`, title, body, `open`/`closed` as source status, URL, update time; site `https://github.com/<owner>/<name>` lower-cased). Schema 32 adds the `github_sync` record. `crewroom-runtime` `providers/github/issues.rs` lists issues with the filters, resolves a milestone title, follows `Link: rel="next"` on the API origin only, drops pull requests, and separates rejected, no access, issues disabled, rate limited (with reset time), unreachable and filter failures. The service adds `POST /api/github/issues/source`, `POST /api/github/issues/sync` and a status-only `githubIssues` workspace projection; the desktop adds **Settings → Integrations → GitHub Issues** and labels a local ticket's caption "Source status". Machine-readable receipt: [for-45-github-issues-2026-10-18.json](for-45-github-issues-2026-10-18.json).

Simulated evidence (loopback GitHub API, not github.com):
- `crewroom-service/tests/github_issues.rs` `github_issues_import_resync_and_report_unavailable_states`: saved filters reach GitHub (milestone title resolved to its number), two pages are read, the pull request is excluded, and three tickets show GitHub open/closed with no run created. A refined ticket's changed title re-syncs into the same ticket with re-acceptance and the brief kept. Not connected, rate limited (reset time shown, last sync and tickets kept), no access (not installed), issues disabled and rejected are each recorded by reason.
- `crewroom-core/tests/github_issues.rs`: status-only changes do not ask for re-acceptance; older observations, pull requests and duplicates save nothing; a sync or failure for a replaced source is refused or ignored; a schema-31 store gains the table.
- `providers/github/issues.rs` unit tests: next-page links on another origin are refused; filters and failure messages.
- Desktop `github_issues_tests.rs` `native_settings_save_a_github_issue_source_and_sync_truthfully` and `acceptance_tests.rs` `native_github_issue_shows_github_open_closed_apart_from_workflow_and_activity`: through the real local service, the section saves a source, states the missing connection, connects, syncs, and rows read "GitHub open"/"GitHub closed"; the facts bar shows "GITHUB STATUS" / "open" apart from workflow and agent activity.

Live evidence (github.com): a debug `crewroomd` built from `6a495e5` ran on loopback port 4359 with an isolated, since-deleted data directory and `CREWROOM_GITHUB_TOKEN` taken from the operator's `gh` login (an OAuth token, never printed; absent from responses and the daemon log). Against the operator-approved private sandbox, using synthetic issues created for this run: the label + milestone-title + state-all filter imported #3 (GitHub open) and #4 (GitHub closed) and excluded unlabelled #5; after the operator brief was saved and #3's title was edited on github.com, a re-sync updated the same ticket to source revision 2 with re-acceptance and the brief kept; an unfiltered sync imported the four issues and not pull request #2; a nonexistent repository was recorded as no access with the not-installed explanation; an invalid state filter was refused (400); a sync without Origin got 403; after restart without the environment token, sync was recorded as not connected while the last complete sync time and the four tickets were kept.

## FOR-46 — Publish pull requests to GitHub (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `2b17b7e25cd96db452801e30b18c01e389863e26` (tree `e53c42666caf72249f8aa54f7c0f47c7ff125923`), documentation and live probe `18da839682fed9a19967b7e961d259e252551f0e`, evidence `0b1ad6d5bc7bd96a80b5c622163b87f1f0d727f2`, integrated onto FOR-38 main `7975dcc`. It was implemented on `80f9421` (FOR-43 integrated) as `ecff62527c1d74c0beb5614ebb652aaf5022755a` with documentation and live probe `81030a787fee221717f65e2c46d1a55ecd261035`; the rebase conflicted only in these two records, and both tickets' entries were kept. The live evidence below binds to those pre-rebase revisions. A `github.com` publish remote selects the new `PullRequestHost::GitHub` (`crewroom-runtime/src/providers/github_pulls.rs`). It authenticates through the FOR-43 token route (`CREWROOM_GITHUB_TOKEN`, then the saved token) and normalizes GitHub pull requests, commit statuses, check runs and comments into the shape publication, readiness and continuation already use, so Bitbucket code paths are unchanged. The durable repository identity is `github:<numeric id>`. GitHub entries in `bitbucket.json` need `repository: {owner, name, id}` matching the remote. The branch is pushed over SSH as for Bitbucket. GitHub-sourced tickets get `Closes #N` (or `owner/name#N`) in the PR body; Jira tickets keep `KEY: title` and no closing keyword. Refusals carry remedies: 401, 403 and 404 keep the PR intent pending for reconcile, a rate limit stays pending, and 422 blocks for inspection. Refused pushes name branch protection or rules, a missing write key or an untrusted host key. The workbench shows the provider's PR state and observed head and base beside the published revision. Configuration is in [operations](../operations.md#github-pull-requests).

Simulated evidence (loopback GitHub REST fixture, not github.com), `crewroom-runtime/tests/github_publication.rs`:
- `github_pull_requests_publish_continue_and_report_readiness_at_a_named_revision`: create → marker search finds the same PR → description update after a new push keeps PR #5 with the new head, and no second PR is opened. Check-run re-runs replace earlier runs. Check runs naming the PR bind the base. `assess()` reports ready for exactly that head and base, and blocks an older revision, an unbound base or a failed required check (`reviewInvalidated`). Merged and closed map to `MERGED` (with merge commit) and `DECLINED`.
- `github_feedback_reads_every_page_of_conversation_and_review_comments`: 120 conversation comments over two pages plus inline review comments.
- `github_refusals_are_reported_with_remedies_and_never_skipped`: 401/403/404/rate-limit/422 messages, pending versus blocking outcomes, token never echoed, and a read-only account refused before any write.
- Unit tests: remote selection, identity normalization, config token route, host identity and SSH remote, push refusal remedies, `Closes` reference rules (`publication::prepare`), desktop `pr_facts`/`repository_label`.

Live evidence (github.com, private disposable sandbox approved by the operator): `examples/github-publication-live.rs` drove the production GitHub host (probe binary sha256 `194f9581…171a`) with the operator's `gh` OAuth token, never printed. Repository verified as `github:1424057732`. PR #2 created on `a0c0c69` over base `c4aba0f`, and the marker search found it. GitHub's `closingIssuesReferences` listed synthetic issue #1. An invalid token returned the 401 remedy, a nonexistent repository the 404 remedy, a wrong repository id "identity changed", and a duplicate create the 422 refusal. After pushing `f5696f0`, keyed continuation updated PR #2 in place and read back the new head. Observation found no statuses or check runs (the sandbox has no CI), so the PR was not ready, and one bot comment (feedback complete). After closing without merging, the PR read `DECLINED`. The issue was closed as not planned. Receipt: [for-46-github-publication-2026-10-18.json](for-46-github-publication-2026-10-18.json).

Checks on the implementation and documentation revisions: `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime` pass (lib **70 passed / 1 ignored**, `github_publication` **3/3**, all suites). `cargo test -p crewroom-service`: all suites pass; a doc-test step failed once on a shared-target artifact from a concurrent build and passed on rerun. `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop`: **136 passed / 1 failed / 15 ignored**. `retired_target_and_open_run_name_the_controls_that_clear_them` returned 409 "Repository or target branch could not be verified." under shared-machine load; that test and the new provider test pass on rerun. Strict Clippy is clean for runtime and service (all targets) and for desktop apart from the pre-existing `useless_conversion` in untouched `feedback_tests.rs`. Formatting is clean. `npm test` **332/332**, `npm run build` pass.

**Integration checks on the final branch head (implementation `2b17b7e`, base `7975dcc`).** `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=1` **323 passed, 0 failed** (the service doctest step hit a shared-target artifact from a concurrent worktree build and passed on a separate `--doc` rerun, 0 doctests); `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop` **138 passed, 15 ignored**; Clippy `-D warnings` (runtime/service all targets, desktop bins) and per-package format checks pass; `npm test` **332/332**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **29 passed, 2 failed**: `refinement.spec.ts:28` INT-03 and `research.spec.ts:27` INT-07/EVD-08 stop in fixture setup because the fixture-only OMP 18.4.12 is not installed on this machine, as recorded at the FOR-43 and FOR-38 integrations. Live runs were not repeated after the rebase.

Not executed: a full Crewroom run publishing live (the probe calls the host directly and the push used plain Git over SSH, not `PublicationGit`); live hosted checks, a live 403 or rate limit, a live branch-protection push refusal and a live merge; a fine-grained or classic PAT; a passing `npm run test:e2e` for the two OMP-fixture specs (see integration checks). ## FOR-38 — Model picker on Linux (2026-10-18 UTC)

Implementation revision `9b1881b418bf98a2efe3a80f277da9c86e0e8be4` (tree `65536eb0b161418086a8e626ad36177ad53342af`, branch `for-38-model-picker-linux`, on FOR-43 main `80f9421`). It was implemented on `93ebcd6` as `b8f38f0` (tree `147be9f0a1b6302c18b7cffac002ad4e0f0c4a4f`), rebased onto FOR-41 main `60be15e` as `a3c1ace` (tree `737130dece58806dc076e0eb8fc4bb0eaa4f727c`), then rebased onto `80f9421` at integration; the second rebase conflicted only in these two records, and both tickets' entries were kept. The rebase had conflicts in `execution.rs` imports, beside the FOR-41 service test and in these records; both sides were kept. The [receipt](for-38-model-picker-2026-10-18.json) holds the check list, result hashes and limits.

**Root cause, observed live.** The operator service on port 4318 reported `executionAvailable: false`, and its workspace had no `modelDiscovery` key. Its process environment had no `CREWROOM_RUNTIME_CONFIG`, and no runtime configuration exists on this machine. `Workflow`, and with it the model catalog, is created only from runner configuration. The desktop rendered the picker only when `modelDiscovery` was an object, so it fell back to free text without saying why. A second, independent cause: discovery accepted only `codex-cli 0.162.0`, `2.1.296 (Claude Code)` and `omp/18.4.12`. Before the change, the opt-in live discovery test failed with the installed `codex-cli 0.163.0`.

**Change.**
- Runtime: discovery accepts a list of verified versions per harness and names the harness, the reported version, the verified versions and the fix. A new `model_catalog::unconfigured` projection runs discovery for `omp`, `codex` and `claude` found on the service PATH. Every choice is marked `selection: "intent"` and stays ineligible by the admission predicate. Execution profiles attach only to CLI versions with a verified launch. `executionNote` explains a listed version without one. Executable hashing runs on the blocking pool.
- Adapters: Codex and Claude accept `codex-cli 0.163.0` and `2.1.302 (Claude Code)` after the live runs below. They record the version each process reported, and Claude's startup readback compares against that version. OMP execution stays pinned to 18.4.12.
- Service: when no runner is configured, the service opens the discovery-only catalog in `<data>/model-catalog`. `modelDiscovery` is always present: catalog facts, runner `reason`/`remedy`, per-harness `remedy`/`note`/`executionNote`, or an explained `unavailable` state. A catalog read error no longer fails the whole workspace.
- Desktop: all four jobs and the role override always render the searchable picker. In intent mode, listed models are selectable and labelled "checked when the runner is configured". The refresh panel and role editor show "Runner not configured: … Fix: …" or "Model catalog unavailable: … Fix: …". Missing executables show their fix in place of the generic error. Saved free text stays verbatim as "Legacy selection: …", flagged "Saved text kept as-is; it matches no listed model". Each harness row has its own accessible identity. Before this change, call-site-identified hints collapsed to one announced row.

**Tests.** Opt-in live `installed_harnesses_list_models_without_inference`, plus discovery drift wording (runtime). `newer_listed_cli_keeps_its_models_without_an_execution_profile` and `startup_readback_uses_the_launched_cli_version` (runtime unit). `unconfigured_runner_still_lists_installed_models_and_names_each_cause` covers a real service with a fake Codex on an isolated PATH, refresh, intent choices, verbatim legacy save and restart (service). `unconfigured_runner_lists_installed_models_and_explains_each_cause` covers native GPUI: all four job pickers plus the role picker, keyboard selection and save, legacy flagging, the runner/missing-harness/OMP notes and inheritance on an empty override. Existing settings tests now set saved values directly, because no free-text entry path remains; the role editor test asserts the picker is next in keyboard order.

**Live evidence (this Linux machine).** Simulated and live classes are separate:
- Live metadata discovery, isolated HOME, no inference: `codex-cli 0.163.0` 8 offerings, `2.1.302 (Claude Code)` 5, `omp/18.11.3` 1 synthetic loopback provider model (never contacted).
- Live disposable daemon on port 4371 with a new private data directory, no runner configuration and the real PATH (`scripts/verify_unconfigured_catalog.py`). Result: 13 intent choices (Codex 8, Claude Code 5, OMP 0 with the provider note), the OMP execution note, 0 runs and a clean exit. The first run exposed a catalog read blocked for 11.5–11.7 s while the debug daemon hashed CLIs on an async worker. After the fix, two runs measured 0.029 s and 0.005 s.
- Live inference through `scripts/verify_catalog_workflow.py` (native picker, production daemon, real Rust Mail, SIGKILL reattachment, pause/resume and durable completion; 7/7 checks each): **Codex 0.163.0** `gpt-6-astra` passed and **Claude Code 2.1.302** `haiku` passed. A **Codex 0.162.0** control also passed on this machine. The first Codex 0.163.0 attempt failed: the run start exceeded the fixture's 60 s limit after the coordinator initialized, the specialist stayed admitted, and daemon stderr was not retained. The immediate repeat passed. These runs used pre-rebase binaries built from the tree committed as `563f075` (rebased `31b3e52`), before rustfmt and one added unit test. Live runs were not repeated after the rebase.
- OMP 18.11.3 launch was **not** verified: there are no API-key provider definitions for the runner here, and the OMP RPC contract is pinned to 18.4.12.

**Integration checks on the final branch head (implementation `9b1881b`, base `80f9421`).** `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=1` **312 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored** (the doctest step hit a shared-target rebuild race from a parallel worktree and passed on a separate `--doc` rerun, 0 doctests); `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop` **137 passed, 15 ignored**; Clippy `-D warnings` and per-package format checks pass; `npm test` **332/332**; `npm run build` passes; `npm run test:e2e` **29 passed, 2 failed**: `refinement.spec.ts:28` INT-03 and `research.spec.ts:27` INT-07/EVD-08 stop in fixture setup because the fixture-only OMP 18.4.12 is not installed on this machine, as recorded at FOR-43 integration. Live runs were not repeated.

**Rebased revision `a3c1ace`.** `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -- --test-threads=1` **308 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**; `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop` **136 passed, 15 ignored**; Clippy `-D warnings` (core/runtime/service all targets, desktop bin) and per-package format checks pass; `npm test` **332/332**; `npm run build` passes.

**Checks at the pre-rebase revision `b8f38f0`.** `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime -- --test-threads=1` **134 passed, 2 ignored**. With parallel threads, unrelated lease/journal tests fail intermittently; single-threaded runs pass, matching the repository gate. `cargo test -p crewroom-service` **20/20**. `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop` **133 passed, 15 ignored**; one earlier full run failed without a retained name and the next two passed. Clippy `-D warnings` passes for runtime/service (all targets) and desktop (bin). Desktop `--all-targets` reports one existing lint in untouched `feedback_tests.rs`. ## FOR-43 — Connect GitHub with a personal access token (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `8babc7ab7c587e17ae07cd129b1113a7a762298a`, documentation `203e2a57a5e4613a5eb9511188e4ace0793b066a`, on base `60be15e` (FOR-41). Scope follows the operator decision of 2026-10-18: personal access token only; GitHub App device sign-in is FOR-51 and Enterprise Server is FOR-50. `crewroom-runtime` `providers/github.rs` calls the fixed `https://api.github.com` origin (loopback fixture override only) with `User-Agent`, `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and an API version header, and separates 401 rejection, 403/404 no access, rate limit and unreachable outcomes. `Integration::GitHub` adds `CREWROOM_GITHUB_TOKEN` to the FOR-39 store. `crewroom-service` `github.rs` adds `POST /api/github/{connect,check,disconnect}` and a status-only `github` workspace projection. `/credentials/github` saves are refused, so every saved token is validated first. Check results stay in memory, bound to a SHA-256 fingerprint of the checked token that is never serialized. **Settings → Integrations → GitHub** offers Connect, Check connection, Check repository access and Disconnect. Token permissions are documented in [operations](../operations.md#github-personal-access-token).

Simulated evidence (loopback GitHub API, not github.com):
- `crewroom-service/tests/github.rs` `connect_validates_the_token_stores_it_encrypted_and_reports_truthful_states`: a rejected token returns 400 with GitHub's reason and is not stored, and the unvalidated route is closed. A valid classic token shows the account, scopes and expiry, and only an encrypted 0600 value is written. Repository checks report granted and "No access to fixture/hidden". Revocation at the fixture reads "Expired or revoked". Foreign-origin, origin-less and unauthenticated mutations get 403. The environment override reports itself, survives Disconnect and shows unchecked → connected. After restart a saved token reads "not checked". Install-key loss reads unreadable with no request sent, then reconnects.
- `an_unreachable_github_is_not_reported_as_a_rejected_token`: connect returns 409 and saves nothing; an override check reads "Connection not confirmed".
- `providers/github.rs` unit tests cover token kinds, `owner/name` validation and the loopback-only origin override.
- Desktop `credentials_tests.rs` `native_settings_connect_github_with_a_validated_token_and_disconnect`: through the real local service, a rejected token shows the reason and clears the field. A valid token shows the account and kind but never renders the token. The repository no-access line appears, then Disconnect.

Live evidence (github.com): a debug `crewroomd` built from `8babc7a` (sha256 `8e67f158…9ccf`) ran on loopback port 4347 with an isolated, since-deleted data directory. It used the operator's `gh` login token, read in-process and never printed. That is a gh OAuth token (kind "other"), not a fine-grained or classic PAT. Observed: a synthetic invalid token was rejected with "Bad credentials" and no file written; connect succeeded as the operator account with the `repo` scope; the token was stored encrypted (0600); the public Crewroom repository was visible with push; a synthetic nonexistent repository returned "No access … (HTTP 404)"; an origin-less mutation returned 403; Disconnect removed the entry. After a restart with `CREWROOM_GITHUB_TOKEN`, the source was environment, unchecked until checked, then connected. The token was absent from all responses, the receipt and the daemon log. Earlier builds of the same change on bases `d08fc59` (`772cc5a`) and `ec08de4` (`07f2086`) produced an identical receipt. Receipt (login redacted): [for-43-github-token-2026-10-18.json](for-43-github-token-2026-10-18.json).

Strict Clippy is clean for runtime/service with all targets and for the desktop binary; formatting is clean. `npm test` **332/332** and `npm run build` pass. Shared-machine interference: one desktop run on an earlier base aborted in two unrelated collaboration/planning tests ("Another controller owns this workbench"; both passed in isolation), one was killed externally (SIGKILL), and one hit a stale `crewroom-core` artifact in the shared target directory; the full desktop suite then passed on this revision.

Pointing `CREWROOM_OMP_FIXTURE_MODULES` at the main checkout's fixture got further but then stopped when the Rust Mail fixture exited. This ticket changes no TypeScript server, client or e2e code. `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service --no-fail-fast` **167 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored**. `cargo fmt --check` is clean for runtime, service and desktop. `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop --no-fail-fast` **136 passed / 15 ignored**, run in an isolated target directory after `cargo build --workspace --bins`. A shared-target build failed earlier on a stale `crewroom-core` artifact left by a concurrent build, and the first isolated run failed the two model-picker tests until the `crewroom-process`/`crewroom-host` helper binaries were built.

## FOR-41 — Provider-neutral PR publication remote (2026-10-18 UTC)

Implementation revision `cde600fa6b70ae141517244c1012c9ef0e4a34b1` on branch `for-41-neutral-publication-remote`, rebased onto main `ec08de4` (FOR-40, after FOR-39); the pre-rebase revision `452d8f2` was on `93ebcd6`. The checks under "Checks executed on this revision" ran on `452d8f2`; the integration checks ran on the rebased branch. It is a prefactor with no intended change to Bitbucket behaviour. `crewroom-runtime/src/providers/remote.rs` resolves a publication provider from a publish remote (HTTPS, `ssh://` or scp-style). It rejects remotes that carry credentials, queries or fragments, and reports any host other than `bitbucket.org` as `Unsupported`. `providers/config.rs` accepts an optional per-repository `remote` in `bitbucket.json`. An absent remote keeps the original Bitbucket entry. A Bitbucket remote must name the configured workspace/slug. An unsupported remote becomes an `UnavailablePublication` with a truthful reason. `publication_remote/host.rs` adds `PullRequestHost`, which carries PR identity, search, create/update, hosted observation, comments, repository verification and the Git transport remote. `Connection`, publication dispatch/prepare, feedback and continuation verification use it. `Connection::new` accepts any `Into<PullRequestHost>`, so existing Bitbucket call sites are unchanged. Existing operator messages keep their exact Bitbucket text. The service projects `publicationProvider: {state: "unavailable", host, reason}` on the repository and `provider` on the run publication view. Settings shows "PR publication unavailable" with the reason.

Checks executed on this revision (Linux x86_64, shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, source/debug builds):
- `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime --lib -- --test-threads=1`: **54 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored**. New: `providers::remote` (3), `providers::config::publication_provider_resolves_from_the_configured_publish_remote`, `publication_remote::host` (2). With default parallel threads, a different `runner::codex`/`runner::journal` test failed on each run with "Host journal already has a writer." Those runner tests are untouched by this change and pass serially.
- `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-runtime` integration targets: all pass (catalog 6, claude adapter 10, comparison security 4, container command 14, host protocol 12, model catalog 5, discovery 5, jobs 3, process ownership 2, gateway 10, rpc 7, supervisor tasks 4).
- `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p crewroom-service`: all pass. New: `unsupported_publish_remote_is_reported_as_an_unavailable_publication_provider` against the real service.
- `cargo test -p crewroom-desktop`: **133 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored**. New: `repository_publication_state_distinguishes_unsupported_remote_from_unconfigured`.
- `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-desktop -- -D warnings`: clean. `cargo fmt --check` for the three crates: clean. With `--all-targets`, desktop clippy reports a `useless_conversion` that was already present in the untouched `feedback_tests.rs`.
- `npx tsx --test scripts/rust/provider-config.test.ts scripts/rust/bitbucket-client.test.ts` against examples built from this revision: **11/11** pass. These are simulated-provider checks of metadata strictness and Bitbucket client paging, identity and create-retry.
- `npm test`: **332/332**. `npm run build`: pass.

Their fixture expects the macOS Docker Desktop socket and pinned check images, and reports "Command engine response is uncertain". It was not re-executed end to end here. `npm run test:e2e` is left to the integrator's serial run. No live Bitbucket or other provider request was made. ### FOR-41 follow-up: Docker-backed publication journeys on Linux (2026-10-18)

Revision `d9d748e` on branch `for-41-bitbucket-continuation-linux`, based on main `3a2add0`. This closes the gap recorded above, where keyed continuation keeping the same PR had not been re-run end to end.

- **Cause.** The journeys failed on Linux because of the fixture, not because of publication code. `publication-workflow-fixture.ts` gave the repository fixture no socket, so the fixture fell back to `$HOME/.docker/run/docker.sock` (macOS Docker Desktop). It also pinned check image `sha256:9d2e5553…`, an arm64 image ID that cannot be pulled. The new `scripts/rust/docker-engine.ts` resolves the socket from `CREWROOM_DOCKER_SOCKET`, then a `unix://` `DOCKER_HOST`, then the platform default (`/var/run/docker.sock` on Linux). It resolves the check image from `CREWROOM_CHECK_IMAGE`, then the historical pin on macOS, otherwise `busybox@sha256:bdf57e528e45e4433820e045b29b4597825a1c9e38353532d90a01445013f82e`, pulled on demand and used by image ID. The fixture passes the socket to the native process and uses it for teardown. `tests/docker-engine.test.ts` covers the resolution order.
- **Drift, not FOR-41 regressions.** After the Docker fix, two cases still failed. Both fail identically when built from a source export of pre-FOR-41 main `ec08de4` with only the Docker resolver applied:
  - `native cancelled continuation retains its PR…` failed with "Needs an accepted implementation plan." The continuation run admits new coding assignments, so it is subject to the implementation-plan admission gate. The test now accepts a plan through the store's `plan_source`/`plan_save`/`plan_decide` (exposed by `repository-fixture`, as `workflow-fixture` already does) before `queue_later`. It also asserts that the continuation carries that plan.
  - `Stop fences a pending second run…` seeded its second run through the legacy TypeScript `Workbench`, which now refuses the native schema ("Workflow database is newer than this application."). It now seeds through native `create_task`/`refine`/`queue`.
- **Checks executed on `d9d748e`** (Linux x86_64, Docker Engine 29.7.2, Node 26.10.0). `crewroom-process` and the `repository-fixture`, `publication-git-fixture` and `container-fixture` examples were built in an isolated target directory from this revision. The shared target was not used, because worktrees with identical relative paths share artifact names there and a fresh build can pick up another tree's binary. Fixture temporary directories were on disk, because the shared `/tmp` quota was exhausted during the first attempt.
  - `tsx --test scripts/rust/publication-service.test.ts`: **20/20** (log SHA-256 `8cf3fd4f…`).
  - `tsx --test scripts/rust/publication-recovery.test.ts`: **13/13** (`cc9c98d9…`).
  - `tsx --test scripts/rust/publication-successors.test.ts`: **4/4** (`6d74f8ec…`). These cases cover target refresh on the same PR, captured feedback publishing a successor on its existing PR, and a cancelled keyed continuation (`continuedFrom.publicationId`) that keeps the first PR's id with exactly one POST and one PUT.
  - Strict `tsc --noEmit` over the touched journey files passes. `cargo fmt -p crewroom-runtime --check` and `cargo clippy -p crewroom-runtime --example repository-fixture -- -D warnings` are clean.
  - `npm test` **334/334** and `npm run build` pass.
  - No check container was left behind (`docker ps -a --filter label=io.crewroom.command` is empty).
- **Boundary.** The provider is the local simulated Bitbucket HTTP fixture with a synthetic token. Git, the Docker check containers, the native store and the process supervision are real. No live Bitbucket request was made. `npm run test:e2e` is left to the integrator.

Integration checks after rebasing onto FOR-40 main `41feb31`. The code revision is now `76031b4` (the checks above name the pre-rebase `d9d748e`) and the evidence revision is `5512b3e`. The only rebase conflict was this file, resolved by keeping both the FOR-40 and FOR-41 entries. Simulated Bitbucket provider only; Git and Docker are real.

## FOR-40 — Whole-state restore across the source migration (2026-10-18)

Branch `for-40-restore-across-migration` from main `3a2add0`; code and tests `df61cab`. This closes the gap left at the FOR-40 integration, where restore across the migration had been checked only by an in-process SQLite file restore. Simulated providers only: Jira issues enter through the daemon's own import operation. No live Jira site, credentials or packaged release build were used.

- `scripts/verify_source_migration_restore.py` follows the documented stopped whole-state backup and same-path restore recipe ([operations](../operations.md#backup-and-same-path-restore)) across schema 30→33, using real daemons. A `crewroomd` built at pre-FOR-40 main `d08fc59` (schema 30, Jira-named fields) seeds a disposable installation over its authenticated HTTP API. The seed has two imported Jira tickets (`In Progress`/`3`, `Backlog`/`1`), two local tickets, three operator refinements, two notes and the resulting requirement revisions. The script stops the daemon and copies the whole installation (data directory, `crewroom.secret`, SQLite with its WAL files, controller files, a non-database marker file and `roles.json`), then takes a SHA-256 and mode inventory. It deletes the live state and restores the backup at the same path; the inventory matches. The current `crewroomd` then opens the restored state and migrates it to schema 33.
- The run checks the following. Ticket identity and order are unchanged. Every projected ticket field is equal after the `jiraStatus`/`jiraStatusId` → `sourceStatus`/`sourceStatusId` rename, and the local "Not linked" placeholder is dropped. Notes and requirement revisions are identical. The stored `imported` JSON is renamed in place, in key order, and stored refinements are byte-for-byte equal. The marker file is restored. A Jira import after the migration matches the migrated tickets by source identity, with no duplicates: a status-only move (`Done`/`4`) keeps acceptance, a title change requires re-acceptance, and briefs are kept. For rollback, the pre-FOR-40 daemon refuses the migrated store ("Workflow database is newer than this application"). That refusal rewrites only the controller epoch and SQLite WAL bytes; the schema version and full SQL dump are unchanged. Restoring the same backup lets the pre-FOR-40 daemon reopen identical facts at schema 30.
- Executed on this revision against legacy `crewroomd` sha256 `a221f5ed…5843` (build of `d08fc59`) and the current debug build from this branch (its service, runtime and core sources are identical to main `3a2add0`; this branch changes only tests, the script and the clients): **passed**, with 9 restored files, 5 owned daemon processes and all of them stopped. The private receipt stays under the scratch evidence directory. It holds synthetic content, but its connection logs and inventory are not published.
- `crates/crewroom-core/tests/fixtures/schema-30-daemon-state.sql` is a SQL dump of that pre-FOR-40 daemon's backup database (synthetic content only, 10 KB). The new test `ticket_sources.rs::whole_state_written_by_the_pre_migration_daemon_restores_and_migrates_losslessly` loads it, backs it up, restores it and migrates it with the current `Store`. It compares every stored field, key order, refinement, note and requirement revision; checks the provider labels ("Jira In Progress", "Jira Backlog", "Not linked"); re-imports Jira by identity; and restores the backup again for rollback. Unlike the earlier hand-written schema-30 rows, this input comes from the old code's actual output, so the test runs in normal `cargo test` without the old binary.
- Local ticket labelling after FOR-45: the native facts bar shows **Source status / Not linked / Local ticket** for a local ticket (FOR-45 `ad18021`). `native_session_shell_groups_tickets_keeps_four_facts_separate_and_drawer_follows_width` now asserts that caption and that neither "JIRA STATUS" nor "Jira ticket" appears for a local ticket. The retired TypeScript web client (`src/main.tsx`, served only by the schema-26 legacy server) still captioned every ticket "Jira status". It now says "Jira status" only for `source === 'jira'` and "Source status" otherwise. Its field stays `jiraStatus`, because the retired TypeScript service keeps that name.

Checks on `df61cab` (simulated providers only):

- `cargo test --locked -p crewroom-desktop -j6 -- --test-threads=4`: **146 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored**, including the extended local-caption assertions.
- `cargo clippy --locked -p crewroom-core --tests -- -D warnings` and `-p crewroom-desktop -- -D warnings`: clean (the first core run was SIGKILLed under shared-machine load and passed when rerun with `-j2`). `cargo fmt -p crewroom-core -p crewroom-desktop -- --check` clean.

- `npm run test:e2e` was not run here; the integrator runs it on the fixed port.

Integration checks after rebasing onto FOR-48 main `d1d4701` (code and tests now `01eba6d`, evidence `b0bdb03`; the checks above name the pre-rebase `df61cab`). The rebase had no conflicts. ## FOR-40 — Provider-neutral ticket source and status (2026-10-18 UTC)

Implementation revision `0afdf129548abf02161a60e29df27e81b65080c0` on branch `for-40-neutral-ticket-source`, rebased onto main `d08fc59` (FOR-39); the pre-rebase revision `f7805e4` was on `93ebcd6`. The pre-rebase checks below were executed on `f7805e4`; the integration checks were executed on the rebased revision. This is a pure prefactor for FOR-45 (GitHub Issues).

- `crates/crewroom-core/src/ticket_source.rs`: `Provider` (`JIRA`, `GITHUB`), `kind`, `provider`, `status`, `status_label` ("Jira In Progress" / "Not linked"), `status_value`, `ticket_label`, `kind_tag`, `belongs_to(task, provider, site)`, `requirements_changed`, and `Store::import_source_rows` over a neutral `SourceObservation`. `jira.rs` only maps Jira issues onto it. Stored key order is kept (`sourceStatus` takes `jiraStatus`'s position) because adopted-requirement messages embed task snapshots.
- Schema 31 (`migrations.rs::migrate_ticket_sources`, `store::SCHEMA_VERSION`): renames `jiraStatus`→`sourceStatus` and `jiraStatusId`→`sourceStatusId` in place. It drops the "Not linked" placeholder only on non-provider tickets (it is derived from `source`). Any other value carries over. A record with both forms fails the version closed and rolls back. Historical run `taskSnapshot`s and message bodies are immutable and are not rewritten.
- Gates: `jira_delivery` (`prepare`, `dispatch`, research-summary check), `jira_sync` retained-issue selection and the research reporter prompt use `ticket_source` in place of hard-coded `source == "jira"` checks. Re-acceptance reason text is `"{provider} requirements changed. …"`, which is identical for Jira.
- Desktop: the ticket row chip, key tag, facts bar and ticket header read the neutral model. Local tickets keep "Not linked", "Local ticket" and the "Jira status" caption. That caption is a deliberate hold until FOR-45 can configure a second provider.

## FOR-39 — Install-time crewroom.secret with encrypted credentials (2026-10-18)

Implementation revision `728ac261b6c40f1385d401de6e38bce3cb99ffc0` (backend `1ba7828`, on base `93ebcd6`). `crewroom-runtime` `providers/secrets.rs` creates `<data>/crewroom.secret` (32 random bytes, 0600) on first start and never creates one while encrypted values exist. It writes tokens to `<data>/credentials.json` only as `$crewroom$v1$` XChaCha20-Poly1305 values with the integration name as associated data. `providers/config.rs` resolves credentials as environment override, then stored token, then (macOS only) keychain. An unreadable stored value returns "Credentials unreadable — reconnect" before dispatch. `crewroom-service` adds `POST /api/credentials/{jira,bitbucket}` (`token`, optional `replaceUnreadable`) and `/clear`, plus a status-only `credentials` projection in the workspace. The desktop **Settings → Integrations** panel (formerly "Jira") saves masked tokens, shows the active source, and offers Reconnect and Remove.

Simulated evidence (loopback provider, not live Jira):
- `crewroom-service/tests/credentials.rs` `jira_import_on_linux_uses_a_token_saved_through_settings`: covers first-run key creation, the settings save, encryption at rest, and import through the stored bearer token against the fixture. It then takes a stopped whole-state backup, restores it at the same path and decrypts again. With the key removed, it checks for no crash, no regeneration, the unreadable label, no dispatched request, a refused plain save, explicit reconnect and clear.
- `providers/secrets.rs` unit tests cover key creation and stability, per-integration binding, input validation, missing, mismatched and non-private keys, environment-source reporting and restore with or without the secret. `providers/config.rs` `stored_tokens_authenticate_on_linux_and_environment_overrides_them` covers Jira and Bitbucket.
- Desktop `workbench/credentials_tests.rs` `native_settings_save_a_jira_token_encrypted_and_never_show_it_again`: typed save through the real local service, cleared field, no rendered token, Reconnect after key loss, then remove.

Checks on `728ac261b6c40f1385d401de6e38bce3cb99ffc0`: `cargo test -p crewroom-runtime -p crewroom-service -p crewroom-desktop` all pass (desktop **133 passed / 15 ignored**, runtime lib **54 passed / 1 ignored**, service suites including credentials **1/1**). Strict Clippy is clean for runtime/service with all targets and for the desktop binary; the only desktop test-target warning is pre-existing in the untouched `feedback_tests.rs`. Formatting is clean. `npm test` **332/332** and `npm run build` pass. Receipt: [for-39-secret-store-2026-10-18.json](for-39-secret-store-2026-10-18.json).

Not executed: live Jira import with a stored token (no Jira token exists on this machine; the operator decided on 2026-10-18 that it is not a gate). No live Bitbucket request was made, and `npm run test:e2e` was left to the integrator. The threat model does not cover processes running as the same OS user.

## FOR-17 — Independent public repository verified (2026-10-18 UTC)

Published root `d9e2607a857950a4bb593534a7c0dc1b0a906269`, tree `47e041c04b09c12642741fab036e61e569f98556`, exactly matches reviewed private preparation `b6393176632ad391c1cf594267f05ed8f866c832`. Preparation changes only seven documentation/agent-instruction files over `5dcbafd0210884ab01adb8cc00245c560c297bfe`; application source, vendored source, locks, package scripts and notices are unchanged. The [publication receipt](for-17-publication-2026-10-18.json) records source binding, checks, private artifact hashes and limitations. This receipt is a documentation follow-up to the single initial source commit.

The full 2,731-file snapshot and commit metadata have zero known-private-pattern matches. Gitleaks 8.30.1 reports 400 tree findings and 401 root-history findings. Independent inspection clears their exact spans: 396/397 SHA-256 source mappings, one public signing-key fingerprint and three ordinary vendor expressions. All 43 binary assets are unchanged; the application screenshot was independently inspected as synthetic fixture data. Standards and spec/privacy reviews report zero findings. Executed in the isolated single-root snapshot: **22/22 native-script tests**, synthetic Linux packaging preserving **four source and ten vendor notices**, **four missing-notice refusals**, **four binary hash checks**, overwrite refusal, and `npm run build` on Node 26.10.0. The existing Vite large-chunk warning remains. Entry-point documentation checks cover **43 local links**; whitespace and Git integrity pass.

The public repository and private archive have distinct GitHub IDs; the new repository is not a fork. A fresh anonymous mirror contained exactly one physical/reachable commit and only main. Anonymous repository/README requests return HTTP 200, with README bytes matching the root. Old private blob/commit-object and former PR probes return HTTP 404 against the new repository; the old archive also returns 404 anonymously while authenticated inspection confirms private and archived. GitHub's higher-level commit lookup returns 422 with “No commit found” for old SHAs, so Git-object and anonymous probes were used to distinguish absence from an API error.

A verified private all-ref bundle and Git metadata archive preceded migration of the existing checkout. Its tracked working files and two pre-existing untracked files are unchanged by that migration; runtime installation state was not modified. The original Git metadata and local branches remain privately backed up. `AGENTS.md` now explicitly governs public-content handling and prevents importing private history. No GitHub Support request or old-object deletion is claimed.

All earlier revision IDs and source hashes in this file remain bound to private development history. They are retained historical evidence, not fresh tests of the public initial commit. No application-domain, native GUI, live-provider, installation or cross-platform suites were repeated for these documentation/history changes. Current reproduction routes remain in [Contributing](../../CONTRIBUTING.md) and [the feature map](feature-map/README.md).

## FOR-18 — Native startup port collision recovered (2026-10-14)

The operator's existing Linux release package identifies source `fdb68d2f0ba5fd9bfe8e9edc79f803bd9412a48b`; diagnosis ran at checkout `a811e494afe4ab73e1f255ffa0a5a8e58bf92d54`. The [receipt](for-18-native-startup-2026-10-14.json) retains all five packaged executable hashes and the verification scope. There is no production implementation change.

An isolated foreground probe of the packaged daemon failed twice on port 4318 with `Address already in use (os error 98)` in approximately 0.05 seconds. The same probe passed authenticated readiness on port 4319 in approximately 0.06 seconds, then stopped and reaped its owned child and verified descriptor removal. The probe and detailed local results remain in ignored `.crewroom/diagnostics/for-18/`.

The requested installation had zero tickets, runs, queue requests and intake requests before startup. Its bundled launcher started successfully with the original data/role paths and `--port 4319`. Authenticated health and workspace reads passed; headless reopen reused the same daemon PID. The Wayland compositor reported a mapped, visible Crewroom window and desktop stderr was empty. The original port-4318 listener's executable and process birth identity remained unchanged. Both operator installations remain running intentionally; all disposable probe children exited.

This verifies startup and window creation only. It adds no interactive GUI, embedded-terminal, agent-execution or provider-delivery claim. Domain/build/browser suites were not rerun because no application code changed. The default port remains 4318, so this installation's stopped restart must retain the explicit port. FOR-17 history/publication work remains separate.

Executed checks: native script unit tests **22/22**; synthetic ELF packaging with **four** source notices and **ten** required vendor notices preserved, **four** missing-source-notice refusals, final binary hashes and overwrite refusal; `npm run build` on Node **26.10.0**; locked Cargo metadata; native-script and documented-shell syntax; **53** local documentation links/anchors; prose lint and whitespace checks. The existing Vite chunk-size warning remains. Independent standards and spec reviews found no implementation defects; a targeted follow-up corrected the core license expression. Triage retains **five local Git-operation capability values** as publication hygiene findings; other findings are recorded hashes, a public signing-key identifier, synthetic ownership markers or ordinary vendored expressions. The implementation-only diff returned zero findings. These counts are scan results, not proof of absence of arbitrary private content.

It includes identifying provider/account data and private repository architecture/delivery details. Current-file deletion would leave historical exposure. The operator subsequently authorized sanitizing and rewriting the existing repository history. The original complete bundle remains private; candidate scanning, fresh checks and hosted-object clearance must pass before public visibility.

Production-daemon scripted lifecycle checks and schema restore are separate from inference evidence. An early launch missed its control revision and was fixed. A result/pause race led to atomic unavailable-result/aggregate-pause persistence. The production-daemon regression failed on frozen build 07 and passed on build 10 across six initial/replacement catalogs, eight rejected self/UUID calls and two peer deliveries. ## MH5 — Intake and accepted implementation plans (FOR-10, 2026-10-13)

Queue with a current accepted plan skips Grill. Otherwise one linked coordinator inspects bounded text at the pinned commit, records missing decisions and stops for the operator. Answers are persisted before external delivery. Acceptance and optional requirements adoption commit together; stopped ownership precedes closing Grill and creating one coding successor. Source/requirements drift holds future admission without rewriting accepted history or already-admitted recovery. Native plan editing supports package dependencies, outputs, criteria and validation; dirty drafts survive source conflicts and cancellation.

Executed evidence:

- Domain `npm test`: **332/332**; browser build passes; `npm run test:e2e`: **31/31** with Node 24.

## M8 S8 — Playbooks (2026-10-06)

Accepted at `c92fe4d`. Settings stores a private, revision-checked playbook catalog next to roles.json. An operator can select a checklist before queueing, accept a coordinator proposal or change it in a delivery/research session. Each selection copies a catalog revision and starts a new selection version. Changes after admission and their steering receipts commit together; paused runs keep pending delivery visible. Queue replay checks the original choice even after a later selection or a missing request receipt.

The coordinator's `playbook_status`, `propose_playbook` and `record_playbook_step` tools are unavailable to specialists and refinement runs. Progress requires a current coordinator and matching selection version. Skipping requires a reason. Durable receipts make retries repeatable; checklist progress changes neither workflow stages nor evidence/readiness. Catalog edits and daemon restart retain the run copy. The header bounds long checklists to a scrolling region.

The [source-bound receipt](m8-s8-playbooks-2026-10-06.json) records native domain **167/167** plus final playbook **4/4**, retained domain **332/332**, browser **31/31**, builds, Clippy and formatting. Desktop coverage is **46/47 plus the repaired new check 1/1**, with ten existing ignored fixtures. The failure searched for the session header behind the intentionally open narrow Brief drawer; the test now closes the drawer first. Earlier fixture setup errors are retained in the receipt. No single full 47/47 desktop run is claimed.

Four actual OMP 18.6.3 agents and external Rust Mail ran against the final package. The coordinator proposed a checklist, operator selection arrived as steering, a reasonless skip failed, valid progress persisted, catalog edits preserved the copy, and daemon interruption reattached the same eight host/agent processes. All eight stopped afterward. Provider replies were scripted; this verifies the runner contract, not live model quality or delivery.

The macOS GUI check created a playbook and selected it on a paused disposable run. Copied steps and pending steering remained separate from Not ready. That GUI proof predates only the final queue replay guard, covered by focused core tests and the final package build. Native interaction tests cover 390 px. Disposable GUI/service and test processes stopped; active installation, real settings and the retained pilot were untouched. **S1–S8 are accepted within this macOS scope.** Linux/Wayland terminal verification remains an explicit follow-up, as approved by the operator.
