## M8 S3 — Needs you inbox (2026-10-04)

Implements [S3](../workbench-redesign.md#s3--needs-you-inbox-read-projection--existing-actions). The service adds one read projection, `needsYou`, to the workspace snapshot (`crates/crewroom-service/src/views/needs.rs`). It is built from the run runtimes the workspace view already loads, plus each run's publication record. It lists repair-limit grants, pending learning proposals (and approved ones whose role save must be retried), source changes needing a brief review or requirement adoption, PR feedback that only an explicit Resume can act on, uncertain or blocked publication and Jira outcomes, and attempts with uncertain process ownership. The feedback rule is a read-only mirror of `queue_feedback_resume`'s gate in core (`feedback_awaits_resume`). It excludes feedback that is in flight, terminally held, or already captured by the latest continuation. Each item is derived from recorded state and disappears when that state is resolved from any screen; terminal output never creates one. Without an execution layer the key is absent, and the workbench says decisions are unavailable instead of showing zero.

The workbench adds a **Needs you · n** sidebar entry and page. Actions post to the same endpoints with the same request keys and form drafts as the screens that own them: repair allowance, learning approve/reject/retry, requirement adoption (reason required once work has started), Resume, and publication/Jira reconciliation (disabled with a stated reason when the integration is unavailable). Ownership items and brief reviews open the session (History or Brief). There is no "All"/resolved view: the projection holds open decisions only. In-flight `sending` publication states are not listed. The narrow navigation overlay now keeps the root's focus tracking and Tab bindings. The new keyboard test found that the overlay was previously unreachable by Tab, which is a pre-existing defect.

Checks on Arch Linux/Wayland: desktop **40/40** with ten ignored. Native core/runtime/service **151/151**, including three new projection unit tests: each kind appears and is removed by its resolution, a source change asks for the brief first while ended runs ask nothing, and addressed feedback is not asked again. `cargo fmt --check` and Clippy are clean for the core, service and desktop crates. The debug package builds. The new acceptance test seeds a paused run with exhausted repair cycles, a recorded learning proposal and a terminal-style "waiting for approval" agent event; the inbox lists exactly the two recorded decisions. Granting the cycle from the inbox persists the allowance through the service. A learning rejection posted directly to the API removes its item without an inbox action. A saved brief revision creates an adoption item whose button stays inert until a reason is given; adoption then updates the run's snapshot. *Open session* selects the ticket, and the page is keyboard-reachable from the narrow navigation at 390 px. A second test covers the unavailable state. The TypeScript domain and browser suites were not rerun; no retained TypeScript contract changed.

**Outstanding:** the on-screen check. The rebuilt package launched against seeded demo data (`demo_seed.rs` now adds a learning proposal and a changed Jira source) on a separate port, and the window opened on Hyprland. It sat outside the visible area of the operator's active scrolling workspace, and it was not moved there to avoid disturbing ongoing work, so no screenshot is claimed. S2's live terminal check is also still outstanding; S3 was taken next as [status](../status.md#continuation-order) directed.

## M8 S2 — Embedded agent grid (2026-10-04)

Implements [S2](../workbench-redesign.md#s2--embedded-agent-grid); native UI only. The session column now holds the coordinator tile with the steering conversation beside it on wide windows (stacked below 1500 px), then the specialist slots, the role picker and the latest candidate's required checks. Tiles are built from the workbench's existing run observation (runtime, events), so no extra polling thread is added. Only current, unretired attempts get tiles; a read-only Ghostty surface is attached only when an attempt has a terminal pane and the service returns its verified attachment, using the same spawn code as the agent wall. Pending team proposals are drawn as dashed *Proposed · not started* tiles with no terminal, and the remaining of three slots open the existing insertion form. Each tile can switch to a timeline of its recorded events or be maximized; hidden surfaces are set invisible. The agent wall remains available as *Pop out agent wall*.

An initial version reused the wall's polling thread as a feed; the deterministic GPUI test scheduler rejected its foreign-thread wakeups (16 failures), so the session moved onto the workbench observation and fetches terminal attachments through the GPUI executor.

Checks on Arch Linux/Wayland: desktop **38/38** with ten ignored, `cargo fmt --check` and Clippy clean for the desktop crate. The new acceptance test covers tiles for admitted attempts without claiming a terminal, a pending proposal drawn as not started with one free slot, the picker's stated blocking reason (no runner in the fixture), steering beside the coordinator, maximize/restore, the timeline toggle and keyboard reach at 390 px. Two existing insertion tests now open the picker first. Visual check: the rebuilt package rendered the coordinator tile beside steering and two specialist tiles above the fold at 1600×1000 with seeded demo data.

**Outstanding:** a live terminal surface was not exercised. The terminal endpoint requires a configured runner and verified process ownership, which this Linux machine does not have; recorded fixtures have no process. Verify on a configured runner (Linux or the macOS package) before treating S2 as accepted.

## M8 S1 — Session shell and sidebar (2026-10-04)

Implements [S1](../workbench-redesign.md#s1--session-shell-and-sidebar-ui-only) of the approved workbench redesign; native UI only, no service or schema change. The sidebar groups tickets into Active runs, Queue (local order, with *Manage queue*) and Other, and each row shows Jira status, workflow state and agent activity as separate labelled chips. The ticket header shows four separate facts: Jira status, Workflow, Agent activity and Readiness (from the run's evidence assessment; "Evidence not current" when the observation is stale). Brief, Results and History moved into a details drawer: beside the session at widths ≥1100 px, in place of it below that with *Back to session*. The coordinator conversation and specialist insertion stay in the session column. Roles, Repositories, Learning and Jira share one Settings view behind the gear, keeping their element IDs.

Two defects were fixed on the way: repeated sidebar headings and rows built with call-site labels shared one accessibility identity (a new `named` label gives each its own), and the coordinator role list hid roles in a 180 px nested scroll area in the narrower drawer.

Checks on Arch Linux/Wayland: desktop **37/37** with ten ignored (nine existing external-fixture cases and the new manual demo seeder), `cargo fmt` and Clippy clean for the desktop crate. The new acceptance test covers sidebar grouping and order, separate row and header facts (a paused run with a recorded coordinator reads "1 agent · admitted", never executing or ready), drawer behaviour at 1280 and 390 px by keyboard, and the settings navigation. Existing tests that clicked moved controls now use a `navigate` helper that follows the new path. Visual check: the rebuilt debug package ran on Hyprland/Wayland against seeded demo data (`demo_seed.rs`; recorded fixtures, no executed agents). At 1440×900 the grouped sidebar, separate chips, four-fact header (readiness "Not ready · Repository work has not been prepared"), session column and Brief drawer rendered as designed; at 420 px the header facts wrapped two by two and the drawer toggle replaced the side column. S1 is accepted.

## Post-migration — Arch Linux source support (2026-10-04)

The operator requested Linux support and parallel implementation. Three workers covered runtime, desktop and packaging. The same workflow authority and separate supervised processes remain in place. Bootstrap selects verified Linux Zig archives, packaging emits an unsigned directory with binary/source/dependency receipts, and the launcher uses GNU permissions checks and direct Wayland launch while retaining authenticated daemon reuse. Provider tokens reach the service and are removed from the GUI environment. Linux Jira uses an explicit OAuth bearer token; missing Jira/Bitbucket credentials remain unavailable instead of attempting macOS Keychain commands. Native Quit and read-only terminal copy use Linux shortcuts. No active installation or global dependency configuration was replaced.

Actual x86_64 Omarchy/Hyprland debug build, package and GUI close/reopen passed. A disposable local ticket appeared in both windows, and the same daemon preserved it. The installed headless launcher separately passed private-path refusal, authenticated reuse, clean stopped restart, role/task persistence and cleanup. The machine lacks system libc++; validation used official Arch packages extracted under ignored checkout-local state with signatures/checksums verified. Ordinary installation dependencies are in the [Linux guide](../linux.md).

Checks: **148/148** native core/runtime/service, **36/36** native desktop with nine existing external-fixture cases ignored, **18/18** vendored terminal unit checks, **332/332** retained domain, **22/22** bootstrap/package/launcher isolation, **13/13** process cleanup, native build/package and TypeScript build. Browser **29/31**: two retained fixtures stop at `/Users/crewroom/.bun/bin/omp` and also retain macOS sandbox assumptions; no pass is claimed. The initial domain run was **278/332** because temporary bare Git remotes inherited the host's default branch. Explicit `main` initialization repaired all 54 affected cases, and the final complete suite passed. The first GUI smoke opened successfully but its close helper used obsolete Hyprland dispatch syntax; the helper was corrected, cleanup verified, and the complete close/reopen passed with unchanged application binaries.

The [receipt](linux-native-2026-10-04.json) binds the base revision and modified source hashes, build/package hashes, checks and retained failures. New Rust 1.98 Clippy suggestions in unchanged code are retained separately from the pinned-toolchain result. Live Jira/Bitbucket/model delivery, external OMP/Mail/Docker workflow acceptance on Linux, physical terminal-surface interaction, Linux optimized-release acceptance, X11/ARM64 and a macOS rerun remain outside this bounded proof. Final process inventory found no attributable test leftovers; no quiet-terminal quality claim is used.

## Post-migration — Test process cleanup (2026-10-04)

The operator requested a way to stop lingering test processes. The standalone Python tool and npm aliases now preview current-user test processes, register future test commands, stop identified processes with TERM, and optionally escalate verified survivors with KILL. Scope comes from a private test-run marker, recognized disposable fixture paths or observed descendants. Before each signal the tool rechecks birth identity, executable and argument fingerprint. It protects normal native/pilot paths, excludes its ancestors, and preserves files and containers.

**13/13 checks pass on macOS** in 5.898 seconds. Real disposable processes cover detached children after wrapper exit, scrubbed descendants after parent death, TERM resistance, fixture scoping, unrelated survivors and normal/pilot exclusions. A process-table double exercises PID reuse refusal. Actual CLI checks cover JSON preview, invalid scope, survivor exit status and force stop; wrapper checks cover private records and exit-code forwarding. The npm preview and tracked-run aliases also passed. The final read-only inventory reported zero identifiable test leftovers and kept the active normal daemon protected.

The [source-bound receipt](test-process-cleanup-2026-10-04.json) records the base revision, exact implementation/test/package hashes and development repairs. Application domain, type/build and GUI suites were not rerun for this standalone utility. Linux inspection is implemented but has no live validation in this record. Processes that are unreadable or already detached without a marker or fixture path cannot be safely identified; a zero result does not prove their absence. See the [operations guide](../operations.md#lingering-test-processes).

## N3.3b1 — Native supervised process lifecycle (2026-10-03)

The native process supervisor now registers driven per-run FIFO work, launches independent host/OMP processes in a dedicated tmux server, verifies their exact OS/HTTP identities, observes bounded event pages and recovers saved journals. A new checked-owned helper mode preserves the host PID and terminal output. Its private configuration and checked descriptor precede dispatch; current control and authority are rechecked around the actual spawn.

Cancelled callers do not abandon launched or queued work. Stop and close fence admission, cancel observers, drain pending launches, stop the exact host and runner, and preserve final Docker receipts before removal. Unclaimed queued attempts remain admitted. Slow HTTP observations with verified live processes are degraded; they do not create replacements. Reattachment requires the saved backend, token, PIDs and session. Delayed dispatch after controller death is sealed. A journal or SQLite receipt failure cannot skip cleanup of other known hosts, and unresolved identity or engine ownership remains held.

**17 new supervisor scenarios pass**: eight lifecycle, four observation and five cleanup cases. These use actual native host/tmux processes and SQLite, including controller SIGKILL before dispatch and before saved PIDs, same-process reattachment, cancelled launch/close waiters, held launches, mismatched identities, replaced/dangling tool-directory paths and SQL failure. Two cleanup cases use actual Docker descendants after controller/host death and during engine unavailability. Scripted runner replies and seeded workflow records are explicit fixtures.

**Four guarded actual-OMP scenarios pass** through the new supervisor: text, tool routing and usage for three provider families, plus controller SIGKILL followed by the same host/OMP/session and a subsequent turn. All owned processes stop. Ten provider responses and three message-backend receipts are synthetic loopback fixtures; no live model or actual Mail delivery is claimed here.

## N5.3b2 — Native publication and hosted evidence (2026-10-03)

Native publication now creates durable push and PR intents from the active coordinator's exact validated candidate. Its evidence proof survives asynchronous Git and credential waits and is rechecked with current control and authority at dispatch. A shared record-mutation lane prevents a slow flush from overwriting a newer summary or successor. Concurrent identical prepares share one intent; a revised description clears stored readiness until its update is confirmed.

A flush registers every pending run before waiting for the first provider. Per-run epochs fence older pending work, cancellation of the caller does not abandon the operation, and close fences and drains the shared flight and checked transports together. Sending intents precede effects. Exact remote-head and PR-marker readback reconcile interruption; an unknown POST with no matching receipt never blindly creates another PR. Retryable rejection, blocked rejection and unknown outcome remain distinct. SQL failure propagates without being reported as provider success or a retryable provider error.

Readiness checks the current local candidate, remote head/base, PR identity and confirmed description, required statuses and exact PR pipeline steps. Older timestamp/build/run observations do not replace retained evidence. Confirmed regressions latch review invalidation; unavailable evidence alone does not. A fresh exact merged-PR receipt completes delivery during local or hosted outages. PR feedback observations remain independent of a held flush and preserve newer, partial and stale comment versions.

**37 native publication tests pass**: **20** service tests, **13** recovery tests and **4** successor/feedback scenarios. They include **four actual controller SIGKILL windows** before/after Git and PR effects, **three SQL-failure cases**, cancelled waiters, held-response shutdown, credential/control/evidence races, queued second-run Stop, concurrent prepares/flushes, foreign/ambiguous PR identities and a changed description/marker during hosted observation. Target refresh, captured feedback and cancelled retained work each produce fresh native candidates, actual isolated Docker checks and independent review artifacts before updating the same PR. The provider, credential and agent-identity sides are explicit fixtures; this is not live provider delivery.

[Machine-readable evidence](rust-native-publication-service-2026-10-03.json) records source/binary/log hashes and the validation selection.

The original compiler, Clippy and two successor-fixture failures are retained as failed development invocations. The fixture corrections distinguish automatic completion pauses from operator Pause intent and avoid releasing a writer twice. ## N5.3b1 — Native fresh targets and provider observers (2026-10-03)

Native remote operations register before awaiting work and continue after a caller is cancelled. Per-run epochs fence older pending work, each command directory has one checked Git owner, and Stop/close drain admitted work. Cleanup uncertainty remains an error on repeated close. Ordered target requests register their sequence before spawning and reuse their original authorization ticket for the inner fetch.

Fresh target requests use immutable request namespaces and recheck current control, candidate and ownership at actual Git dispatch. Historical request replay precedes provider access. Target observers read the configured remote itself; a moved target preserves the original branch and its explicit hold. Cancelled-work observers verify an open PR's marker, source head, branches and repository at queueing and admission without reacquiring repository locks. Configured serialized repository identity is kept separate from the client's normalized schema.

Ordinary target candidate association now saves its receipt and coordinator notice in one transaction. Startup recovers recorded work once and puts unresolved nonterminal recovery in attention-required state. Conflict promotion keeps its separate proof-producing transaction. No new target fetch is started by startup recovery.

**12 native target/observer scenarios pass**, including an **actual controller SIGKILL** during checked fetch, cancelled waiters, queued Stop, final-dispatch cancellation, a moved live target, concurrent request replay, normalized operator retries, an injected atomic notice failure and restart recovery, fresh PR checks, late changed receipts, HTTP close/drain and repeated unverified cleanup. Git, processes and SQLite are real; attempts, retained publication receipts, credentials and local HTTP replies are labelled fixtures.

## N5.3a — Native Jira synchronization and durable reporting (2026-10-03)

Native Jira synchronization gathers complete search pages and retained active issues before one import transaction. Failed, stale or incomplete observations preserve the last complete intake, accepted refinements and history. Source identity is checked separately from saved synchronization state. Shared refreshes continue after a waiter is cancelled; close drains admitted work and prevents another refresh.

Native Jira reporting authorizes the active reporter, or a research coordinator without one, and saves keyed intents independently of provider effects. Coding, PR-link and research text comes from the native evidence services. A proof of relevant durable facts is checked after asynchronous assessment and at final authorization; credentials are resolved before the last dispatch guard. Pauses, cancellation, close, merges and changed evidence prevent stale sends.

Comment marker/body and issue-status readback reconcile uncertain outcomes without blind retries. Controller review/close intents follow publication receipts, not agent activity. Merge supersedes unsent or unknown progress/review; separate research-revision lanes let a current verified outcome pass an older blocked summary. Close waits for driven flush work even if its original caller was cancelled.

**35 tests in 23 top-level native Jira scenarios pass**: 24 delivery tests (14 scenarios), four sync, four evidence/research, and three durability tests (one scenario with two fault windows). Evidence includes **two actual controller SIGKILL windows**, **two actual Docker checks**, three injected SQLite failure cases, actual managed commits, artifact tampering, a Git-observation pause race, credential-time controls/policy/merge changes and cancelled shared waiters. Agent identities and PR policy receipts are labelled fixture records. No live provider or credential was accessed.

## N5.2 — Native checked publication Git (2026-10-03)

Native publication Git now holds one OS lease per command directory, saves immutable checked descriptors/specifications before prepare and launch, and wraps actual dispatch in the caller's final authorization. Registered driven workers survive cancelled waiters. Close fences admission, stops verified owned children, drains workers and rechecks the complete journal before releasing its lease. Historical commands are never relaunched; missing old executables remain recoverable through exact retained identity, while unfamiliar or altered journals stay held.

Protected branches, exact expected-head leases, strict remote observations and request-owned target refs have actual disposable bare-Git evidence. Live helper reports must agree with their numeric exits and complete captures. Missing reports, capture failures, the actual 30-second deadline and output overflow remain unknown outcomes. The shared helper's new truncation markers are negative evidence only; they permit prompt single-stream overflow cleanup without rejecting the exact 128KiB combined boundary.

**14 native publication-Git scenarios pass**, including **four actual controller SIGKILL windows**, recovery-time authorization and close races, cancellation while a checked child remains live, missing helper outcome after a real remote effect, both combined and single-stream overflow, removed historical executable, altered journal/lease refusal, reopen and shutdown. One unprepared-journal case is explicitly synthetic. The exact-limit case uses synthetic Git output and asserts zero remote effect; neither is claimed as live provider delivery.

## N4.2b2c — Native cancelled retained-work transfer (2026-10-03)

Cancel remains terminal. Queueing a successor verifies the saved source and records intent; admission verifies it again before atomically transferring the existing branch and worktree. Keyed replay precedes physical or provider access. Dirty files, historical attempts and predecessor evidence remain intact. The successor uses its newly accepted brief and starts without inherited candidate/check/review evidence.

Historical Git intents remain under the source run. Live or uncertain ownership blocks transfer; unlaunched work is sealed. A published source requires fresh observer verification, and admission rechecks source, configuration, publication, control and dependencies after awaits. Pre-first-execution replay revalidates the source after restart. Repeated successors retain the same publication identity. Moved targets retain the accepted base until explicit refresh; dependency merges must already belong to that base.

**10 native retained-work scenarios pass**, including one actual controller SIGKILL with a surviving checked Git child, sealed unlaunched work, changed manifests, an atomic-transfer failure, five observer race variants, repeated cancellation and three moved-target variants. Provider callbacks and agent identities are scripted fixtures; Git, SQLite and checked processes are real.

## N4.2b2b — Native conflict completion and promotion (2026-10-03)

Native conflict completion binds the ready isolated revision to its source candidate and stopped resolver. Promotion records its exact checked reset before preparation, revalidates source after target observation, and launches only from paused stopped ownership. Recovery never relaunches a saved operation: verified stopped effects are adopted, unchanged originals are sealed and rejected, and ambiguous ownership stays held. A target failure after reset preserves the local result with operator attention.

Candidate lineage includes source and scoped resolver authors. The candidate, completion, refresh association and one target notice commit together; two forced database failures prove rollback before cleanup. Cleanup preserves changed branches and external or ignored files. It can resume with the worktree absent and its exact temporary branch still present. Retained process identities remain verifiable after historical working directories or executables disappear, while current launch paths and manifest/owner proofs remain strict. A target-refresh observer race now preserves a run that changes from paused to needs attention.

**10 native promotion scenarios pass**, including **four actual controller SIGKILL windows**, two atomic-write failures, four source/control races, unavailable/moved target observations, changed revision/source identities, corrupted process records, missing old executable recovery and branch-only cleanup. Agent identities and provider callbacks are fixtures; Git, SQLite and checked children are real.

## N4.2b2a — Native scoped conflicts and continuation (2026-10-03)

Native source tools now validate and use the isolated conflict worktree while pinned candidate reads retain their original revision. Conflict-scoped claims and stopped-writer restoration preserve their request identity and do not spend an automatic repair cycle. Edits are limited to recorded conflict paths with accepted instructions, requirements, original-branch ownership and pinned target checks. Ready workspaces reject further edits and assignments.

Checked staging and rebase continuation persist their exact identity before dispatch and settle only with a live child outcome, stopped ownership and valid source. Successive conflicts keep separate receipts; old keys cannot advance later state. Complete marker blocks are refused and plain separator text is retained. A lost parent, pause or unavailable outcome keeps the continuation unresolved. Externally completed rebases require explicit Resume with stopped ownership before adoption.

**11 native scenarios pass**, including two actual controller **SIGKILL** windows, pause during continuation, missing live outcome despite complete captures, successive-conflict replay, scoped/restored writers, source/target drift, instruction/detachment holds and explicit recovery. Read-only shell preparation names the isolated workspace. Agent identities and provider observations are fixtures; Git, SQLite and checked OS processes are real.

## N4.2b1 — Native target refresh and rollback (2026-10-03)

Native target refresh binds the exact stopped source candidate, records rebase intent before checked Git dispatch, and accepts a new base only with matching clean source scope, instructions, requirements and target observation. A failed or scope-dropping rebase restores the original before a conflict is retained. Isolated workspace preparation records ownership before add/rebase and leaves instruction changes or uncertain operations held for the operator. Refreshed candidate and target receipt association is atomic and replayable.

## N3.3a — Native external Mail and durable delivery (2026-10-03)

N3.3a passes after independent-host checkpoint `db8fe6a` was committed, pushed and verified. The native Mail client now couples external delivery to the accepted schema-26 message transactions. Registration binds one of 100 deterministic identities before the external call and uses the current attempt's resolved model. Send intent precedes transport; storage, acceptance, runner receipt and processing remain distinct. Lost replies retain the original key. After the retry window, only one exact inbox match confirms storage; absent or ambiguous outcomes stay uncertain without resending. Acceptance saves recoverable input before external acknowledgement.

**Eight transport scenarios** cover retained JSON/SSE results and private session headers, shared initialization failure/recovery, 64-call capacity, safe versus ambiguous retries, five-attempt bounds, Retry-After, total initialization/body/retry deadlines, redirects, malformed/uncorrelated/duplicate/oversized replies and credential redaction. The policy comparison covers all **100** names and **23** numeric/date delay inputs. Five added Rust unit tests cover endpoint/budget validation, retry safety, parsing and safe integer/name rules.

**Ten native-store scenarios** cover send/ack ordering and reopen, lost receipt and expired-key matching, unknown/ambiguous outcomes, strict transport receipts, a stale lookup racing a confirmed send, observer throttle/single-flight/fairness/deadline/backlog/byte limits, number canonicalization and rollback of failed page transactions. They verify retired indices, current-attempt fallback, lost registration reply replay and identity exhaustion. Network awaits leave the store responsive. Observation persists whole pages and cursors without consuming unread messages or acknowledging them.

The guarded **actual Mail0.3.36** fixture runs five native-process **SIGKILL receive windows**: before input persistence, after persistence, before acknowledgement, after acknowledgement and after processing. Reopen and repeated keyed recovery produce one saved/external reply effect in each case. The same run verifies passive receipt state, lost send response, expired local retry retention, absent-send uncertainty, service downtime and two Mail restarts. All native, worker and three Mail-generation processes are gone; sandbox allow/deny probes pass. Assignment records and fault responses are fixtures; no model/Jira/Bitbucket delivery or supervisor/host dispatch is claimed here.

## N3.2b — Native contained commands and receipt-preserving cleanup (2026-10-03)

N3.2b passes after N3.2a was committed and pushed as `a2185fa` (remote HEAD verified). Rust now owns Docker command admission, create/start reconciliation, inspection, wait/timeout, stop, final/live logs, execution receipts and removal through the configured local Unix socket. The production boundary uses no Node/Python helper or Docker CLI. Native workflow dispatch remains fenced until full host, supervision and repository/evidence integration passes.

**Eight real-Docker scenarios** pass: the four retained containment cases and independent cleanup-controller case, plus live-log cancellation, explicit temporary execution/resource settings, and current-format Rust/TypeScript journal adoption in both directions. A detached child with closed descriptors and an empty environment stops with its container. Engine loss stays uncertain and cannot admit the same command twice. Natural completion, timeout and interruption retain different receipts; deletion preserves exact final output and identity. A separate cleaner survives controller loss, refuses foreign PID/label/path identities and reconciles a lost deletion reply. The later supervisor startup-cleanup case remains an explicit N3.3 integration gate.

**14 Rust tests** cover multiplex framing and thirteen Unix-socket fault fixtures. They exercise concurrent admission, cancellation during create/start, a start still in flight after the engine reports running, lost create/start/delete replies, altered containment, copied/symlinked journals, malformed flags, invalid specs, redirected engine replies, bounded/truncated final logs and observation failure. Completion rechecks the engine after final logs and uses current durable flags before saving a receipt. These scripted engine faults are distinct from actual Docker tests.

The [Docker API 1.54 contract](https://docs.docker.com/reference/api/engine/version/v1.54/) supplies operation statuses and eight-byte multiplex framing. Rust writes intent before any engine effect and holds an OS launch lease until create/start settlement. Stop can save its request while the engine is slow, but cannot certify stopped ownership while launch could still dispatch. Atomic private journal writes retain identity across restarts; exact root/label/ID/image, mount and containment checks precede cleanup. Final output is bounded at four MiB and incomplete or oversized logs cannot become a passing receipt. Live observation is independently cancellable and never proves completion.

The first real run exposed Docker’s omitted `ReadOnly: false` field: the strict native check rejected three writable fixtures while the read-only case passed. The corrected check accepts the omitted false default and rejects malformed values. All three leftover fixture containers were independently matched by full ID/name/image/configuration, stopped, logged and removed by exact ID; their cleanup receipts and the original failure remain retained. Clippy found two style issues before the frozen run. No broad cleanup or image pull occurred.

## N3.2a — Native provider gateway, usage and input budgets (2026-10-02)

N3.2a passes after N3.1b1 was committed and pushed as `854be93` (remote HEAD verified). The Rust gateway authenticates local requests, fixes provider routes and model/effort identity, keeps upstream credentials private, enforces conservative input budgets and proxies JSON/SSE with cancellation and bounded backpressure. Reported token usage stays distinct from unavailable accounting. Opaque reasoning bounds persist as hashes and counts across gateway replacement. Native workflow dispatch remains fenced pending full host, supervision and repository/evidence validation.

**1,568 direct TypeScript comparisons** pass: 396 input-protocol cases, 1,158 usage/outcome cases and 14 durable-budget cases. These cover split UTF-8/SSE bytes, invalid/truncated decoding, provider completion, actual versus unavailable usage, opaque callbacks, configured input positions, budget reopen and parser caps. **Five retained gateway scenarios** run against Rust through a test-only HTTP proxy. **Nine direct Rust HTTP tests** independently cover route/auth/query isolation, redirect refusal, policy races/reopen, exact split request bytes, failed budget storage, status taxonomy, deadlines, cancellation, stalled readers and client disconnect. One receipt survives each request termination; interrupted authorization does not become a retryable transport error.

**Four guarded actual-OMP scenarios** pass with OMP 18.4.12 and its recorded binary/addon hashes. OpenAI, Anthropic and Gemini each complete two turns through the native gateway with synthetic reported usage; the OpenAI second turn uses a remembered opaque reasoning bound. A fourth runner attempts an unadmitted effort substitution after a scripted provider error and the gateway refuses it before a second upstream request. All four runners and gateways stop; seven synthetic provider requests are recorded. This is interface validation, not live provider delivery or model-quality evidence.

Compression was an additional transport requirement: [Node 26's fetch implementation](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v26.10.0/deps/undici/src/lib/web/fetch/index.js) decodes content codings before the retained gateway sees bytes. **21 compression cases** now cover JSON/SSE with gzip/x-gzip, wrapped/raw deflate, Brotli, Zstandard, chained/concatenated bodies and unknown-coding pass-through, plus malformed, empty and excessive-coding responses. Native decompression is bounded by streaming readers and the same cancellation/deadline. Invalid compressed streams fail closed. The initial malformed-body fixture assumed headers would arrive first; the corrected assertion accepts transport rejection at either fetch or body consumption and still requires the disconnect record.

The port also corrects two conservative-admission hazards: split valid request UTF-8 is decoded only after reassembly, and in-memory reasoning bounds change only after durable persistence. A failed budget write retains actual usage but records a nonretryable storage failure and cannot authorize unknown replay. Invalid UTF-8 and unsafe saved counts are refused. The 8 MiB JSON receipt cap is applied before allocation; the retained 1 MiB incomplete SSE-line cap remains. Full-host ownership will provide the single active writer for each reasoning ledger.

## N3.1b1 — Native OMP transport, model preflight and observations (2026-10-02)

N3.1b1 passes after N3.1a was committed and pushed as `ad6f6f4` (remote HEAD verified). Rust now owns correlated OMP RPC requests, protocol-v2 negotiation and complete bounded chunk reassembly, exact assignment selection/configuration/readback, isolated model settings, progress coalescing and readable pane rendering. Native service dispatch remains fenced. This is not the production independent host or supervision cutover.

The installed **OMP 18.4.12** is revalidated with its binary and native-addon hashes. **12 guarded scenarios** pass across six real OMP processes and eight synthetic OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini requests: exact model/effort admission and current readback, same-model 429 retry, hard 403 failure, three unsupported declared efforts, three stale declarations rejected by current runner capabilities, and a **1,200,000-byte** host-tool exchange. Every owned process is confirmed stopped. The OS guard proves the fixture port is allowed while external, other-loopback and child egress are denied. No live provider action or credential access is claimed.

The [versioned framing contract](https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/v18.4.12/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-frame.ts) requires negotiation because protocol v1 may shorten large events. Rust bounds physical frames at 1 MiB, reassembled frames and commands at 64 MiB, rejects incomplete/corrupt/interleaved chunks and explicit frame errors, and sends input as plain JSONL. Scripted real-process tests cover correlation, duplicate pending IDs, a real 20-second timeout, cancellation, partial-write poisoning, late-response fencing and verified shutdown after cancelled negotiation. Unresolved IDs cannot be reused before their response arrives; a generation token prevents stale cleanup from removing a newer registration. The pending set is bounded at 1024. The JSON parser retains its default nesting limit and fails closed.

Two actual-runner discoveries are retained. The [output writer](https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/v18.4.12/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-output.ts) spools under backpressure: the first fixture denied its system-temp write, so the corrected fixture gives each attempt a private writable TMPDIR without weakening the guard. The next fixture incorrectly assumed large results remain fully model-visible. OMP deliberately retains the complete 1.2 MB result artifact and sends a 40,985-byte truncated view with its artifact reference. The final check verifies both the artifact bytes and exact provider projection independently. The host implementation must preserve this distinction and provide private temporary storage.

**621 direct model-policy comparisons** cover selection, configured fallback, identity, isolated settings and all seven configuration RPC steps, including early failures and zero-level/off admission. **12,590 observation/pane comparisons** cover raw equivalent numeric spellings, bounded activity, full completed events and rendering. Core progress projection now accepts `1.0` and `-0.0` as safe integer indexes. Native text truncation keeps complete Unicode scalars within UTF-16 budgets; presentation remains independent of workflow/evidence gates.

## N2.7c — Source dates, serialized identities and store closure (2026-10-02)

N2.7c passes its frozen gates after N2.7b was committed and pushed as `69089dc` (remote HEAD verified). **N2 schema-26 durable transaction parity is accepted within the recorded boundaries.** Native execution and release cutover remain unavailable pending N3–N7. Full historical schema upgrades remain N7.1; controller change-listener wiring remains N3.3.

The Rust source-date parser covers the retained partial/expanded ISO dates, calendar rollover, local and explicit offsets, fractional seconds, legacy month/time-zone forms and invalid inputs. It follows the [pinned V8 parser](https://github.com/v8/v8/blob/e3ddb93205db9db66565447a4eee2143dfe72710/src/date/dateparser-inl.h), with the BSD license retained in `crates/crewroom-core/licenses/V8-LICENSE`. The **51,516** comparisons cover **8,586** dates in six separate timezone processes, including repeated hours, gaps, skipped days and expanded-year limits. macOS mktime rejected old local years in development; localtime_r offset probes now round-trip the chosen instant. Local offsets still use the host timezone database, so this is evidence for the recorded host/runtime versions. The service gains no JavaScript runtime dependency, and source date text stays unchanged in storage.

Captured JSON identities and hashes now use ECMAScript Number formatting and numeric property ordering while preserving ordinary key insertion order. The pinned pure-Rust `ryu-js` 1.0.3 formatter supports **6,414** exact-byte comparisons, including raw decimal/negative-zero forms, exponent thresholds, finite IEEE-754 values and nested numeric keys. Queue, notice, learning, team, retained-work and feedback identities use the shared serializer; feedback capture no longer substitutes structural equality for serialized identity. Native JSON remains Unicode-scalar data; JavaScript-only values and lone UTF-16 surrogates are outside this boundary.

The **65** closure comparisons cover atomic Jira stale-date rejection, comment ordering/currentness, notice replay, filtered event reads, captured configured-check IDs, exact owned paths, insertion refusal order, coding-slot availability, dependency deferral and trusted learning records. - The new `scripts/rust/verify-attempts.ts` passes **143** operation comparisons across **25** schema-26 tables. Rust opens a fresh process/store for every operation; TypeScript reopens at seed and finish. Each successful/refused/replayed operation compares saved state. The provider refusal matrix covers stale or missing identities, failed required work, unconfigured steps, status branch mismatch and optional skipped steps.
- The oracle maps only newly generated UUIDs; fixture/request IDs and timestamps remain exact. It retains the N2.1 exclusion of incidental derived `holdReason` values in run rows/results. Streaming progress uses the host's UTF-16 length budget but keeps complete Unicode scalars at a truncation boundary; final message content is unchanged.
- Workspace build, example build, formatting and Clippy with warnings denied pass. Intake compatibility **16** and workflow compatibility **31** pass. Retained `npm test` passes **332/332** in 588 seconds, `npm run build` passes, and `npm run test:e2e` passes **31/31** in one invocation. No focused retry was needed.

The initial compile rejected unsupported unsigned SQLite conversions; bounded values now use SQLite's signed integer representation. Oracle setup failures corrected a missing research request ID and the documented derived-hold normalization. Inspection found and fixed Unicode progress limits before freezing the candidate. These development failures are not passing validation receipts.

This is store-only parity for the assignment/event boundary. PIDs, host identities, messages, repository ownership and publication observations are fixtures. No native process execution, model, Mail dispatch, container command or provider delivery is certified. Native Start/Resume remain unavailable. The [154-operation inventory](../native-workbench-operation-map.md) adds explicit N2.6 repository ownership/target-recovery transaction work; N2 and N3–N7 remain incomplete. N2.3 launch/slot scheduling is next after this iteration is committed/pushed.

## N2.1 — Durable dependencies, plans and requirements adoption (2026-10-02)

The first N2 slice is validated on `codex/rust-gpui`. **N2 remains incomplete.** This slice ports local dependency decisions and delivery projections, Explore/Grill/research plans, read-only research target pinning, explicit requirements adoption, safe refinement closure, queue-position normalization and terminal steering settlement. The authenticated Rust API exposes these operations; native editor controls remain N6 work.

Adoption keeps historical assignment briefs, flags changed/removed criterion indices, rejects outdated operator insertion proposals and updates their pending notices, changes the repository requirements hash, and requests a safe pause plus pending steering for active-state records. An accepted edit alone does not rewrite a run. Reopening completed research requires an explicit adoption decision and no other open run; its prior evidence remains historical. The service still reports execution unavailable and rejects Resume/Start.

The frozen source fingerprint is `7a25a3ca73a462c96198dd73842f591af8f7836c8ae8c004ee48c5590de71af4` at baseline `e91d25894b3eae8890344fa87ef5682557f87b1d`. The [machine-readable evidence](rust-native-workflow-2026-10-02.json) retains all source and log hashes. Logs are in `.crewroom/test-results/n21-20261002/`; all nine command receipts confirm unchanged source. Executed checks:

- Rust workspace tests: **19/19**, including seven new workflow tests and the unchanged **4,536** lifecycle comparisons. A real SQLite trigger aborts the run write after an assignment edit; the transaction rolls both back. Other cases cover concurrent connections, replay/reopen, dependency cycles, target pinning without checkout mutation, uncertain ownership, terminal/merged adoption guards and research reopening exclusion.
- Workspace build, formatting and Clippy with warnings denied pass.
- Retained implementation: `npm test` passes **332/332** in 719 seconds, `npm run build` passes, and `npm run test:e2e` passes **31/31** in one invocation. No focused retry is needed for this candidate.
- The original intake compatibility script passes **16** cases. `scripts/rust/verify-workflow.ts` passes **31** operation steps through a separate Rust daemon and the TypeScript oracle, comparing 15 tables after each step, followed by daemon restart and TypeScript reopen. It checks immutable briefs, rebrief flags, insertion rejection/notices, requirements hashes, source reacceptance, queue compaction and stored-versus-undelivered steering.

The oracle normalizes newly allocated UUIDs and timestamps; fixed fixture/request identities remain exact. It excludes only the derived `runs.data.holdReason` field: TypeScript incidentally persists that view during queue compaction, while Rust recomputes it and also exposes the unavailable runner. Requirement-change holds and dependency-state projections are checked separately. The initial verifier used unsupported insertion fixture fields; correcting it did not change production behavior.

These are real local SQLite/Git/HTTP/daemon checks with seeded assignment and provider records. No OMP host, live model, Jira, Bitbucket or publication delivery is exercised by the Rust slice. No native screen journey is newly certified. N3 supervision, N4 evidence execution, N5 delivery and N6 UI parity remain required. Next in N2: durable launch/slot scheduling and immutable assignment/attempt transactions, followed by the remaining proposal, research, insertion, cancellation-successor and provider-intent records.

## N1 — Rust native intake and service foundation (2026-10-02)

The operator selected a Rust port of both the UI and workflow service. The first migration slice now runs as a GPUI Kit 0.7.0 application and a separate Rust `crewroomd` process, with a SQLite core. [ADR 0009](../adr/0009-rust-native-workbench-and-service.md) records the changed runtime contract; [the migration plan](../native-migration.md) lists the remaining N2–N7 work. This evidence accepts the N1 foundation only. The complete workflow service, native operator flows and cutover remain incomplete.

Implemented and exercised: local and snapshot Jira intake; source/local-field separation; optimistic accepted briefs and immutable history; validated repository selection; immutable queued plans; idempotent pending steering, reorder and removal; user role revisions; authenticated loopback HTTP/WebSocket; controller exclusion across runtimes; graceful shutdown and token rotation; and retained native drafts. Unsupported runner and delivery operations are explicitly unavailable. The Rust runtime does not launch Node or evaluate TypeScript. The 0.1.0 implementation remains available.

The [machine-readable evidence](rust-native-intake-2026-10-02.json) identifies baseline `e91d25894b3eae8890344fa87ef5682557f87b1d`, branch `codex/rust-gpui`, and exact working-tree source hashes. Checks cover:

- `cargo test --workspace --locked`: 12 tests pass, including a 4,536-case lifecycle corpus generated from the original TypeScript rules. Real HTTP/WebSocket and detached-daemon tests verify the origin/token boundary, push updates, loopback binding, shutdown with a live observer, launcher independence, persistence and reopening. A native headless test types Unicode, navigates away/back, preserves a draft through a stale push, opens the Command-Q confirmation, and keeps editing.
- `cargo build --workspace --locked`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and Clippy with warnings denied pass. The development app is ad-hoc signed and verified. `block 0.1.6` emits a dependency future-incompatibility notice; this is not a current build failure. Distribution signing and notarization are unverified.
- `scripts/rust/verify-compatibility.ts`: 16 cross-runtime cases pass against a real Rust process and the original Workbench. Coverage includes both directions of schema-26 persistence, Unicode/source/refinement/history/notes, queue and steering replay, reorder/removal receipts, pending-message settlement, repository create/retire receipt replay after removal of the canonical checkout, controller exclusion and SIGTERM ownership release.
- Native macOS inspection creates and saves an isolated ticket, navigates Roles/back without losing edits, closes the GUI while the daemon stays alive, restarts the daemon, observes automatic reconnection, reopens the saved Unicode brief, and verifies both unsaved-close actions. Screens and accessibility controls were inspected; full IME, VoiceOver, terminal output and all 31 native workflow journeys are not certified by this check.
- Retained implementation: `npm test` 332/332 and `npm run build` pass. The full browser invocation passes 30/31; the known feedback journey hits its 30-second timeout. Its unchanged focused rerun passes 1/1. No single 31/31 invocation is claimed. Both logs retain a `ghostty-web` bounds diagnostic at `src/terminal.tsx:39`; it remains outside the native intake acceptance.

Validation found and repaired macOS parent-symlink handling for SQLite, WebSocket-held shutdown ownership, daemon lifetime after launcher exit, the native close-dialog footer/keyboard route, and repository receipt shape compatibility. These failures were not treated as passing evidence. Fixture repairs used the actual API route names and canonical temporary paths. No live Jira, Bitbucket or model delivery was run for N1. The retained pilot and retired installation were not used.

## M2.1 — Strict assignment preflight

Complete, 2026-09-27. Explicit role/default resolution, project allowlists, required input/tool capabilities, opt-in fallback targets and requested/resolved identity are implemented in `server/runtime/model-policy.ts`. Unknown tool support fails admission when tools are required. Role configuration supports explicit OMP effort tiers. Preflight checks the current runner catalog and available effort levels, applies the exact selection, disables automatic compaction/session retries, and rejects mismatching readback. Auxiliary chat roles are pinned in private settings; advisor, prewalk, recap, memory, autolearn, automatic thinking keywords, context promotion and fallback chains are disabled. The host's enforced launch/configuration isolation is integrated in M2.2.

Validation: `npm test` 24/24 and `npm run build` pass. `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH python3 scripts/test-model-preflight.py --output <new-owned-directory>` exercises the production TypeScript module through a test RPC bridge. [The sanitized report](model-preflight-2026-09-27.json) records 11 successful assertions: three admitted model/API/effort identities with actual scripted turns, same-model 429 recovery, hard 403 failure, three unsupported effort rejections before launch and the same three rejected against current OMP metadata despite stale optimistic declarations. Six guarded OMP processes exited; six scripted provider requests, no unexpected requests.

Diagnosis preserved: an initial assertion assumed disabling session retries also disabled HTTP retries. The 18.3.1 [HTTP transport](https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/v18.3.1/packages/ai/src/utils/openai-http.ts) has its own bounded retries (six attempts at that layer). That test failed at its 15-second deadline and then exhausted its one scripted error response; it did not demonstrate a model substitution. The corrected test verifies recovery on the same model and immediate hard-denial failure. Session-level automatic fallback stays disabled. M2.4 must enforce overall active-time limits; this report does not claim one HTTP request per turn under transport faults.

Sources: exact [18.3.1 settings](https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/v18.3.1/docs/settings.md) and [RPC types](https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi/blob/v18.3.1/packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-types.ts). The separately installed 17.4.0 source package is not the running binary's authority. No operator model configuration was modified.

## M2.2 — Application hosts and external messages

Complete, 2026-09-27. Crewroom now admits immutable assignments and inputs, starts private OMP RPC hosts in its dedicated tmux session, verifies process/session/model identities, and records structured activity in SQLite. The Rust Mail HTTP MCP adapter binds senders to authenticated attempts, validates recipient scope, persists logical IDs before sending, preserves causal replies, and saves accepted input before mailbox acknowledgement. Passive observations use independent durable cursors. After retry-key expiry, a confirmed matching stored message is adopted; an unknown outcome blocks a resend. Credentials and isolated runner configuration remain server-side.

Validation: `npm test` 26/26, `npm run build` passes, `npm run test:e2e` 2/2. `scripts/test-application-runtime.py` drives the real HTTP API, three actual tmux/OMP hosts, all three scripted API families, and Rust Mail through four causal hops. Nine provider requests, no unexpected requests, distinct host/OMP/session identities, visible pane events, rejected forged host credentials, and no surviving test-owned processes. `scripts/test-application-mail.py` verifies reopening, non-consuming observation, lost send response reconciliation beyond retry retention, and blocking an expired unknown send. [Sanitized results and source hashes](application-runtime-2026-09-27.json).

The runtime can be supplied through a private `CREWROOM_RUNTIME_CONFIG` file. This is a tested messaging integration, not coding admission: safe child-tool pause and crash ownership are M2.3; automatic continuity and browser controls are M2.4–M2.5. No live model, Jira, or Bitbucket calls occurred.

## M2.3 — Safe interruption and ownership

Complete, 2026-09-27. Pause closes admission before stopping work, retains pausing until proof is available, and records attention-required when ownership is uncertain. The backend holds an exclusive OS lock; private host and OMP launch records combine process identity and inherited locks. Restart adopts a matching surviving host/session or reconciles and stops old work before another launch. A dead OMP process alone no longer marks the attempt fully stopped. Legacy attempts without ownership records remain unresolved.

The original native command design failed the detached-child test: a child started with a new session and closed descriptors survived while the process group appeared empty. Do not reinterpret that failure as success. The command boundary was replaced with a per-command Docker container, durable create/start identity, scoped workspace mount, no network, pinned image, and backend-only local engine access. Container termination stops the process namespace, including children that detach and clear their environments. Missing engine control or uncertain start outcomes block replacement. [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-contain-repository-commands.md) records the trade-off; the pilot repository needs a configured Linux check image during onboarding. The native lock helper is limited to the known host/runner topology.

Validation: `npm test` 30/30; build passes; browser journeys 2/2. `node --import tsx --test tests/container.integration.ts` passes two real engine tests, including the detached child and unavailable engine. The HTTP/OMP interruption harness passes pause, host SIGKILL, dedicated tmux loss, supervisor SIGKILL with surviving host adoption, detached tool child, and combined supervisor/host loss. Each uses an actual OMP turn and actual container command; no live model/API calls. The final combined-loss run independently finds no surviving test-owned host/OMP processes and removes its confirmed-stopped container. The three-process/four-hop messaging regression still passes (nine requests, no unexpected calls or survivors). [Results and source hashes](interruption-2026-09-27.json).

Reproduce each fault with `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH python3 scripts/test-application-interruption.py --fault <pause|host|tmux|supervisor|supervisor-host|detached> --output <new-owned-directory>`. The fixture uses a pre-existing pinned Python image and no pulls. M2.4 remains responsible for session continuation, recorded replacement attempts, budgets, and complete control semantics; M2.3 does not certify coding delivery or automatic recovery.

## M2.1 follow-up — Provider transport policy

Before M2.4, an additional real-OMP fault exposed a lower-level effort fallback missed by preflight: after a scripted HTTP 400 rejecting `low` and advertising `medium`, OMP sent a second request at `medium` while session configuration still identified the admitted effort. The failed characterization is retained in `.crewroom/test-results/m2-model-guard-red/`.

Fixed at the actual provider boundary. Each host now sends requests through its authenticated local gateway to the configured upstream. It rejects a different model, enforces the explicit OpenAI effort, and pins the serialized reasoning configuration from the verified session for the other API families. Provider credentials stay in the host; OMP receives a per-attempt gateway token. An unsupported transport substitution returns a hard failure and a recorded policy-rejection event before another upstream model call. This is a request-policy check, not a claim about the provider's internal reasoning.

Validation: the same real-OMP fault now makes exactly one upstream request and records the blocked `medium` attempt. The three-provider/four-hop exchange passes through the gateway (nine requests), the supervisor-restart/container-pause regression passes, all 30 domain tests pass, and the build passes. The first gateway regression exposed Anthropic SDK path handling; preserving the upstream base path fixed it before completion. [Sanitized report](provider-policy-2026-09-27.json). M2.4 begins only after these regressions pass.

## M2.4 — Continuity and limits

Complete, 2026-09-27. The application now records replacement generations, retains original assignment inputs, and restores usable OMP sessions from a stable assignment working directory. The initial restore test failed because per-attempt working directories caused OMP to refuse a cross-directory session switch; the refusal remains recorded, and the corrected test verifies the same session ID, a different process, and the saved checkpoint in the actual provider request. An unfinished first turn instead produces a recorded fresh session from durable input.

Automatic recovery stops and reconciles the whole admitted team before restarting it; only the failed assignment consumes a recovery cycle. Two automatic cycles are allowed. Exhaustion, model-policy substitution, uncertain ownership, and unresolved delivery effects require attention. Peer storage reconciliation retains the original transport key after the sender exits. Host input journals correlate durable input IDs with actual OMP prompt results; identical concurrent delivery shares one prompt. Replacement context includes received peer messages, steering, input history and tool progress. Completed and unfinished work remain distinct.

The active-time clock persists its accumulated duration and current interval. Confirmed pauses exclude elapsed time, while stopping and unresolved ownership continue counting. Budget exhaustion requests a safe pause; an explicit extension retains prior usage. Cancel ends execution and preserves the work. The control revision ensures that a newer cancel wins over an in-progress resume. Busy steering uses the tested OMP prompt/steer contract; received does not become applied until the coordinator provides a scoped disposition with a reason.

Focused checks have passed for session restoration, first-turn recovery, two-cycle exhaustion, busy steering, cancellation during resume, supervisor loss after input delivery, concurrent identical input, heterogeneous peer recovery, retained-mail reconciliation, and provider-policy stop. The final gate passes 11 application scenarios, 35 domain tests, build, and two browser journeys. The first combined run stopped at an overlong tmux socket path; a new admission check rejects that configuration before claiming a process. The affected and remaining cases passed from the shorter `.crewroom/test-results/m2final/` path. [Sanitized results and source hashes](continuity-2026-09-27.json). M2.5 is next. No live model, Jira or Bitbucket calls occurred.

Browser polling reopens saved observations; connection loss is explicit and retains an unsent steering draft. There is no direct specialist steering surface.

## M3.1 — Assignment coordination and user roles

Complete, 2026-09-27. The coordinator can inspect available roles and propose only the specialists needed. The workflow service validates identity, capacity, role availability and configured model policy, persists immutable assignment inputs, and inserts the team after delivery reconciliation and confirmed process termination. A durable transition intent resumes a team change after controller loss, including the boundary after assignments were saved. Proposed specialists remain visibly pending until application. The browser supports role creation, editing and persistence.

No live model, Jira or Bitbucket requests occurred.

## M3.2 — Isolated repository ownership

Complete, 2026-09-27. Repository admission persists the selected target, branch, pinned start/base and worktree destination before Git writes. It preserves the operator checkout, adopts an already-created matching worktree after a missing receipt, and rejects competing branch ownership. Explicit continuation requires a local branch and its observed head; remote PR lookup remains M4. Project instructions and accepted requirements are retained. Repository observations distinguish target, head, working-content, requirements and instruction changes. Interruption checkpoints retain edits and commits without resetting or cleaning them.

Writer ownership is transactional and remains held until the original attempt's process/command termination is proven. The storage admission boundary now also rejects another run while any earlier attempt has unresolved ownership. The supervisor passes recorded project rules to OMP, blocks launch on unreconciled drift, and checkpoints/releases a stopped writer during pause. Generic fixture commands are excluded from an owned coding worktree; M3.3 supplies scoped editing/check tools. Git calls disable hooks, filesystem monitors and effective content filters, including filters inherited from included configuration files. Edited Git pointers and unsafe branch selections fail closed.

Validation: 48 domain tests, build, and three browser journeys pass. Real Git tests cover dirty operator checkout preservation, existing-branch continuation, missing mutation receipt, restart-retained edits/commits, exclusive/replacement writers, unresolved competing runs, independent drift facts and hook/filter suppression. A guarded three-process OMP exchange then claims a writer through the public service boundary, stages a fixture edit, pauses actual agents, and verifies that the writer was released only after stop proof with the edit retained. Nine scripted provider requests, no unexpected calls or survivors. The complex team regression also passes (thirteen requests); its reporter fixture now edits the newly available default role rather than trying to create a duplicate. [Sanitized results and source hashes](repository-2026-09-27.json).

The first filter test lacked a valid positive control. After confirming that the disposable filter actually created its marker, the test exposed included configuration bypassing local-only filter enumeration. Effective configuration enumeration fixed that failure. This gate does not claim agent-generated edits, executed repository checks, or independent review; those are the next item, M3.3. Prepared repositories containing unsupported submodules or non-regular/oversized observed files stop with an explicit error rather than receive incomplete revision observations.

## M3.3 — Coding execution and revision evidence

Complete, 2026-09-27. Coordinator grants now lead through scoped compare-and-set edits, a confirmed process stop, one candidate commit, actual contained repository checks, explicit independent review, and at most two automatic repairs. Required check records include command, pinned image/platform, start/completion/timeout/exit facts, producing attempt, exact candidate, source manifest, and hashed output. Review records identify assignment/session, candidate, findings and criterion coverage. Missing, failed, interrupted, tampered, self-authored or stale evidence cannot establish readiness. Agents cannot submit command exit results through the review or check-finish API.

The production OMP tools use the same coding, repository and evidence services as the HTTP workbench. Each candidate retains its base/head/tree and requirements/instruction hashes. Review reads the assigned revision. Completion waits for all active processes and mail to settle, stops the team, commits, and resumes from durable inputs. Completed check receipts and file edits are reconciled before replacement; an interrupted writer receives its retained grant before resuming. Automatic repair keeps the logical implementation budget even if another specialist takes over. Completed specialists can retire at a safe point; their identities, messages and evidence remain, while active slots become available. The verified Rust Mail adapter has 100 distinct lifetime identities per run and four active agents, with an explicit error at exhaustion.

Validation: **57 domain tests, build, three browser journeys and four real-container integration tests pass.** Eight guarded application cases pass: actual failing unit test → repair → independent review rejection → repair → readiness (51 scripted provider requests); writer SIGKILL after a retained edit (22); pause/steer/resume (23); backend crashes before commit, after commit with no receipt, and after completion receipt (19 each); specialist insertion during coding (24); and five historical identities sharing four active slots (18). Every case used real OMP processes and Rust Mail, denied external model/network access, and left no test-owned host/OMP processes running. Container tests verify stopped ownership and remove their owned commands. [Sanitized results and source hashes](coding-2026-09-27.json).

The interruption test first failed because replacement input arrived without renewed writer ownership. The runtime now restores the retained grant before dispatch. Lost command receipts use a stable evidence identity, and replay adopts the existing command rather than executing another. A crash-fixture timing failure froze an open SQLite transaction; the fixture now allows that transaction to complete before staging its durable boundary. Failed runs remain in ignored local evidence rather than being relabeled passed.

This is the local portion of E2E-01 and E2E-03. Scripted review validates orchestration; live quality remains M5. No live model, Jira, Bitbucket or the pilot repository changes occurred. Continue with **M3.4**, the result/evidence view and usage provenance.

## M3.4 — Change and evidence view

Complete, 2026-09-27. The browser presents local readiness independently of workflow and process activity, exact base/commit/tree identity, a bounded diff, actual check command/environment/exit/output, hashed artifacts, independent review findings and criterion coverage. The candidate selector retains failed revisions and earlier findings. Artifact requests are scoped to the selected run and verify stored hashes. Changed code removes current readiness; connection failure removes the current-readiness badge until observations refresh. Publication remains explicitly unavailable.

Usage comes from retained OMP assistant response records, with duplicate observations excluded. Each assignment shows available token totals and runner cost estimates with the number of responses covered. Missing values remain unavailable, partial coverage stays visible, and provider billing is not inferred from OMP estimates. Desktop and mobile screenshots were visually inspected.

It made 51 expected scripted provider requests and left no test-owned host/OMP processes. [Results and source hashes](results-view-2026-09-27.json).

## M4.2 — Jira synchronization complete

The accepted saved-filter source now uses Jira enhanced search with complete cursor pagination, Rank order, stable site/issue identity, and separate local refinements. Incomplete, stale and invalid refreshes retain the prior complete state. Explicitly admitted tickets remain synchronized even after leaving the filter. Import does not queue or start anything. The loopback server reads the existing ACLI OAuth Keychain account; `.crewroom/jira.json` contains only source/account/transition metadata. `CREWROOM_JIRA_CONFIG` can select another metadata file. Manual refresh and a 60-second refresh interval use the same service.

An independently hosted delivery reporter can request configured In Progress, milestone/blocker comments, and controller-composed revision evidence. Durable intents precede dispatch. Known unsent writes remain pending; lost replies remain uncertain and are reconciled by reading matching markers. Restart adopts the existing comment/status receipt without a duplicate. Later updates retain their order behind an unresolved one. Required-check/review evidence is validated again before sending; agents cannot supply a replacement requirement description, arbitrary transition, or invented evidence URL. Externally accessible PR/evidence links follow in M4.3.

Changed source requirements preserve the local brief and accepted run snapshot, stop affected agent processes, and block stale continuation. This is the conflict-hold contract from INT-05. Explicit scope acceptance and reconstruction of affected evidence remain M6.3; saving a brief alone does not authorize old assignments to continue. Rejected permissions and inconclusive write outcomes remain visible for recovery rather than being silently cleared.

Validation: **66 domain tests, build, four Playwright journeys, and a fresh guarded runtime/browser test pass**. The runtime test uses two real OMP processes and distinct sessions, two Rust Mail receipts, seven scripted model requests and one remotely accepted comment. Its response is lost, the server restarts, and the existing comment is adopted. A requirements refresh stops both processes. No test-owned processes survive. Real model/network access is denied by the fixture guard.

## M4.3 — PR publication and hosted validation complete

The active coordinator can persist a publication request for the current locally validated candidate. Backend-owned Git commands verify repository identity, use an explicit remote/ref lease, refuse protected targets and retain process ownership. Push and PR receipts remain separate. A lost PR response is reconciled by its durable marker; an inconclusive empty lookup never causes a second create. Repository, source or target drift blocks publication or readiness. PR content includes accepted criteria, candidate/base identities and local check hashes. The independent delivery reporter can post the verified PR link and current candidate evidence to Jira.

The workbench shows created PRs separately from ready-for-review results. Readiness requires the current local evidence, exact remote/PR head and base, required commit-status identities, and successful required steps from the latest matching PR pipeline. Optional skipped steps remain visibly skipped. Older observations cannot overwrite newer failures. Merge remains an operator action.

Validation: **72 domain tests, build, five Playwright journeys and a fresh guarded two-process Jira reporter/restart test pass.** A new regression test caught source drift between confirmed push and PR creation; the fix passes. The browser test now waits for the real asynchronous reconciliation response before inspecting its result. [Evidence and source hashes](publication-2026-09-28.json).

The provider fixtures use real HTTP, SQLite and local Git. The focused PR fixture uses labeled controller evidence receipts; the full real OMP/container delivery path is **M4.4**, the next sequential item. No live provider mutation or the pilot repository change occurred. Direct server access to the existing Bitbucket OAuth Keychain item timed out and remains unverified; the CLI's successful read access does not prove server access. Production configuration accepts metadata separately from server-only credentials. The direct REST transport avoids the method-independent mutation retries found by source inspection in [bkt 0.26.6](https://github.com/avivsinai/bitbucket-cli/blob/v0.26.6/pkg/httpx/client.go). Immutable publication is currently one candidate per run; explicit feedback continuation follows in M6.3.


## M4.4 — Synthetic delivery complete

The simple and rework paths now connect imported Jira work to a validated PR and confirmed Jira reporting through the actual application services. Each run uses four independent OMP processes/sessions, three verified scripted-provider model assignments, Rust Mail, an owned Git worktree, real Docker checks, a local bare remote, and controlled Jira/Bitbucket HTTP endpoints. The simple path made 36 scripted model requests and exchanged six peer messages. The rework path made 69 requests and exchanged twelve messages; one failed check and one requested review repair produced three candidates before final validation.

Both paths hold permission errors and outages, lose the PR-create, In Progress and comment replies, restart the server twice and reconcile exactly one accepted write of each kind. Browser assertions verify current review readiness, the confirmed Jira PR link and controller delivery notices. Unknown outcomes never become success through an empty lookup. Durable controller notices now resume coordinator work when provider receipts change; these are separate from user steering and agent mail, with their own dispatch/receipt recovery.

Additional failures found and fixed: refresh previously erased the reason for a blocked PR write; older successful pipeline retries could replace a newer failed retry. Branch-specific status observations now must match the published branch. Test fixture corrections changed an internal enum label to its actual UI text and selected the final candidate's check hash after rework. Failed attempts remain in ignored local logs.

Validation: **74 domain tests, build, five Playwright journeys, the complete simple and rework deliveries, and the retained Jira reporter/conflict regression pass.** All guarded runs deny external model/network access and leave no test-owned agent processes. Check containers are stopped and removed by the driver. [Results, revision identities and source hashes](synthetic-delivery-2026-09-28.json). Reproduce with `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH python3 scripts/test-application-runtime.py --delivery --output <fresh-short-directory>`; add `--coding rework` for the repair path.
