## R1 — Supervision under observation load

A timed-out host request now checks both owned process identities. When both match live process leases, Crewroom reports degraded observation. It leaves the run and recovery counters unchanged, then retries on the next pump. A confirmed exit still enters recovery. Unknown ownership requires attention. Startup uses the same distinction and verifies host identity before it resumes observation and delivery.

The runtime snapshot no longer includes event history. The `/api/runs/:id/events?after=<rowid>` endpoint returns ordered pages of at most 200 events. The browser fetches these pages after a cursor and retains the coordinator conversation and each agent's recent activity. Scripted runtime drivers use the same cursor contract. Repository and result observation use asynchronous Git and a cache keyed by HEAD, target, index, worktree status, changed-file metadata, saved work, and accepted requirements. Evidence creation, candidate checks, and publication gates still use uncached exact revisions.

The new slow-host regression uses two owned test processes, a host response delayed past the 3-second timeout, and concurrent fast and slow observers. Its working process advances during the timeout. The run remains `running`, with zero new automatic cycles. A simulated restart keeps the live process degraded until identity succeeds. A confirmed exit enters recovery. Cursor tests cover ordered delivery without duplicates, and a browser reconnect displays replies across 233 saved events. Repository tests detect a second edit to the same dirty path.

Final validation on Node 26: `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npm test` **83/83 passed**; `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npm run build` **passed**; `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npm run test:e2e` **7/7 passed**. The guarded offline run `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH python3 scripts/test-application-runtime.py --fault specialist --output /tmp/cr-r1-an3aqni3/run` **passed** with 11 scripted provider requests, no provider errors, no surviving processes, and one automatic cycle for the confirmed-dead specialist. The temporary report is at `/tmp/cr-r1-an3aqni3/run/report.json`. The first guarded run failed because its driver expected events in the old snapshot; the migrated cursor driver passed. A later attempt could not start because its macOS tmux socket path exceeded 104 bytes; the short `/tmp` path passed. The browser test first failed because the same progress text appeared in two elements; a specific progress locator passed.

These are disposable, guarded checks. They did not restart the retained pilot, call a live model, change the pilot repository, or reset its consumed cycles. The original 45-second live monitoring attempt remains a failure. R3 still owns per-host scheduling and synchronous process inspection; R4 still owns event retention and store structure.

## U1, R4 and the R3 live agent wall — 2026-09-28

**U1.** Interrupted, incomplete or timed-out checks are labelled as such; only a completed check with a non-zero exit shows *Failed*. Unsaved brief edits are kept per ticket across panels and ticket switches, and the ticket list marks them. Operators can record an explicit, idempotent *repair allowance* (`POST /api/runs/:id/assignments/:assignmentId/repair-allowance`, reason required) that raises one assignment's automatic cycle limit by one; history is never removed, and the exhausted state is shown on the agent's tile. Resume stays available because operator resumes never consume automatic cycles; the tile states that automatic repair stays blocked until an allowance is recorded. Cancel requires a second confirmation. Queue run lists why it is disabled.

**R4.** The abandoned lifecycle work was reviewed and completed: `server/run-lifecycle.ts` is the single pure authority for run transitions (conflicts map to HTTP 409); schema v15 adds generated, indexed `status`, `desired_state`, `state` and `ownership` columns, gives events a stable cursor column, and compacts only progress frames superseded by a recorded `message_end`. The migration crashed when it ran on a database whose version had been lowered after migrating; each step is now idempotent. `scripts/check-pilot-migration.ts` migrates a `VACUUM INTO` copy of a database and asserts runs, attempts, assignments, coding records and every non-progress event are unchanged and the indexed columns match. It passed on a v14 database created by the `c124b56` code. It later passed on a copy of the retained pilot database; see [the pilot-copy check](#r4--pilot-copy-migration-check).

**R3 (live wall, [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-live-agent-wall-over-push-channel.md)).** The browser no longer polls. One WebSocket (`/api/live`, loopback host and same-origin required) carries state topics (`workspace`, `run:`, `result:`) pushed only when they change after a store commit, a POST, or a 2-second server heartbeat; event topics that resume from a cursor; and read-only terminal topics. `server/terminals.ts` keeps one tmux control-mode client attached to Crewroom's private socket, forwards `%output` per pane, writes a 2 MB bounded per-attempt `terminal.log`, and replays it once a pane or tmux server is gone. Hosts now render readable activity into their pane (streamed response text, tool calls, command start/exit) instead of event JSON, and container command output is streamed live to `tools/<hash>/live.log` and a check tile; final receipts still come from the engine's completed logs. The run view is a grid of `ghostty-web` tiles (80×24 panes), proposed specialists as placeholders, running checks as their own tiles, retired/earlier attempts as chips, a focus mode, and a docked Coordinator / Change & evidence / Brief panel with Markdown replies and timestamps. Slow viewers are cut to a `lagging` state above 2 MB buffered and resynchronised from a fresh snapshot. Supervisor tmux calls are asynchronous and `stopRun`/`close` await the pump instead of busy-waiting.

Checks executed in a Linux container (Node 24.21, tmux, Chromium 1194) because the Mac's installed packages could not run there: `npm test` minus `processes` and `supervision-load` **86/86 passed**; those two files depend on macOS process-identity behaviour and fail in this environment for that reason. `npm run build` **passed**. `npm run test:e2e` **8/8 passed**, including the new `wall.spec.ts`: two real tmux panes stream live with zero HTTP requests over 2.5 s idle, a check tile streams appended output, a proposed specialist appears without a terminal, a repair allowance is recorded, focus and reload reconnect work, and a 390 px viewport has no horizontal overflow. `tests/live.test.ts` covers origin rejection, change-only pushes, cursor resume across reconnect, live check files, and a real tmux pane through viewer detach/reconnect and replay after `kill-server`.

**Still outstanding for R3:** the supervision pump is still one serial loop per 100 ms (per-host independent observation is not done); the native OMP TUI alongside RPC is untested; there is no automated slow-viewer test; and none of this has run against real OMP/Rust Mail processes. Run on the Mac before relying on it: `npm install && npm test && npm run build && npm run test:e2e` and the guarded `python3 scripts/test-application-runtime.py --fault specialist`. The retained pilot was not touched.

**Live pane timeout diagnosis.** The reported failure timed out while waiting for `tick 399` after viewer reconnect. The isolated probes were not retained: standalone tmux reached `tick 399` 13 seconds after launch; `TerminalHub` reached it 11.1 seconds after observing `tick 40`; and the `LiveHub`/WebSocket probe reached it 11.5 seconds after the second viewer's reset. No pane stop reproduced. **[INFERENCE]** The 400-tick fixture's 15-second wait was timing-sensitive under the reported run; this does not establish that viewer detach stopped the agent. The test now holds output until each viewer's pane/live reset: the first viewer receives `tick 0`–`tick 40`, then the reconnected viewer receives `tick 41`–`tick 159` as live data. The retained log still replays after tmux shutdown. The focused test passed 1/1 in 6.2 seconds; `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npm test` passed 90/90 in 46.1 seconds. These checks cover the uncommitted test change based on HEAD `8106b19840e8deced3d6f49df35fad0245faa460`; `tests/live.test.ts` SHA-256: `fc071ed8b1e94827ea186e3274ad954fe80310f4c99a7e219bb7742e1887017d`.

## R3 — Event-driven supervision, 2026-09-28

Complete on the Mac, on HEAD `8106b19840e8deced3d6f49df35fad0245faa460` plus the uncommitted working tree. The unchanged pre-existing files still hash to `package.json` `c9c3a7ca…8950` and `tests/live.test.ts` `fc071ed8…017d`. Implemented by Claude Opus 5.5 across two sessions. The first session ran into its one-hour limit and delivered no checks; its diff was treated as unverified until the checks below ran.

**Supervision.** The 100 ms serial pump is gone. Each admitted host gets its own observer (`observeHost`), which long-polls the host's `events` op for up to 5 s. The host answers as soon as a new event arrives. When a request fails, the observer checks owned process identity. A live, identity-matching owner is degraded observation and charges no recovery cycle. A confirmed exit queues recovery for that assignment. Unknown ownership requires attention. tmux control mode subscribes to `pane_dead`, so a pane exit wakes the observer without waiting out the long poll.

Each run has one FIFO `RunQueue`. It serialises start, resume, pause, cancel, recovery, coding and team transitions, peer-message sends and reconciliation. Signals are coalesced per run, and a slow transition in one run does not delay observation of another. A reentrant admission reservation spans start, continuation, recovery, coding completion and team change, so a competing start cannot claim the runtime between stopping old hosts and claiming new ones. Launch aborts if a pause or cancel is recorded at any point up to and including the final identity response. An observer failure never rejects: it blocks the run for attention. `stopRun` still confirms process ownership when a saved journal is unreadable, then requires attention.

The supervision path uses asynchronous tmux, `ps`/`lsof`/lease probes, stop, commit, writer restore and revision gates. There are no busy-waits: the launch identity retry (50 ms) and the stop confirmation (30 ms, 600 ms bound) both sleep with a timer.

**Asynchronous repository and evidence Git (closes the R3 synchronous-call gap, 2026-09-29).** The first R3 pass left agent tool handlers and operator routes on synchronous `git` (`execFileSync`, 30 s timeout), so every repository read, writer claim, edit, completion request, check snapshot and evidence gate stopped the event loop and every observer. Claude Opus 5.5 implemented the cutover:
- `RepositoryWork` has one asynchronous `git()`; the synchronous twins (`statusUncached`, `readFileAsync`, `claimWriterAsync`, …) are gone. No `execFileSync`/`spawnSync` remains under `server/`. Check-snapshot hashing uses `fs/promises`.
- `Evidence.assess`/`registerCandidate`, `Coding.claim/edit/requestCompletion`, `Checks.prepare`, `Results.read/diff`, `Publication.view/read/prepare` and `JiraDelivery.prepare` are asynchronous. The Bitbucket and Jira `beforeDispatch` guards may be asynchronous and are awaited immediately before `fetch`.
- Mutating agent tools (claim, edit, completion, check preparation, publication and Jira requests) run in the run's FIFO `RunQueue` and reauthorize inside it. Read-only tools stay outside the queue.
- Awaits introduce interleavings. Each durable step rechecks current state synchronously after its last await: writer ownership before a file write; run intent before a completion request, a prepared check spec, and each commit mutation (a `beforeDispatch` hook runs after Git's configuration probe); coordinator/reporter authority before publication and Jira intents; run state as the last check before a push or provider write. Evidence readiness reads checks and reviews after the repository await. Operator repository admission is serialized per run.
- `RuntimeSupervisor.request()` replaces `AbortSignal.any([signal, AbortSignal.timeout(t)])` with an owned timer. Under Node 22.14.0 (the shell default at first), the supervision-load and four-host tests failed deterministically with the combined signal: an 8.2 s scripted events reply completed even though the 8 s timeout should have aborted it. The garbage-collection explanation is an inference, and whether Node 26 is affected was not tested. The owned timer made both tests pass.

Regressions in `tests/repository-work.test.ts`: a Git shim delays every backend Git call by 250 ms while a 10 ms observer timer runs. Before the change, the first status call stalled observers for 3,180 ms with zero ticks. After it, status, list, read, pinned review read, diff, check snapshot, writer claim, edit and completion request each keep the worst gap under 150 ms. Two ordering tests, both deterministic without timers, cover a writer stopped while its edit awaits Git and a pause recorded while a completion request awaits Git. The first failed before the change; the second covers a review finding.

Independent review: `reviewer-deep` served by OpenAI `gpt-6-astra` (runtime transcript), for work produced by Anthropic Claude Opus 5.5. It reproduced five interleavings with isolated smokes: two P1 (a push dispatched after a stale authorization, and stale evidence records after the status await) and three P2 (completion, check preparation and commit ignoring a newly recorded pause or cancel). All five were repaired. The retained reviewer's narrow recheck passed all five smokes and found no new blocking defect.

Final tree on the supported Node 26.9.0 (`/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin`; `package.json` requires Node 24 or newer): `npm test` **106/106**, `npm run build` passed, `npm run test:e2e` **8/8**. Guarded real-process sweep: real OMP, Rust Agent Mail, production hosts and a private tmux socket under `sandbox-exec`, with scripted providers. **17/17** passed, and no processes survived. The sweep covered:
- coding simple, rework and large;
- six coding faults;
- coding with the browser;
- repository, Jira and delivery;
- the specialist fault;
- interruption pause and supervisor;
- continuity steering.

Outputs, the runner script and the unit log are in `/tmp/cr-r3-git-node26/`. The same gates and the 17 runs also passed under Node 22.14.0, both before and after the review repairs (`/tmp/cr-r3-git/`, `/tmp/cr-r3-git-final/`). The `package.json` and `tests/live.test.ts` hashes are unchanged. [r3-tested-tree.md](r3-tested-tree.md) lists the base tree and a SHA-256 for every modified or untracked source, test and script file these gates covered, how to check them, and the evidence limits.

**Repairs made during recovery:**
- The partial diff gated operator steering on coordinator idleness. That broke busy steering, which OMP `steer` mode accepts, and the guarded `continuity --scenario steering` run caught it. The gate is removed.
- Independent cross-vendor review (runtime-reported OpenAI `gpt-6-astra` via `reviewer-deep`; usage-level provenance unverified) found three P1 defects, each reproduced with a smoke:
  - an observer rejection could escape and block cleanup;
  - a pause arriving during the final identity response still dispatched initial work;
  - a competing start could abort an in-flight continuation.

  All three are fixed, and the retained reviewer's recheck passed all three smokes. `tests/supervision-hosts.test.ts` gains a malformed-journal observer-failure regression.
- Five harness drivers were stale or timing-sensitive, and all fail at HEAD as well:
  - `supervision-driver` and `results-driver` used Chromium offline emulation, which does not close an established WebSocket. The new `scripts/live-connection.ts` routes and closes `/api/live`.
  - `results-`, `jira-` and `delivery-driver` now select the *Change & evidence* tab.
  - `interruption-driver` waited only for `tool_started`, so the detached child had not yet written output. It now waits for that output.
  - `delivery-driver` restarted the supervisor while the Reporter's reply was still queued; a probe measured a 394 ms queue wait. It now waits for the reply.

**Native OMP TUI alongside RPC (spike, negative).**
- On OMP 18.4.2, RPC mode's command list (44 commands) has no `/collab`. The text is sent to the model as a prompt, so an RPC-controlled session cannot share a native TUI view.
- The interactive TUI can auto-host a view-only collab room: with `collab.autoStart: view` and a loopback relay, the TUI in a private tmux pane connected as `role=host` and appeared in `omp collab list`. That path lacks the RPC control contract. It needs a relay, and none ships with the binary; the default relay is external. An isolated agent directory also opens the first-run setup wizard.
- Decision: keep RPC control plus the formatted pane stream. Take-over stays deferred.
- The unsandboxed RPC probe sent both `/collab` texts to the default provider. Both were rejected with HTTP 403 (invalid token), so no model response was produced. No other probe traffic left loopback.

**Slow viewer.** `tests/live-viewer.test.ts` pauses one viewer's real TCP socket while a check log grows. That viewer is marked lagging; the server buffer stays between 2 and 4 MB and does not grow further. The fast viewer and the producer are not throttled. On resume, the slow viewer resyncs from the bounded 96 KB tail and continues live.

**Validation (Node 26.9.0, tmux 3.7c, Docker 29.4.1):**
- `npm test` **103/103** passed.
- `npm run build` passed.
- `npm run test:e2e` **8/8** passed.

Guarded real-process sweep on the final tree: real OMP 18.4.2 (`~/.bun/bin/omp`, SHA-256 `2b49620c…c14a`), real Rust Agent Mail (`mcp-agent-mail`, SHA-256 `554d6f0a…8075`), production hosts, and a private tmux socket, all under `sandbox-exec` with scripted providers. **33/33 passed**, and no processes survived:

| Harness | Cases |
| --- | --- |
| `test-application-runtime.py` | specialist fault (Specialist cycles 0/1/0); four agents; browser supervision; team before/after apply; rotation; coding simple/rework/large; six coding faults; coding with browser; repository; Jira; delivery |
| `test-application-mail.py` | message operations |
| `test-application-model-guard.py` | model guard |
| `test-application-interruption.py` | 7 faults: pause, host, tmux, supervisor, supervisor-host, detached, budget |
| `test-application-continuity.py` | 5 scenarios: session, first-turn, cycles, steering, lost-receipt |

The outputs are under `/tmp/cr-r3-final/`.

## R7 — Per-run ownership, 2026-09-29

Complete on the Mac (Node 26.9.0, tmux 3.7c, Docker 29.4.1), on HEAD `8106b198` plus the uncommitted working tree. Implemented by Claude Opus 5.5.

- **Explicit policy.** `server/execution-policy.ts` holds the first-release rule: at most one executing coding run (a run with repository work). `Workbench.claimAttempt` applies it transactionally against `executingRuns()` (runs with a claimed attempt whose ownership is not confirmed stopped). It previously refused any claim while any other run held ownership.
- **Per-run ownership.** `RuntimeSupervisor.startRun` checks only its own run for existing agents. The single global `admittingRun` is now a per-run reservation set; `admitting()` applies the same policy to start, continuation, recovery and coding/team transitions, so a coding run's stop-and-relaunch window cannot give its slot to another coding run. `claimRepositoryWriter` enforces one writer per worktree instead of one writer across all runs. A tmux session race between two runs launching at once is handled when the pane opens.
- **Allocated host ports.** The host binds its HTTP server before starting OMP, writes `models.yml` with its own origin and publishes `endpoint.json` atomically once it can answer. Without configuration it binds an OS-assigned port. `hostPorts` is now an optional pool, leased synchronously per launch and probed before use; sandboxed harnesses use it to keep their network allowance exact. Adopted hosts keep their recorded ports after a restart.
- **One `authorizeHost`.** Messaging, steering receipts, coordinator, publication, Jira and coding operations share one rule: a registered host's bearer token (constant-time comparison), a supervisor that is not closing, an accepted run status and a current, non-retired assignment. Callers add only service availability. Coding operations previously also accepted a retired assignment's attempt.
- **Concurrency defect found and fixed.** The first two-run real-process test failed: Rust Mail answered `fetch_inbox_events` with `RESOURCE_BUSY` (`recoverable`, `safe_to_retry`, "database is busy") while the other run wrote mail, and the run stopped for attention. `RustMailClient.call` now retries only tool errors marked recoverable and retry-safe, at most five attempts, and keeps the error text. It failed 1 of 13 runs before the fix and passed 15 consecutive later runs, apart from one run that loaded a file mid-edit.

Tests: `tests/runtime.test.ts` (research run executes beside a coding run; a second coding run cannot claim until the first stops; a paused run keeps its writer while another worktree gets its own), `tests/supervision-hosts.test.ts` (tokens authorize only their own attempt, a non-coordinator is refused, a stopped host loses authorization while the other run keeps executing), `tests/messaging.test.ts` (bounded busy retry; other rejections are not repeated). `REP-02` now asserts the policy conflict. Real process: `python3 scripts/test-application-runtime.py --concurrent` runs a coding run and a research run at once under one supervisor with four distinct pooled ports, and refuses a second coding run with HTTP 409 without admitting any agent.

## R5 — Actual usage, 2026-09-29

Complete on the same tree. Implemented by Claude Opus 5.5.

Each host's provider gateway parses the proxied response without altering it (`server/runtime/provider-usage.ts`) and records one `provider_usage` receipt per upstream request. The receipt holds either the provider's own counts (input including cached, cache read/write, output, reasoning, total) or `unavailable` with the reason: interrupted, non-2xx status, no report, or no response. OpenAI counts come from terminal `response.completed`, `response.incomplete` or `response.failed` events. Anthropic counts require `message_start` plus `message_delta`. Google counts require a candidate with `finishReason`. `Results` reports usage per attempt. It reconciles `provider_request` events against receipts, so a request whose host stopped first stays unavailable. The state is `actual` only when every request reported usage; otherwise it is `partial` or `unavailable`. Tokens are never inferred as zero. The Change & evidence panel shows per-attempt provider tokens, receipt counts and reasons beside OMP's cost estimate, which is still labelled an estimate. Attempts recorded before R5, including the retained pilot's, show no receipts and are labelled unavailable.

Tests: `tests/provider-usage.test.ts` (all three API families, split stream chunks, token-limited OpenAI response; interrupted, failed and unreported responses are unavailable) and `tests/results.test.ts` (sums, replay deduplication, missing receipt after host exit, per-attempt rows). The real-process roundtrip asserts that every current attempt's usage is actual, at 10 input and 15 total tokens per scripted response. The browser results driver asserts the per-attempt table.

**Independent review (R7 + R5):** `reviewer-deep`, context model `aperture-openai/gpt-6-astra`. Native serving telemetry was unavailable, so vendor independence is unverified. It found no R7 defect and two R5 P2 defects, both reproduced: token-limited OpenAI responses were discarded, and a request cut off by host exit was omitted, so the attempt showed as fully actual. Both were repaired, and the reviewer's narrow recheck passed.

**Validation (both items):** after the review repairs, `npm test` 115/115, `npm run build` passed and `npm run test:e2e` 8/8 (before the repairs: 115/115 and 8/8). The guarded real-process sweep (real OMP, Rust Agent Mail, production hosts, private tmux, `sandbox-exec`, scripted providers) passed all 36 cases, with no surviving processes. ## R3 repair: requirements read after asynchronous Git, 2026-09-29

R3's asynchronous Git opened a window: a brief refinement recorded while a repository probe was in flight could be missed. Authoritative status would then report the old requirements, and the evidence, publication and Jira gates could accept a candidate against superseded criteria. Authoritative status and both observed-status cache paths now rebuild the requirements hash after their last asynchronous probe. An edit whose ignore probe is pending when a refinement lands is rejected.

Regressions: `tests/publication.test.ts` (a refinement while the final Git probe is pending blocks stale evidence, publication and Jira reports, with no provider request); `tests/repository-http.test.ts` (an observation in flight during a refinement reports requirements drift); `tests/repository-work.test.ts` (an edit waiting on its ignore probe is rejected). The repair was made in an earlier session, and its producer is not recorded.

**Independent review:** `reviewer-deep`, context model `aperture-openai/gpt-6-astra`; serving telemetry unavailable. Correct, no finding. Both refinement regressions passed. An extra real-Git smoke showed that a stabilised Git cache entry is reused while concurrent observers still see the newly refined requirements and evidence readiness becomes false.

## R6 — Structured briefs, 2026-09-29

Implemented by Claude Opus 5.5. The coordinator's team proposal now carries a brief for each specialist: the accepted criteria it owns, its obligation for each one, the configured checks that cover each one, and the teammates it depends on. `snapshotBriefs` (`server/runtime/brief.ts`) validates each brief when the proposal is saved: every criterion exists and appears once, every check is configured for the repository, dependencies name real teammates and form no cycle. It stores the accepted criterion text with the obligation. A retired teammate stays a valid dependency, and its brief stays visible to dependents. Each host presents a specialist's own brief and its dependencies' obligations in the first request. The host schema and server share one set of limits (10 dependencies, 100 checks per criterion).

Tests: `tests/brief.test.ts` (snapshot content; unknown criteria, unconfigured checks, unknown or self dependencies and cycles rejected; retired dependencies) and `tests/supervision-hosts.test.ts` (a proposal snapshots validated briefs and replays idempotently). In the real-process sweep, each team and coding case asserts `briefsPresented`: the real runner's first request for a specialist contains its brief and its teammates' obligations.

**Independent review:** `reviewer-openai`, session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol`; serving telemetry unavailable. Four P2 findings:
- the host's dependency limit rejected valid briefs that include retired assignments;
- a criterion could not list every configured check;
- replaying a tool call failed for proposals saved before R6;
- a retired implementer's brief was hidden from its dependent reviewer.

All four were repaired. A follow-up recheck found a limit mismatch between host and server, now fixed with shared limits. The final recheck found no remaining issue.

## R2 — Specialist tools, 2026-09-29

Implemented by Claude Opus 5.5. [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-contained-specialist-tools.md) records the decision. Specialists have `repository_search` (`git grep` over the owned worktree, or a reviewer's pinned candidate), `edit_file` (exact-span replacement read at `expectedHash`, through the same writer-owned, durable edit ledger as `write_file`) and `run_shell` (an ad-hoc command in its own ADR 0006 container with the worktree mounted read-only and no network). Shell output goes only to the agent and is never check evidence. The contained full native harness is deferred: there is no Linux OMP build, and it would need gateway-only egress and would bypass the edit ledger.

Tests: `tests/coding.test.ts` (search scope, regex and path scoping, rejected escapes and pathspec magic, pinned-revision search, unique-span patches, stale hash, ambiguous span, non-writer and replay) and `tests/container.integration.ts` (reads and `/tmp` writes succeed through the read-only mount; worktree writes fail). Real-runner contract: `python3 scripts/test-application-runtime.py --coding simple --coding-fault shell`. A specialist runs `run_shell` with a detached child, and the operator pauses during the live command. The container stops, and neither process writes further output. Ownership is confirmed stopped, and resume creates replacements without charging an automatic cycle. The replacement uses `repository_search` and `edit_file`, and independent check and review certify the candidate (21 scripted provider requests, no provider errors, no surviving processes).

**Independent review:** `reviewer-deep`, context model `aperture-openai/gpt-6-astra`; serving telemetry unavailable. Three P2 findings: user Git configuration could enable column output and corrupt parsing; the per-file match limit truncated results without saying so; the search path was not treated as a literal pathspec. All three were repaired, and the recheck passed.

## R8 — Learning proposals, 2026-09-29

Implemented by Claude Opus 5.5. The coordinator's `propose_learning` tool records a proposed change to a role or to project guidance. The proposal must cite recorded evidence from its own run (steering, check or review), and it records the version it was based on. The workbench's Learning panel shows the rationale, the evidence, the current and proposed text, and the operator's decision with a required reason. An approved role change becomes the next role revision, with the proposal ID as its provenance. Approved project guidance becomes a new guidance version. Only assignments admitted afterwards receive either. Historical assignments, the check policy and the validation rules are unchanged. A proposal against an outdated version, or one that a role edit overtakes before the save completes, is shown as *Approved · not applied*.

Crash safety: the approval is recorded with a pending-save marker before the role file is written. Startup, every role read that feeds admission, and an operator retry complete a pending save exactly once. Operators cannot set the provenance field, and a later edit clears it. A replayed tool call returns the saved proposal.

Tests: `tests/learning.test.ts` (5: evidence required and future-only versioning; durable approval before the save, with interrupted saves completing once; a transient save failure retried while running; replay after the target changed; versioned project guidance snapshotted into new assignments only) and `tests/roles.test.ts` SCF-09 (an edit records the next revision). Real-runner: `python3 scripts/test-application-runtime.py --coding simple --learning`. The coordinator proposes an Implementer change citing the unit check that certified the candidate. The proposal stays pending until the operator approves it through HTTP. Approval raises the role revision by one and keeps its model and effort (19 scripted provider requests, no provider errors, no surviving processes).

**Independent review:** `reviewer-openai`, session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol`; serving telemetry unavailable. Two P1 findings: a pending role save could take effect before the approval was durable, and interrupted approvals were not tied to their proposal, so an identical twin could claim another proposal's save. Four P2 findings: a rejected proposal could keep a partially saved change; a replay revalidated its target before looking up the saved call; a pending team kept stale role instructions at admission; and a transient role-file failure had no retry while the server ran. All six were repaired, and the final recheck found none remaining (focused learning and roles tests 13/13).

## M6.3 (target drift, pulled forward for M5.3) — Target refresh, 2026-09-29

Before resuming the pilot, `git ls-remote` showed the pilot repository `main` at `10fa6dc8`, 15 commits past the admitted base `0ca3df57`. The candidate's two files did not overlap the 185 changed files, `git merge-tree` was clean, and the root `package.json`/lockfile were unchanged. Publication refuses a moved target, and the accepted drift policy (rebase the owned branch, then rerun checks and review) was not implemented. Its target-refresh part was therefore built ahead of the rest of M6.3. Implemented by Claude Opus 5.5.

- **Operator refresh.** `POST /api/runs/:id/target-refresh` and the *Refresh from target* button run in the run's supervisor lane and need a paused or attention-required run with every agent stopped, no writer, and no unresolved publication receipt. The server fetches the remote target into the run's own `refs/crewroom/<run>/target` (operator refs untouched). Without a remote, it uses the admitted target reference. It then rebases the owned branch with hooks, GPG, autostash and custom merge drivers disabled, and records a new candidate with `refreshedFrom`. Earlier checks and reviews never cover it. The coordinator receives a `target_refresh` notice naming the candidate that needs checks and review.
- **Safety.** The intent is saved before Git runs. A conflict, a rewritten target, or a result that changes different files leaves the branch at its previous head and is recorded as *conflicted*. After a restart, an unfinished rebase is undone (*interrupted*). A rebase that finished before its record is completed, and its candidate is registered exactly once. Launch refuses while a refresh is in progress.
- **Publication continuation.** A candidate derived by refreshes from the published one reuses the same branch and PR. It is pushed with a lease on the previously pushed head, and the PR description is updated once to name the new candidate, base and checks. Other replacement candidates still need the explicit review continuation.

Tests: `tests/target-refresh.test.ts` (3, real Git with a bare remote and a loopback Bitbucket fixture). A moved target produces a stale new candidate; replay adds nothing, and an unmoved target is a no-op. Its checks and review are recorded, then the PR is updated without a second create. The conflict case leaves the branch unchanged. Crash recovery covers both an unfinished rebase and one finished before its record.

**Independent review:** `reviewer-openai`, session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol`; serving telemetry unavailable. One P1: a periodic flush that had loaded the earlier publication could overwrite the successor saved while it waited on Bitbucket (reproduced). One P2: the local fallback built `refs/heads/<target>` instead of using the admitted tracking reference. Both were repaired. The P1 regression fails with the wait removed and passes with it. The recheck passed.

Validation: `npm test` **128/128**, `npm run build` passed, `npm run test:e2e` **9/9**. Not yet covered by a scripted real-process case; the live pilot below exercises it with real OMP agents.

## Ownership probe latency and exit window, 2026-09-29

After M5.3, the full unit suite failed 1–4 timing-sensitive supervision tests per run. Each passed in isolation. Bisecting reproduced the failures only while `tests/repository-work.test.ts` ran concurrently. There were two causes, both in `server/runtime/processes.ts`:

- **Slow lock probe.** `inspectOwned` confirmed a live leader with `lsof -t <lease.lock>`. That query scans every process's open files: about 1.3 s idle, and past its 5 s timeout under load, when it returned no holders. It now asks first with `lsof -t -a -p <leader>` (about 0.07 s) and runs the full scan only when the leader is not the holder, so escaped descendants are still found. The suite's duration fell from about 150 s to 82 s.
- **Exit window.** A process caught mid-exit briefly reads as *uncertain*. Its lock is released while it is still listed, or its leader is gone while the lock is still held. Crewroom treated that one reading as final. Processes with the `E` (exiting) flag are now filtered like zombies. `stopOwned` and the supervisor's observation check re-inspect an *uncertain* result every 50 ms for at most 1 s. Each recheck skips the all-process scan and is cut off when the window ends. *Stopped* and *live* keep their exact requirements, and nothing is signalled unless it is *live*.

Evidence: before the change, about one in three full runs failed at `supervision-load.test.ts:107`, with each of the two transient reasons. The test now reports the recorded ownership reason. After the exit-window change, 5 of 5 consecutive full runs passed 133/133. The final bounded version then passed 11 of 12 full runs at 133/133. The one failed run was not captured (a filter dropped the test name), and it did not recur in the next 9 runs. It is recorded as a known rare intermittent; further investigation was stopped at the operator's request to limit spend. `reviewer-openai` (session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol`) reviewed the probe change and the settle window. It found two P2 issues: the window could exceed 1 s, first through the full scan and then through a slow recheck. Both were repaired, and the final recheck found no issue.

**Closeout validation.**
- Guarded real-process sweep: 38/38 on OMP 18.4.3, with no surviving processes (`/tmp/cr-final-sweep/`). It ran before the exit-window change.
- `npm run build` passed, and `npm run test:e2e` passed 9/9.
- After the change, the guarded ownership-dependent cases passed 10/10 (`/tmp/cr-own/`): interruption pause, host, supervisor, supervisor-host and detached; coding pause and shell; specialist; continuity cycles; delivery.
- After the final bounded version, interruption pause, detached and supervisor-host passed again (`/tmp/cr-own2/`).

## M6.1 slice 1 — Requirement revisions, 2026-09-29

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced). Started from `main` HEAD `972aaa4afa21547fb2129cf0b19d496479bbbc40`, tree `afa1d8f192351c99064ecc38684ecc636000a998`; the staged implementation tree exercised before adding this record was `ffe4e769c874a05ff43bf91c8380c2c0661ea74f`.

- **Accepted requirements.** Brief saves append immutable, source-revision-linked records with origin, optional decision note and proposal provenance. Migration v17 imports the current refinement as the initial history entry without rewriting Jira source or any run/assignment snapshots. The workspace and `GET /api/tasks/:id/revisions` expose history; the ticket panel marks the latest accepted entry.
- **Explicit adoption.** `POST /api/runs/:id/requirements/adopt` takes a UUID requestId, latest accepted revision and (for claimed attempts) a required reason. Queued adoption replaces the run snapshot once; active adoption records a durable decision, pauses at an ownership-safe point, and delivers a persisted coordinator steering message with separate pending/received/applied states. Changed or removed criterion obligations flag only affected assignments; the coordinator retires and replaces them through the existing team proposal path, leaving historical briefs intact. Coding requirements hashes change upon adoption, revoking prior candidate checks/review/readiness and blocking publication and Jira evidence reports until matching work is validated. A Jira source-conflict pause clears only after adopting a revision accepted against the current source.
- **Proof.** Domain cases cover reopen and v16 migration, queued replay, active reason and affected-brief transitions, Jira conflict, stale publication and operator-driven coding continuation. A guarded real-process scripted-provider case paused, restarted and resumed the coordinator; its adoption message was applied, the provider saw the changed criterion and reason, and no processes survived. Playwright edited the brief after queueing and in a claimed-attempt fixture, then checked decision/history/undelivered status after reload; the fixture truthfully showed unconfirmed process ownership, not a live agent.

## M6.1 slice 2 — Explore and Grill, 2026-09-29

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced), from `main` HEAD `3fec425` after slice 1. This slice does not implement research/design outcomes or queue ordering; M6.1 remains open for slice 3 and its single independent review.

- **Run history.** Migration v18 replaces the unique ticket-to-run index with a transactional partial unique index on non-terminal runs. A ticket can retain several closed/cancelled runs but only one open run; keyed replays return the saved run across connections. Direct coding queue remains available without refinement. Repository reassignment and Jira source-change handling now consider the open run, not an arbitrary historical one.
- **Coordinator-only refinement.** `POST /api/tasks/:id/refinement-runs` accepts Explore or Grill. The operator starts one independent OMP coordinator in a tmux pane, using the existing Rust Mail, steering, observation, active-time and recovery paths. Explore develops requirements; Grill challenges assumptions and criteria. Neither prepares a worktree nor admits coding specialists, grants, publication, Jira operations, repository tools or contained commands. When repository access cannot be pinned read-only without a second convention, this slice gives the refinement run no repository access. `POST /api/runs/:id/close` waits for owned host/OMP processes to stop, then marks the run terminal while retaining its transcript.
- **Proposals and decisions.** The coordinator's `propose_refinement` tool saves a validated brief, criteria, decisions and unresolved questions against its admitted requirement/source revision; keyed tool replay is idempotent. Notes are saved with proposal and base revision even if the operator discards. Accept (with optional edited brief/criteria) atomically creates a refinement-origin accepted revision and links its notes to the new revision. Discard requires a reason and retains the proposed notes as discarded. Stale-base acceptance is blocked; accepted revisions never rewrite historical or running run snapshots. The workbench shows proposal status, notes, revision history and run history after reload. `/api/runs/:id/requirements/adopt` remains a separate operator decision.
- **Proof.** `tests/refinement.test.ts` covers across-connection replay and one-open-run transitions into coding, validation, accepted/discarded/stale proposals, retained notes, Jira refresh and reopen. ## M6.1 slice 3 — Research outcomes, 2026-09-29

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced), from `main` HEAD `0f5602c` after Explore/Grill. The independent M6.1 review belongs to the lead after this slice commits; no live Jira, Bitbucket or model-quality proof is claimed.

- **Admission and source.** `POST /api/tasks/:id/research-runs` explicitly queues an accepted brief with criteria, with or without a target repository. Repository-backed admission records the target commit and serves only read-only Git blobs/search at that commit in the configured operator checkout; it never checks out, writes a ref, creates research worktree state or grants a writer lease. Research does not consume the one-coding-ticket slot. The existing R6 team proposal admits advisory specialists and a technical writer for draft artifacts, validates their criterion obligations and refuses coding check IDs; implementation/reviewer roles and coding, command, publication and PR tools are absent.
- **Evidence and outcome.** `submit_research_artifact` writes Markdown bytes and SHA-256 to the evidence store, preserving assignment/attempt/model, accepted requirement revision and pinned commit. Each citation checks commit, path, range and whitespace-normalized excerpt; missing limitations, unsupported citations or absent/invalid heading coverage leave a retained rejected artifact with explicit reasons. A same-title corrected submission supersedes the rejected record. The coordinator's completion request checks every current accepted criterion against unsuperseded verified artifacts. It freezes further submissions, stops and confirms process ownership, then records a distinct completed research outcome. Later adoption marks earlier evidence and completion stale; it does not change historical snapshots.
- **Jira and presentation.** An admitted delivery reporter, or the coordinator only when no reporter is assigned, queues the server-composed `research_outcome` comment through `jira_report` after a verified completion request. Delivery waits for actual completed ownership; a lost reply is reconciled through the existing Jira marker. The comment includes titles, hashes, limitations and a Crewroom results reference, without transition, attachment or imported requirement edit. The workbench renders saved Markdown, citation status, coverage, limitations, provenance and rejected/superseded history; research has no coding check, diff, commit or PR sections.
- **Focused proof.** Domain cases cover repository-free research, research alongside an executing coding run, pinned source and four invalid citation modes, missing limitations/coverage, wrong heading/criterion, correction, completion refusal/acceptance, adoption staleness, zero coding records and reporter-authored Jira idempotence. ## M6.2 slice 1 — Repository configurations, 2026-09-30

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced), from `main` HEAD `2e92462cbe8fab2973ff0a101fffc3cde1fabba3`. This slice changes repository creation, explicit selection and display; queue ordering, scheduling, dependencies and repository editing/deletion remain later work. No database version change was needed.

- **Validated creation.** The operator can save a name, canonical absolute checkout path, target branch, optional branch prefix, structured checks and optional UUID `requestId`. The root must be an existing Git checkout, not a Crewroom-owned linked worktree; independent linked checkouts remain eligible. The target resolves as `RepositoryWork.prepare` does: a local head first, then a remote-tracking ref. A duplicate resolved path/target is a 409. A keyed identical replay returns the same record even after target drift; changed content with that key is a 409. Git commands use argument arrays without a shell.
- **Truthful selection.** The workspace flags each repository's Bitbucket path match without exposing connection credentials. The Repositories panel edits check rows with digest, command, per-line arguments, limits and required status, and shows target/prefix/check count/publication availability. The ticket target card previews the selected repository's branch, checks and publication availability and distinguishes an unsaved choice from a saved target. Older selection labels remain compatible with existing browser drivers.
- **Evidence.** `tests/repository-config.test.ts` selects two distinct repositories on separate tickets, persists checks/prefix across a store reopen, prepares from a remote-tracking target on the configured prefix, and configures the run's evidence checks; it also rejects duplicate canonical paths, invalid Git roots/targets and Crewroom-owned worktrees while accepting an independent linked checkout. It verifies keyed replay after the target ref disappears. `tests/repository-http.test.ts` checks one matching and one unmatched publication connection without leaking a fixture credential. Playwright adds a checked repository, sees the bad-target response, saves the good target and verifies the check and unavailable-publication card after reload. Its visual receipt is `test-results/workbench-REP-01-configure-069a0--truthful-publication-state/repository-selection.png`. Existing tests that had used non-Git paths now seed actual disposable Git roots rather than weakening save validation.

## M6.2 slice 2 — Durable local queue, 2026-09-30

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced), from `main` HEAD `d3399522baf7612faa95e7d997cb1226a9128909`. This slice queues coding plans only; it does not start agents, schedule work, release an execution slot or introduce dependencies. Explore, Grill and research continue outside the ordered queue.

- **Order and persistence.** Store v20 replaces the former run-count `position` with a zero-based local position only while a coding run is `awaiting_runner` with no assignments. Existing queued coding plans migrate in creation order, ties by insertion order; nonqueued runs lose the obsolete position. Admission or cancellation removes a plan and compacts remaining positions. Reordering uses the complete queued run-ID permutation and an optimistic queue revision. Reimporting Jira rank never changes local order. `Workbench.codingQueue()` and `Workspace.queue` expose `{revision, plans: Run[]}` in order; each plan carries its accepted task snapshot, repository selection, optional launch spec and queue position. The revision changes on membership changes and reorders.
- **Launch and controls.** `POST /api/tasks/:id/runs` optionally captures a launch spec with the same team parser as manual `Supervisor.startRun` (one coordinator, distinct names, bounded structured briefs). Repository selection shares `RepositoryWork.prepare`'s schema: new branch by default, or explicit continue with branch and expected head. Queue admission validates enabled role IDs and brief obligations against accepted criteria and configured checks. `POST /api/runs/:id/launch` sets or replaces it before assignment admission; an admitted run cannot mutate it. `POST /api/queue/order` and `POST /api/queue/:id/remove` have UUID request IDs and transactional receipts; changed-key replays conflict, exact replays retain their original result, stale revisions and non-permutations return 409. Removing a plan applies terminal cancel semantics without spawning agents, retains history and any prepared repository work, and permits a new plan for that ticket. The existing keyed `/api/runs/:id/cancel` route uses the same queued cancellation operation.
- **Operator proof.** The ticket's Queue run form captures an enabled coordinator role (defaulting like the manual start form) and repository mode new; queued launch details can be replaced there. The sidebar lists ordered key, title, repository, coordinator, launch-detail presence and a scheduling hold placeholder, with keyboard-operable move/remove buttons. It labels plans as queued and not running. A stale reorder displays the conflict and requests a fresh pushed workspace. Playwright queued two tickets, moved the second above the first, reloaded, removed the first, reloaded again and observed zero agents and cancelled history. Domain tests exercised changed Jira ranks, two-store revision races, invalid permutations, keyed queue/launch/order/cancel replays, catalog/brief validation, prepared-plan cancellation, reopen, simulated v19 upgrade and the single-open-run invariant across cancel/requeue.

Validation on Node 26 (`PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH`): `node --import tsx --test tests/queue.test.ts tests/queue-http.test.ts` **8/8 passed**; `node --import tsx --test tests/brief.test.ts tests/supervision-hosts.test.ts` **16/16 passed** after the shared brief-schema repair; `node --import tsx --test tests/research.test.ts tests/repository-work.test.ts` **23/23 passed** after restoring the Zod import; `npx playwright test tests/e2e/workbench.spec.ts --grep 'QUE-05'` **1/1 passed** against live Vite source. The first focused Playwright run observed stale `dist` UI (0 queue rows); running the new journey against current source passed 1/1 before the final production build. ## M6.2 slice 3 — Scheduler and readiness release, 2026-09-30

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced), from `main` HEAD `142c27f376b6a5555aa44338cdbf88b3e48158d6`. This slice schedules coding plans only; ticket dependencies remain S4, and PR-feedback continuation/readiness revocation remain M6.3. No live Bitbucket or remote-model quality claim is made from the synthetic provider fixture.

- **Durable slot and release.** Store v21 has one `coding_slot` row. A transaction chooses the first eligible plan in local order, records its decision ID/time on the run, removes it from the queue, and reserves the slot before Git or agent work. Paused and attention-required unready runs retain it. Current publication readiness requests a durable `review` pause and records candidate/time/release; a verified merge requests a distinct merged pause/release; cancellation releases the slot. Process admission still uses `executionConflict`, with an additional pre-claim wait for any unstopped coding attempts (including legacy manually started runs without worktrees). Repeated readiness observations do not repeat the release; a subsequent explicit Resume cannot displace another holder. The released candidate remains recorded so its same ready observation does not immediately auto-pause an explicit continuation again.
- **Restart and controls.** Scheduler signals follow startup reconciliation, queue/order/launch/remove controls, run notifications and publication flush notifications. A single shared launch path prepares or reuses the owned worktree, configures evidence checks and starts the captured team. The initial team admits in one transaction, and restart reuses the original assignments; interrupted, confirmed-stopped initial hosts receive replacement attempts under the same slot. An uncertain process never licenses a successor. A launch failure is recorded as needs attention and retains the slot. Source-changed unadmitted plans stay queued with a requirements hold. Manual coding starts record the same decision and return 409 with the holding ticket/reason; research and refinement stay outside this slot. `dependencyHold(run: Run, task: Task, publications: PublicationRecord[]): string | undefined` is S4's sole eligibility seam and currently returns eligible.
- **Truthful workbench.** Queue and ticket show slot ownership and per-plan holds; queued, running and paused-awaiting-human-review remain distinct. The queue form explains automatic launch; manual start explains the slot. The selected coordinator role prefers an enabled role with an explicit model when one exists.

Playwright's QUE-03 fixture shows the waiting-key hold and paused-awaiting-review status with a screenshot at `.crewroom/test-results/m62/scheduler-review.png`.

## M6.2 slice 4 — Dependencies and queue scenario, 2026-09-30

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced), from `main` HEAD `288dd735c997044c8d2ebdd8f2e02e290e3cd555`. - **Declaration and eligibility.** `POST /api/tasks/:id/dependencies` replaces up to 12 local ticket IDs in a transactional UUID requestId/revision operation. A keyed replay returns its original result across connections; stale revisions, duplicate/unknown/self references and graph cycles fail without a partial write. The set resides in ticket refinement, not the Jira source snapshot. A queued run checks the current declarations when scheduled, without invalidating the accepted brief for dependency-only edits; a started run keeps its admitted work. The Queue panel and ticket display distinct none, open, closed-unmerged, unknown and merged states with ticket-keyed reasons. An exact `PublicationRecord.merged` on a dependency coding run releases the provider hold. Before claiming a slot, asynchronous Git checks that the checkout target contains each same-configuration dependency merge commit. An uncontained or missing commit holds the plan with an instruction to update the checkout target; `RepositoryWork.prepare` repeats this against the exact prepared base before reserving a worktree. A target changing between screening and preparation returns the still-unadmitted plan to the queue instead of creating work. No fetch or remote write was added.

A opened a fixture PR, remained slot holder through created and CI-pending, then readiness auto-paused A; only independent B started, after A's host stopped. C did not start while A's provider PR was OPEN, then DECLINED; after the fixture returned MERGED with matching head/branch/target and a checkout-contained merge commit, C started only after B stopped. An unqueued backlog ticket never started; the fake provider saw no merge API request. Three separate coordinator OMP PIDs were observed, four scripted turns (a second A observation), zero provider errors and zero surviving owned processes. The report is `/tmp/crewroom-m62-s4-queue-final/report.json`. **Synthetic record boundary:** A's PR-created, CI-pending and ready flags were seeded as `PublicationRecord` values because this queue-only agent fixture does not produce a coding candidate/check/review receipt. The fake Bitbucket boundary subsequently observed OPEN, DECLINED and MERGED, with the merge proven through the production publication reconciliation path; Jira order came through fake Jira search, not direct ticket-row writes. This is simulated provider evidence, not a live Bitbucket merge.

## M6.2 review repair, 2026-09-30

Implemented by session-reported `aperture-openai/openai.gpt-6-sol` (reasoning effort not surfaced), from `main` HEAD `3362e39` after the independent Anthropic review of `2e92462..3362e39`. The reviewer identified two P1, two P2 and one P3 defects. Five named regression families were red on `3362e39` (focused command below: 0/9 before repairs); they are green with the original queue/dependency coverage.

- **P1 retained-store migration:** v21 no longer throws after v20 commits when several legacy coding runs exist. One unmerged retained run holds the slot; among several, the first with unstopped claimed ownership holds it; if none has such ownership, there is no holder. Other runs remain retained with a migration release marker and truthful paused/attention state. A prepared-only legacy run can Resume into a manual start without inventing an agent. Tests: `REVIEW-P1 migration: two retained paused v19 coding runs reopen without fabricating a holder`; `REVIEW-P1 migration: a sole retained run holds the slot, while among several only claimed ownership wins`; `REVIEW-P1 migration: prepared-only paused work resumes into manual start without inventing an agent`.
- **P1 launch replay:** scheduler restart checks `launchCompletedAt` and each assignment's current admitted attempt, ignoring historical stopped attempts. Its real scheduler path prepares the retained worktree and completes the replay without admitting a second logical assignment. Test: `REVIEW-P1 scheduler: restart after a claimed initial attempt replays current admitted attempt through dispatcher`.
- **P2 pre-admission failures:** manual starts validate team, enabled role, model/effort and brief before a slot decision, but still check the holder first for a ticket-keyed 409 and check again transactionally after preflight. Queued plans with a role that loses its model retain their priority, get a retryable hold, and automatically retry when a role decision restores the configuration. If a race still fails after the decision but before worktree reservation or any assignment, the transaction releases the slot and restores the original queue position; after repository reservation or admission, the run keeps the slot for attention as D2 requires. Tests: `REVIEW-P2 manual start: invalid team does not claim a slot and corrected retry reaches admission`; `REVIEW-P2 queued launch: model disappears before scheduler admission, leaving a retryable plan with an explained hold`; `REVIEW-P2: unadmitted decision restores original queue priority without a second worktree or assignments`.
- **P2 dependency revision:** local dependency declarations use their own nonnegative `dependencyRevision`, initially zero, for optimistic concurrency and replay. They no longer advance accepted requirement revision, block brief adoption, or alter coding requirement hashes. The ticket form and guarded queue driver use the separate token. Test: `REVIEW-P2: dependency edit between accepted brief and run adoption does not alter requirement identity` plus the existing QUE-04 cross-connection/replay/merge cases.
- **P3 truthful queue reasons:** after release, unconfirmed old coding ownership names the former ticket and waits for stopped agents rather than showing an eligible plan. Slot descriptions distinguish pausing from paused and identify operator, active-time limit, recovery, requirements and source reasons. Test: `REVIEW-P3 queue holds name unstopped owner and the exact pause reason`.

**Validation (Node 26 PATH).** Before edits, `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH node --import tsx --test tests/scheduler-review-repair.test.ts tests/dependencies.test.ts` **0/9 passed** with the five findings observed. After repairs, `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH node --import tsx --test tests/scheduler-review-repair.test.ts tests/dependencies.test.ts tests/coding-slot.test.ts tests/scheduler-http.test.ts` **18/18 passed**. `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npm test` **180/180 passed**; `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npm run build` **passed** (TypeScript and Vite; existing large-chunk advisory); `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npm run test:e2e` **16/16 passed**. `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH npx tsx scripts/check-pilot-migration.ts .crewroom/pilot/workflow.sqlite` **passed** on a copy (v16 → v21, 176 tickets, one paused merged run without a slot, 84 attempts, 3,044 retained receipts). Guarded `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH python3 scripts/test-application-continuity.py --scenario scheduler --output /tmp/crewroom-m62-review-scheduler` **passed** (2 scripted turns, zero survivors); the corresponding `--scenario queue --output /tmp/crewroom-m62-review-queue` **passed** (4 turns, zero survivors). The queued role-repair unit fixture deliberately uses an unavailable mail endpoint after it confirms actual admission, so the scheduler logs that expected external failure; the guarded process scenarios use the real Rust Mail boundary. No live Bitbucket or remote-model-quality proof is claimed.

**Repair reruns.** The first post-edit focused run passed 13/15: a manual request with a pre-existing holder got a model error before its ticket-keyed slot conflict, and the pause fixture required the literal word “budget” rather than the clearer active-time-limit reason. A read-only slot check before preflight, followed by the transactional claim, restored conflict precedence; the fixture now verifies the observable active-time-limit wording and distinguishes pausing from paused. Focused 18/18 and the gates above passed after these bounded repairs.

**Follow-up after recheck of `b77097a`.** The retained Anthropic reviewer confirmed the five findings above, then identified a resumed-run replay race and preparation-hold churn. A bounded Astra diagnosis (`aperture-openai/gpt-6-astra`; effort not separately exposed, `agent://M62LaunchDiagnosis/decision`) fixed the boundary: first coding execution, not a successful launch wrapper, is the monotonic `launchCompletedAt` fence. `startExecution` and persisted `agent_start` now set it in the same run transition; claims and admitted-only runs remain unfenced. Startup normalizes markerless retained runs only from positive running/active-time/agent-start evidence, including host journal events read during a safe stop. A resumed run uses host adoption or history-linked continuation, never the original launch input after execution. The supervisor FIFO counts queued and active lifecycle work before the callback starts; the scheduler excludes busy runs, revalidates schedule/control/current attempts after asynchronous Git preparation, and checks again inside the serialized scheduled-start callback. Superseded starts return without marking the retained run as needing attention. No schema-versioned migration was added.

**Preparation hold ownership.** `launchHold.source` distinguishes automatically rechecked `preflight` holds from `preparation` holds; older unclassified records conservatively mean preparation. Generic kicks or role saves cannot clear a preparation hold. A successful fresh-requestId Save launch details clears it and retries at the original queue priority; replay of that ID cannot clear a later hold. Manual Start retries can persist the preparation hold again. Missing checkout, generated/checked-out branch and unowned destination reasons are distinct, path-free, actionable messages; the missing-checkout conflict retains the original error as its diagnostic cause. Dependency ancestry holds keep their separate merge fingerprint and task dependency revision. Test receipts include real `Workbench` and `RepositoryWork`, the scheduler's actual selection, `RuntimeSupervisor.resume` with a mail registration barrier, two real independently leased `/bin/sleep` host/OMP processes for live adoption and stopped-host replacement, saved input journals, and HTTP launch-request replay. The suite asserts no second initial prompt, unchanged queue revision across unrelated kicks, stable hold timestamp/order across restart and a successful explicit retry.

The isolated archive was removed; no retained store was edited. ## M6.2 — Accepted, 2026-09-30

M6.2 is complete on `main` at `34bb44c`. The range is `2e92462..34bb44c`: slices `f2d552f` and `d339952` (repositories), `142c27f` (queue), `288dd73` (scheduler and readiness release), `3362e39` (dependencies and E2E-07), then review repairs `b77097a` and `34bb44c`. The operator approved the plan and these decisions before any code was written:

- The slot holder auto-pauses at PR readiness, which releases the slot.
- A paused or needs-attention run that isn't ready keeps the slot.
- Queuing a plan with launch details consents to automatic launch.
- Dependencies are local operator declarations, released only by a provider-observed merge.
- Only coding plans join the ordered queue.

All code was implemented by OpenAI GPT-6 Sol. One independent Anthropic review (Claude Opus 5.5) of `2e92462..3362e39` found two P1, two P2 and one P3 defects: migration refusal with several retained coding runs, a stuck slot after a crash during launch, pre-admission start failures stranding the slot, dependency edits blocking adoption, and misleading hold reasons. The first repair (`b77097a`) resolved all five. The recheck found that it introduced a resumed-run replay race (P2) and preparation-hold churn (P3). A bounded Astra diagnosis (GPT-6 Astra) chose an execution-transition fence, supervisor in-flight exclusion and source-owned holds, and `34bb44c` implemented that decision with regressions that fail on `b77097a` and pass after. The second narrow recheck reported both resolved, the earlier five still resolved, and no new defects.

Lead validation on `34bb44c` (Node 26.9.0 on PATH):

- `npm test` **193/193 passed**
- `npm run build` **passed**, with the existing chunk-size advisory
- `npm run test:e2e` **16/16 passed**

The producer's results on the same revision: pilot-copy migration **passed** (v16 → v21; the paused, merged pilot run holds no slot), and the guarded real-process `scheduler`, `queue` (E2E-07) and `adoption` scenarios **passed** with zero surviving processes (`/tmp/crewroom-m62-r2-{scheduler,queue,adoption}/report.json`). The full guarded sweep was not rerun.

Limitations carried forward:

- The E2E-07 fixture synthesizes ticket A's PR-created, CI-pending and ready publication records. Closed-unmerged and merged states come from the fake Bitbucket provider through production reconciliation. No live Bitbucket or remote-model quality claim is made.
- Crewroom does not fetch the target branch. A dependent ticket in the same repository stays held until the operator's checkout contains the merge commit.
- "Publication available" means a configured Bitbucket path match, not verified credential health.
- Once released at readiness, a run is not re-held if readiness is later revoked. While another run holds the slot, Resume on a released run is refused with a reason; queued PR-feedback continuation belongs to M6.3.
- Preparation holds (checkout unavailable, branch or destination conflict) retry only on a fresh Save launch details or a manual Start.
- Repository editing and deletion remain M6.4 work.

## M6.3 slice 1 — Confirmed readiness re-hold and queued Resume, 2026-09-30

Accepted, 2026-09-30, as the first bounded M6.3 item after the user approved the work breakdown and queue/conflict choices. Tested source/test state: `main` at HEAD `1a49bbbf783caa70ebfd407e839a91a9ae41e8ac` plus working-tree diff SHA-256 `5a9dffddd01c5f4f4063ca8419cf8c6d0d7b56ca8c5f607f1a96bcfcd24b2f43`. No feature commit, remote publication, or live Jira/Bitbucket operation.

**Decisions and behavior.** The operator's Resume remains explicit. A released review run re-holds ahead of ordinary local queue plans only after a confirmed source/revision/requirements change or a failed current required check/review. The `reviewInvalidated` reason is latched until full readiness returns. Provider/observation outages and unreadable local evidence still block a Ready claim but do not by themselves reserve the next slot. A queued Resume waits for a safe coding slot and confirmed prior ownership, survives restart, does not preempt a holder, and continues the existing run once. Readiness regression does not start agents. Cancel retains its existing run-level meaning and clears the queued request; there is no request-only withdrawal action.

**Implementation.** `EvidenceAssessment` identifies confirmed invalidation causes instead of inferring them from a blocker count; `PublicationRecord.reviewInvalidated` separates known regressions from unavailable observations. `CodingScheduler` blocks normal admission while coding ownership is unresolved, prioritizes queued Resume and confirmed re-hold, releases a passive re-hold only when current readiness is restored, and keeps the run paused. The workspace derives pending/held readiness state from the durable release and publication records. The wall distinguishes review release, validation re-hold, queued Resume, and no-agent explicit-Resume state. Resume requests are stored in the run JSON; no SQLite schema migration was needed.

**Focused checks (Node 26, all passed):** `tests/scheduler-replay-holds.test.ts` + `tests/scheduler-http.test.ts` for M6.3 — **3/3**; `tests/publication.test.ts` DEL-03/04/06 — **1/1**; `tests/target-refresh.test.ts` M6.3 — **3/3**; `tests/evidence.test.ts` EVD-01/04/05 — **1/1**; `tests/coding-slot.test.ts` QUE-01/03 — **1/1**; `tests/results.test.ts` R5/LIM-04 — **1/1**; `tests/supervision-hosts.test.ts` manual Resume — **1/1**. Changed-file LSP diagnostics reported no errors; existing Zod deprecation/unused-import hints remain.

**Independent review.** The high-risk Anthropic standards reviewer and the Anthropic spec reviewer found no remaining findings on the tested candidate. The P1 request-loss issue was repaired. The duplicate-resume race concern was withdrawn after checking that the state/slot claim and enqueue contain no await boundary and the per-run busy/revision fence coalesces continuation. Reviewer model identities are reviewer-reported: Fable 5 and Opus 5.5 respectively; serving telemetry was not independently available for all review roles. Producer was session-reported OpenAI GPT-6 Luna, effort unavailable.

**Integrated validation on the same source/test hash.** `npm test` **196/196 passed**, 0 skipped; `npm run build` **passed** (TypeScript plus 1,897 Vite modules; one nonblocking >500 kB chunk warning); isolated-snapshot `npm run test:e2e` **16/16 passed**, 0 skipped. The canonical receipt is `/tmp/crewroom-m63-s1-verification-final.md`, gate record `/tmp/crewroom-m63-s1-gates-final.json`, browser evidence `/tmp/crewroom-m63-s1-browser-final.json`, and runtime manifest `/tmp/crewroom-m63-s1-runtime.json`. Lead inspected the receipt, full queued Resume view, and E2E scheduler screenshot. Browser scenarios passed for explicit Resume, durable queued UI while another run holds the slot, cancellation without holder displacement, and readiness re-hold UI with no agent start. The re-hold browser fixture exercised the public Workbench projection; provider integration and scheduler transition are covered by the focused service tests.

**Repairs and limits.** The first integrated build found two type errors in the new evidence assessment contract and its Promise-or-snapshot test assertion; both were repaired, focused checks passed, and the final build passed. The first full E2E attempt failed 3/16 at an intermediate hash: two isolated snapshot setup failures due a missing local mailbox test executable, and one stale exact-copy assertion. The verifier copied only the non-pilot test executable into `/tmp`, and the obsolete callout assertion was removed while the behavior-bearing slot/hold browser assertions were retained; final E2E passed 16/16. The user-owned untracked image and default `.crewroom` state were untouched. No live Jira/Bitbucket provider calls or credentials were used; browser fixtures created no agent assignments or attempts. Conflict reconciliation and PR-feedback continuation remain open; M6.4/M7 and MVP completion remain unstarted.

Verifier runtime cleanup completed after lead ACCEPTED: only the owned temporary service/tab were stopped; 47863 and 4331 have no listeners, and E2E temporary data was removed.
