## M6.3 slice 2 — Crash-safe target conflict recovery, 2026-10-01

Accepted on the tested local candidate: `main` at HEAD `1a49bbbf783caa70ebfd407e839a91a9ae41e8ac`, tracked dirty diff SHA-256 `f7dd8f5ac4b730548a50693886f16aeaade2e1226276694f04ca51ebb8cfc1ee`, and untracked `tests/e2e/target-conflict.spec.ts` SHA-256 `de1de9ad94510ad4092761c3f46f639366cf2fbfcd92bae3015143148e991e61`. No commit, remote publication, or live provider write.

**Decisions and behavior.** Ordinary and reasoned target rebases record the observed Git-return and rollback phases. Reopened `rebasing` operations missing positive exit evidence, or interrupted after `undo_started`, remain held for inspection; HEAD or absent rebase metadata never certifies a stopped child. Workspace preparation and conflict continuation persist in-flight barriers before mutation. A continuing operation is keyed by writer attempt and tool-call identity through Git staging, rebase, and validation. Resume, writes, retry, checks, readiness, publication, and cleanup remain blocked while ownership is unverified. Version 23 migrates ambiguous v22 `conflicted` and `operator_required` workspaces—including superseded history—to nonretryable ownership-unverified holds while preserving artifacts and lifecycle facts. Only a coherent terminal checked-promotion rejection with exact completion, refresh, workspace, and process identity retains retryability.

**Implementation and regression evidence.** A real Git reproduction held a pre-rebase hook, killed its Node controller, observed no rebase metadata and a no-op abort, then released the original child and observed it move HEAD after the recovery decision. Real-process regressions cover ordinary rebase, isolated workspace preparation, and conflict continuation; they verify the restarted workbench stays held while the old child remains alive, and release/reap all test-owned helpers. Additional tests cover v22 migration, exact/near-miss checked-rejection provenance, superseded ownership barriers, keyed continuation replay, truthful Results state, and retained history. No generic process supervisor or orphan-kill mechanism was added.

**Focused checks (Node 26, pass).** `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH node --import tsx --test tests/target-refresh.test.ts tests/results.test.ts tests/store.test.ts tests/coding.test.ts tests/repository-work.test.ts tests/supervision-hosts.test.ts` — **101 passed, 0 failed**. `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node/bin:$PATH ./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit` — passed without diagnostics.

**Independent review.** A high-risk cross-vendor Fable review of the final production diff found no blocking findings. Its narrow recheck of the corrected E2E fixture also found no blockers. Reviewer identity was harness-reported Anthropic Claude Fable; no fallback was observed, but provider identity/effort telemetry was not independently available. Producer model was session-reported OpenAI GPT-6 Sol; effort/fallback were not verified.

**Integrated validation (same candidate).** The isolated validation snapshot check passed **274 paths, 0 mismatches**. `npm test` passed **262/262**, 0 skipped; `npm run build` passed (TypeScript plus 1,897 Vite modules; one nonblocking >500 kB chunk advisory); final `npm run test:e2e` passed **18/18**, 0 skipped, including both target-conflict browser cases. The earlier E2E attempt on the prior spec had 15/18 pass and is retained as preliminary failure evidence; the final corrected spec and snapshot rerun are recorded separately. Actual-browser AC4/AC5 passed for continuing and legacy ownership holds, checked-promotion rejection, ordinary conflict, and interrupted retry. The five local views each made five local HTTP requests, **0 POST and 0 external requests**. Main inspected the canonical receipt, assertions, and full-page screenshots; screenshot payloads were verified PNG. Canonical receipt and artifacts: `/Users/crewroom/.omp/agent/artifacts/m63-s2-final-20261001/receipt.md`.

**Residuals and continuation.** If a controller dies before direct `execFile` returns, no crash-durable child-owner receipt exists; the affected operation remains blocked for operator inspection rather than being automatically killed or inferred stopped. A failure after a durable `continuing` marker but before Git dispatch can also require operator inspection. Legacy versionless promoting records remain held. The conflict-reconciliation item is accepted; PR-feedback continuation remains open and requires the user's go-ahead. M6.4 and M7 have not started.

## M6.3 slice 3 — PR-feedback continuation, accepted 2026-10-01

Accepted on implementation commit `ad4c44a` (base `dec01c7`). That commit holds exactly the verified 22-file manifest; all hashes matched before and after the gates (`.crewroom/test-results/m63-slice3-verify/candidate-manifest.json`). External boundaries were simulated: local Git and disposable Bitbucket/model-host/check fixtures. There were no live Bitbucket, Jira or retained-pilot writes.

**Behavior.** Bitbucket PR comments (general, inline, replies, all pages) are read on a separate path from PR/check observation, keyed by repository UUID + PR ID + comment ID. A comment's version is `(updated_on, deleted)`, and a newer version wins. A stale or repeated read cannot replace newer state. An equal version with a conflicting body or deletion flag is held visibly. A metadata-only change updates in place. Absence never implies deletion. A complete, partial or unavailable read is stored with its observation time. A comment read never starts an agent, writes to the provider, revokes readiness or re-holds the slot. The existing Resume control pins the full last-known feedback set and its read state into one immutable continuation, bound to the run, publication, PR, branch and source candidate/head. A repeated, queued or restarted Resume reuses that continuation. Later comments stay observable and visibly newer than the captured version, but they never enter the captured input. The generic replacement context omits live feedback. Comments are presented as untrusted reviewer material within the accepted brief.

A feedback writer claim is authorized only for that continuation. A restored writer is authenticated against its stopped prior grant and the matching interrupted checkpoint, so retained dirty edits survive interruption without admitting a second writer. A changed completion creates a successor candidate (C1→C2, and C2→C3 for in-scope rework of a failed feedback candidate). Each successor needs fresh checks and independent review. It is published to the same PR with a leased push from the previously published head. A lost PR-update response is reconciled without another PR. A no-change completion keeps the source candidate and its evidence and creates no commit. If the PR closes or merges, or the branch/repository identity changes, a queued feedback Resume becomes terminal with an explained hold, even when nothing had been captured, and the scheduler moves to the next plan. The slot is released only when no writer or unstopped attempt remains. Resume with no known feedback behaves as before. No merge path, comment reply or resolution, webhook, or new control was added.

**Review.** Producer: OpenAI GPT-6 Sol (implementer). Reviewer: Anthropic Claude Opus 5.5 (retained `reviewer`, cross-vendor), as an early contract review plus narrow rechecks. The first batch had two P1s (a closed PR stalling the global queue; an interrupted feedback writer with dirty edits not restorable), two P2s (an unconsumable second continuation or source pin; whole-comment conflict comparison) and one P3 (an undefined-identity replay match). These were repaired in one consolidated pass (counter 1/2). A recheck found one remaining P1 path (no captured continuation), which was fixed within the same pass. The final recheck reported no findings. During focused checks the producer also found and fixed three issues: dedup that had included the observation timestamp, live feedback leaking into the replacement context, and a cancelled continuation still labelled queued.

**Integrated verification** (verifier, Sol; canonical record `.crewroom/test-results/m63-slice3-verify/gates.json`, receipt `receipt.md`):

| Case | Result | Covering evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 Observe | Pass | `bitbucket.test.ts` pagination; `publication.test.ts` newer-row/partial read; browser panel shows source, PR, read state, no agents executing and no provider PUT |
| 2 Resume | Pass | Queued and replayed Resume tests; browser Resume queued behind holder, reopen keeps the original pinned comment despite a later edit |
| 3 Validate and update | Pass | Same-PR successor with lost PUT reconciliation, no-change retention, C2→C3 rework; browser real Git candidate → one PR POST, one PUT, same PR #9 |
| 4 Interrupt | Pass | Interrupted dirty writer restored and completed; uncertain-owner hold on restart; existing LIF-04/05 and newer-control cases unchanged and passing |
| 5 Stay within scope | Pass | Late comment excluded, failed-check and closed-PR gates, uncaptured close terminalized; `target-refresh.test.ts` 55/55 ×3 |

Gates, run one at a time on the frozen tree: `npm run build` exit 0; `npm run test:e2e` **19/19**, exit 0 (includes the new feedback journey). `npm test` **269/270, exit 1**. Recorded as a failed gate, not a pass. The single failure is the unchanged Slice 2 test `tests/target-refresh.test.ts:1744`, "a live checked reset holds promotion even when the remote moves during execution": `Checked helper exited before registering Git ownership` (:1771). The same assertion fails on a clean `dec01c7` baseline worktree (**261/262, exit 1**), and the whole file passes 55/55 three times on both baseline and candidate. It is classified as a pre-existing, load-sensitive intermittent of the full suite and accepted under the timing-test rule in [status](../status.md); no repair rounds were spent. Fixing it belongs with M7 operational readiness.

**Observations carried forward.** The candidate browser suite logged four ghostty-web `RangeError: offset is out of bounds` stacks at `src/terminal.tsx:39` while rendering stopped-attempt panes in the new feedback fixture. The baseline suite, which lacks that spec, logged none, and `src/terminal.tsx` is unchanged. No assertion failed and no cause is claimed. Bitbucket does not document that deletion tombstones always remain listed, so a vanished comment stays at last-known state with unknown currentness. Live OMP delivery of the continuation input beyond the host seam was simulated in this slice. M6.3 is complete. M6.4 begins only on the user's direction.

## M6.4 slice A — Role and model editing, accepted 2026-10-01

Base `d22824c`. Scope record: [Slice A](../mvp-plan.md#slice-a--role-and-model-editing). External boundaries simulated (scripted model hosts, real process leases); no live provider, Jira or retained-pilot writes.

**Behavior.** `RoleCatalog.update` requires the revision being edited; a stale save returns 409 and leaves the file unchanged. Every caller sends it: the HTTP route, `RoleEditor`, learning approval (which records a stale role as a superseded, not-applied outcome) and `scripts/team-driver.ts`. The editor shows the current revision and learning provenance, keeps a draft across panel switches and on conflict, guards switching roles with unsaved changes, and offers *Reload current definition* using whichever of the pushed and fetched definitions is newer. Wall tiles show each assignment's captured role revision, model selector, effort and instructions, and say when the catalog has a newer revision. Disabled roles are refused at admission, team proposal and coordinator selection, and are not offered in queue selection. A pending team proposal naming a role disabled before it applies ends `rejected` with a reason and one coordinator notice in the same transaction; the run continues, and restart replay of an interrupted transition still stops old owners and relaunches the existing team. A queued plan resolves role definitions at admission, as `LaunchSpec` documents.

**Review.** Producer: OpenAI GPT-6 Sol (implementer). Reviewer: Anthropic Claude Opus 5.5 (retained `reviewer`; session-reported, serving telemetry unavailable). First review: two P2 (team-driver not migrated; a disabled role stalling a pending proposal) and one P3 (stale conflict notice). Repair pass 1 fixed all three but introduced a P2: restart replay skipped stop/relaunch. A bounded Astra diagnosis confirmed the cause and selected removal of the replay pre-check. Repair pass 2 (counter 2/2) applied it with a parameterized restart regression; final recheck reported no findings.

## M6.4 slice B — Repository editing, accepted 2026-10-01

**Behavior.** Repository edits pass asynchronous Git preflight, require the current revision, and retain keyed update/retirement receipts. Open runs guard checkout/target changes and removal of referenced check IDs, naming the affected tickets. Permitted edits apply to later preparation; prepared coding runs retain their repository snapshots in Results and history. Retirement retains the row and historical references, blocks new selection and queueing, and requires affected tickets to select an active configuration. An active configuration may reuse a retired checkout/target. The editor preserves a conflicted draft and confirms retirement.

## M6.4 Slice C — implementation brief (2026-10-01)

Baseline: `01bc1d8`. One implementation owner; one retained opposite-vendor reviewer. Repair counter: 0/2. Scope: recorded causes and operator next steps, read-only attempt detail, shared usage aggregation, URL-addressable run/attempt history, and keyboard/narrow-layout behavior. Reuse the durable event, evidence and provider-receipt stores; keep supervisor and recovery controls unchanged.

## N3.3b2a — Native repository shutdown integration (2026-10-03)

Checked Git mutations now register before asynchronous command configuration, retain controller authority through cancelled callers, and recheck their admission ticket around actual dispatch. Stop fences the run immediately and drains its registered work; overlapping Stop requests cannot clear a newer fence. Close stays closed. Passive reads do not signal processes.

Recovery inventories both saved commands and physical process directories. It includes promotion descriptions that reached SQLite before any process manifest existed and reads again after pending preparation settles. Each verified child is cleaned up independently of corrupt records or unavailable SQLite ownership data. Missing records, changed directories, links, special files and unresolved process identities remain explicit holds. Another run's known process is not stopped. Passive repository preparation shares the bounded record reader and refuses a process directory whose ownership record is missing.

**12 shutdown scenarios and three native lifecycle tests pass.** Five scenarios kill the actual controller: ordinary Git, corrupt command records, SQLite-only promotion, running promotion and a missing command record. Other cases cover cancelled mutation/Stop waiters, closed admission, unrelated-run preservation, real controller-lock retention, overlapping Stop generations, late configuration reads, malformed/linked/oversized/FIFO records and unavailable SQL ownership. A delayed helper cannot execute a sealed promotion. Two separately reproduced missing-record defects were fixed before acceptance; their original failures remain in the evidence.

Tested native source fingerprint `510eb5e794c835ed420091fa83bf887419924793d26adc4abeb9165f0b47f40f`, based on `d333c022ae3839f6daf185d26ffba2cd867c284a`. All **19 final jobs** pass: Rust **135/135**, workspace build/format/Clippy, oracle/example builds, repository-store comparisons, the new shutdown suite, repository/evidence/target/conflict/retained/publication regression suites, native harness types, production build and browser **31/31**. Retained domain **332/332** is one separately identified invocation whose entire TypeScript dependency source is unchanged; its global native fingerprint changed during the last repair, and no fresh domain invocation on the final native fingerprint is claimed.

The final Git whitespace check removed exactly one trailing newline from the extracted shared fixture. A byte comparison confirms that all other tested source is unchanged, and the affected harness type check passes again. The acceptance fingerprint and this normalization are recorded separately.

[Machine-readable evidence](rust-native-repository-shutdown-2026-10-03.json) identifies every source hash, command, log, superseded run, audit finding and retained-source check. Full workflow composition is next. Native daemon admission remains fenced, and N6–N7 are still required. No live provider delivery or release cutover is claimed.

## N3.3b2b2 — Native workflow supervision (2026-10-03)

The Rust workflow controller now connects independent agent hosts and external Mail to repository work, revision evidence, contained checks, research, Jira and publication. Immutable assignment, brief and model snapshots survive handoffs, steering, Pause/Resume, Cancel and team changes.

Recovery reattaches verified live processes, bounds automatic replacement, reconciles stored inputs and stopped commands, and preserves completed or unstarted workflow records. Source conflicts and corrupt journals hold execution after cleanup. Coding completion stops all owners before producing revision evidence and restoring current assignments; research completion waits for verified artifacts and a saved Jira summary when required. Shutdown fences admission and drains initialization, workflow, host, Git and provider work. A replay defect discovered by the integration tests is repaired by normalizing operator insertion requests before comparing saved identity.

## N3.3b3 — Native daemon and scheduling (2026-10-03)

The production Rust daemon now composes the validated workflow, Git/evidence, external Mail, Jira and publication services. It holds controller authority through startup recovery and complete shutdown. Operator requests require the private bearer and own Origin; agent tools require their own immutable host bearer. Admitted mutations survive disconnected callers. Stable-origin restart reattaches verified hosts; a changed origin stops endpoints that can no longer be trusted. Dropping the service or cancelling a startup/close waiter still drives owned cleanup.

The scheduler uses the durable coding slot, checks configured roles and dependency ancestry before worktree admission, and replays a killed initial decision without a second team or worktree. Hosted readiness releases a stopped run; confirmed invalidation reclaims it, and queued Resume takes priority over ordinary plans. The API exposes workflow controls, requirements adoption, team insertion, delivery/feedback, target recovery, results, artifacts, revision diff, history and usage. Workspace/run/result subscriptions and durable event cursors remain observation, not quality gates. Missing configuration stays unavailable. The launcher builds the runtime helpers as well as the daemon and GUI.

**137 Rust tests, 11 daemon scenarios, two daemon/provider scenarios, 21 controller regressions and 135 scheduling comparisons pass.** Four separate **actual OMP/tmux + Rust Mail** scenarios pass under the network guard: handoff/steering/crash/session recovery; configured compatible fallback; absent compatible fallback; and nonretryable provider failure. All owned processes stop and all provider-fixture error lists are empty. Actual Git and Docker establish coding/check/review evidence; Jira, Bitbucket and model responses are scripted. No new live provider delivery is claimed.

**Domain 332/332, browser 31/31, Rust/workspace and production builds, format, Clippy and harness types pass.** The first 19-job candidate had three failing jobs: one scheduler fixture advanced before saved provider invalidation, and two failure fixtures omitted five of OMP's six 503 retries. A controlled overlapping observation reproduces the first error and passes after the fixture waits for confirmed invalidation. Six focused follow-ups corrected those fixtures, then exposed a startup signal race: readiness was published before SIGTERM/SIGINT registration. A blocked startup-output socket reproduces it deterministically. Registering handlers before initialization passes that regression for both signals. Fourteen final Rust/daemon/provider/actual-runner jobs pass with the repaired executable. The TypeScript domain/build/browser inputs and native workflow/scheduling library inputs remain byte-identical to their preceding passing runs. This is 19 accepted job results across 39 invocations, not one fully passing 19-job invocation.

The [machine-readable evidence](rust-native-daemon-scheduling-2026-10-03.json) binds the final source fingerprint `e2376655ed1e5bc3e072bf2f2b0b03ac8542396ebc74551459db700a2114e6d3` to baseline `ba64555d81b1f5b7608017c24c16f7f406aa9569`, records both manifests and original failures, and verifies unchanged inputs for reused results and identifies the repaired daemon binary. The process-marker fixture now publishes its body atomically, closing the prior checkpoint's empty-marker race.

**Five of seven major stages are accepted: N1–N5.** N6 native operator flows, terminal wall and accessibility, then N7 historical upgrades, install/restore, resource measurements and cutover remain required. The retained TypeScript release remains available and the live pilot is unchanged. Self-assessment: this iteration completes the planned service integration; test-fixture corrections and shutdown/startup ownership review added time, but no accepted workflow was deferred.

## Packaged redraw diagnosis and acceptance reconciliation (2026-10-04)

## MH2 compatibility spike — FOR-7 (2026-10-12)

The operator clarified that harnesses supply model access through existing setup. This supersedes the prior gateway-first recommendation. Production execution remains OMP; the bounded spike adds reproducible probes and evidence, not the production Codex adapter.

On application source `77054f8690ad361f582892042323dae454568c0e`, pinned Codex 0.162.0 passed **23/23 synthetic-provider cases for gpt-6.1-sol and 23/23 for gpt-6-astra**. Captured flat-function turns passed the unchanged Rust gateway; native-call injections were denied, streaming/tool interruption completed as interrupted, durable history/tools survived process replacement, and separate app-server processes received addressed results. Expected gateway denials count as successful diagnostics, not successful model turns.

The native-auth check passed **two live gpt-6-astra/low turns** using the existing ChatGPT login: one harmless host-tool roundtrip followed by SIGKILL, a new process and correct retained result from the same thread without another tool call. Final cumulative usage reported 15,421 total tokens (91 output); this is observed accounting, not a hard token/spend limit. No reroute was observed. Private state and a read-only auth mount left source auth size/mtime unchanged; all recorded children were reaped. No external-Mail or production ownership/refresh acceptance is claimed.

Crash injection distinguishes original side effects from Codex history: before a reply, resumed history says `aborted` even when the stand-in effect and host receipt exist. After reply delivery, `SAVED_RESULT` survives. Production integration must reconcile original logical-operation receipts before new work; this fixture does not prove exactly-once execution.

The [report](../research/codex-mh2-compatibility-spike-2026-10-12.md) and [receipt](codex-mh2-spike-2026-10-12.json) bind source/probe/binary hashes and retain initial fixture failures (oversized prompt, missing compaction response and missing CA bundle). Rust gateway/oracle examples built; Python syntax checks passed. No production domain/build/user-flow code changed, so application suites were not rerun. Historical Linux browser 29/31 remains separate. Next: OMP seam preservation, Codex host integration and receipt recovery, native refresh/budget capability handling, then both adapters' full acceptance. FOR-7 remains In Progress and continues to block MH3/FOR-8.
